# Apartment finder: design

Living document. Source of truth for architecture. Any change to behaviour, schema, config shape or a decision updates this file and appends to the [decision log](#decision-log) in the same commit (see `AGENTS.md`).

## Context

A personal tool on one machine: SQLite plus files on disk, the dashboard on `127.0.0.1`. The live copy runs on a home server (systemd user service, deployed with `scripts/deploy.sh`) and is reached through a Cloudflare tunnel under a secret base path. Any number of goals (two in the committed example) share one global candidate list; goals are created and edited in the dashboard. The main output is a map dashboard that takes manual comments, ratings and verification checks. Text is read by Claude Haiku and Sonnet through the `claude` CLI (subscription, no API key); listings come through Apify; routes from Google.

## Goals (`config/goals.toml`, edited in the dashboard)

The goals below are the committed example (`config.example/goals.toml`); the real ones live in the gitignored `config/`. `goals.toml` is only the seed: goals are edited, created and deleted in the dashboard, and each edit is an `annotations` row (`entity_type` goal, `entity_key` the goal id, attribute `definition`, `comment` the JSON `{label, rules, nice, people?}`; a delete is `{"deleted": true}`, a tombstone). The effective set (`filter.goals.effective_goals`) is the file overlaid by the latest row per goal id: seed goals keep their order, new goals follow in the order first saved; `[verify]` always comes from the file. With no goal rows, the effective set is exactly the file.

| | Goal 1: `temp` | Goal 2: `lease` |
|---|---|---|
| What | Airbnb, Furnished Finder, Blueground or similar furnished stay for one person | Apartment or house for a group |
| Sizes | private room is fine; bathroom must be private | 3bd with 2 or more baths (3/2, 3/2.5, 3/3) |
| Dates | Dec 1, 2026 to Jan 1, 2027 | from about Jan 1, 2027; availability ignored for now |
| Hard filters | at most 8 min bike, or at most 25 min walk or transit, to downtown Palo Alto; private bathroom; furnished; at most $4,000 a month | under $2,500 per bedroom; long-term lease |
| Nice to have | none | at or under $2,000 per bedroom; gym; walk to Caltrain 20 min or less; Google bus stop nearby (`google_bus_stops.csv`: employer shuttle stops, filled in the private config only); living room size or total sqft |

## Principle: gather wide, filter late

- **Ingest takes everything in the area**, whatever its beds, baths or price. Goals are filters over stored data, so changing a threshold or adding a goal needs no new scrape.
- **Everything is timestamped and kept.** Each fact carries when it was observed and which source and run produced it. Nothing is overwritten or deleted.
- **Unknown never rejects.** Any filter whose input is missing passes the place with `needs_check` and names the missing field.
- **Filters report, they do not drop.** A failing place stays in the database and on the map (dimmed) with its fail reasons.
- **Paid routing is the one lazy step.** Straight-line distance is computed for everything for free. A place without its own Google route for a checked (destination, mode) cell reads a KNN range from Google-routed places within 400 m (`filter.knn`); a place whose trip row for the cell has no time (transit, which the straight line cannot estimate, or a Google row with no route) and no routed place within 400 m reads the middle of the 3 nearest Google times at any distance as an estimate (`knn.far_estimate`, judged through the estimate band). A Google row without a time never hides a trip that has one (`best_trips`). Google routing runs first for places passing a loose straight-line prefilter, and on demand for any other place from the dashboard.

Search area (`config/area.toml`): every city along Caltrain from Redwood City to Sunnyvale: Redwood City, North Fair Oaks, Atherton, Menlo Park, Palo Alto (including Stanford), Los Altos, Mountain View, Sunnyvale.

## Storage: logs are the truth, entities are derived

One SQLite file `data/apartments.db`, plus `data/raw/` (untouched scraper output, one JSON file per run and source) and `data/media/` (mirrored photos and floor plans named by content hash, because listing image URLs expire). `data/` is gitignored. Seed/demo data lives in a separate file, `data/seed.db`, so it never mixes with real observations.

### Append-only logs

Triggers reject UPDATE and DELETE on every log table. The only exception: a `runs` row may be updated once to close it out (`finished_at`, `counts`, `est_cost`, `status`, `error`) while its `finished_at` is still NULL; its identifying columns can never change.

- `runs`: id, started_at, finished_at, component, source, `params` JSON, `counts` JSON, est_cost, status (`running`, `ok`, `error`, `aborted`), error. `params.scope` is `full` (the whole area for that source; the default) or `targeted` (e.g. direct enrichment of one place). A listing is `active` when its latest observation is from, or newer than, its source's latest successful full run; targeted runs never mark anything inactive. A listing that is off the market (view `withdrawals`, below) is not active either.
- `observations`: id, run_id, observed_at, source, source_id, url, content_hash, price, price_basis (`month`, `night`, `week`, `stay`), query_checkin, query_checkout, `data` JSON, raw_path. **One row per listing per run, always**, so "seen in run N" is a fact in the table and a taken-down listing is distinguishable from an unchanged one. `content_hash` is the hash of the normalized parsed fields plus query dates (not the raw payload, whose image URLs expire); history views collapse consecutive rows with the same hash.
- `links`: id, ts, author (rule name or `user`), source, source_id, place_key, unit_key, reason. "This listing is this place." The current link for a listing is the newest `user` row if any, else the newest rule row; identity appends a rule row only when its decision differs from the current rule link.
- `trips`: id, computed_at, place_key, point_id (`set:name`), mode, minutes, distance_m, `legs` JSON, depart_assumption, provider, o_lat, o_lon (the position the trip was routed from; added 2026-11-25 by `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` in `init_db`). A trip counts only while its place is within `trips.STALE_M` (30 m) of that origin (`trips.is_current`); a row from an old position, or with no origin, is stale: readers skip it (KNN or estimate stand in) until it is routed again.
- `checks`: id, ts, author, queue (`sample`, `conflict`, or `adhoc`: the detail drawer's 'no longer for rent' button), observation_id or trip_id, field, stored_value (JSON), verdict (`correct`, `wrong`, `cannot_tell`), true_value (JSON), note. A `wrong` verdict's `true_value` overrides the scraped value in the derived views **only while the listing's latest observed value still equals `stored_value`**; once the source changes the value, the correction lapses and the new value is shown. The same rule defines *check state* everywhere (view `current_checks`: latest check per listing and field, valid while the value is unchanged), so a new run with the same value keeps a listing checked, and a changed value puts it back in the queues.
- `api_calls`: id, ts, provider, mode, ok, note. One row per paid routing call (see Google metering in the decision log).
- `media`: id, fetched_at, url, kind (`photo`, `plan`), status (HTTP status; 0 = network error), error, sha256, phash, same_as, distance, width, height, bytes, stored_bytes. One row per download attempt by `aptf-media fetch`, failures included. `sha256` and `phash` (64-bit pHash, hex) are of this download re-encoded; `width`, `height`, `bytes` of the original. `same_as` set: an exact or near copy of a file already stored for the same place, so nothing was written (`distance` = pHash bits apart, `stored_bytes` NULL); the URL is served from `COALESCE(same_as, sha256)`. New data (what we downloaded and stored), not a copy of an observation fact, so allowed as a log.
- `geocodes`: id, ts, address, status, precise, lat, lon, location_type, types (JSON), place_id, formatted. One row per Geocoding API answer (`identity.addresses`, since 2026-11-25): the first result whose house number and street are the written ones (`precise` 1, `geocode_parts`), else the first result (`precise` 0). New data (Google's reading of an address string), not a copy of an observation fact, so allowed as a log; the latest row per address is the current one. Read by the `places` view (position below). The street and city parts identity keys on stay in `data/geocode_cache.json`.
- `annotations`: id, ts, author, entity_type, entity_key, attribute (free text: `gym`, `walk_to_caltrain`, `living_room`, `overall`, `status`, ...), goal_id (optional; the dashboard no longer sets it on place notes and ratings, and nothing reads it there: a rating is a fact about the building, shared by every goal), rating (1 to 5, optional), comment. The latest row per entity, attribute and author is the current value; for a place, the latest rating of an attribute stands until another rating replaces it (a later comment-only row does not clear it). Only the web app writes here.

### `observations.data` JSON

Parsed fields, all optional: `address`, `unit`, `city`, `zip`, `lat`, `lon`, `beds` (0 = studio; a private room is `beds = 1` with `kind = room`), `baths`, `sqft`, `kind` (`apartment`, `house`, `townhome`, `room`, `entire_place`), `term` (`long` or `short`), `furnished`, `private_bath`, `available_from`, `available_to`, `amenities` (list), `floor_plan`, `property_name`, `operator`, `title`, `description`, `media` (list of `data/media/` paths), `extra` (anything source-specific). One `extra` name is shared by every source: `floorplan_image`, the URL of the plan's or unit's floor plan drawing as the source gives it (Avalon `floorPlan.highResolution` on `resource.avalonbay.com`, Zumper plan media of type 2, Zillow `richMedia[].rmxData[].floorPlan.heroImageUrl`, Apartments.com `models[].imageLarge`, a render's page image whose label names the plan); absent when there is none. A `direct` `site_info` row may carry `plan_drawings`, {label: URL} (Prometheus' own plan-name map; on other sites the images whose alt, class or URL says floor plan, label = alt text plus file name), never read as plans.

### Derived views (never written)

`latest_obs`, `current_links`, `listings`, `units`, `places`, `operators`, `current_checks`, `field_overrides` and `obs_history` are SQL views over the logs, created by `store.init_db`. `listings.price_per_bed` is the monthly price over `MAX(beds, 1)`: a studio counts as one bedroom (as in Verify's too-cheap check, `plans.inherit_prices`, Verify's goal relevance and the drawer's plan rows); unknown beds give none. They are views, not tables, so there is no second copy to drift. If they get slow, they become materialized tables rebuilt by one `derive` command with no change to readers.

Goal outcomes are not stored at all: `filter.evaluate` computes them at read time from the views, `trips` and the effective goal set (`goals.toml` plus goal edits), so changing a threshold takes effect on the next page load.

A place's `lat`/`lon` is the mean of its listings' best positions, by tier: (0) the pins of listings from sources that place the building; else (1) for Craigslist rows (`store.keys.APPROX_PIN_SOURCES`: the pin is the poster's account or area point, not the building), their address where Google placed it precisely (latest `geocodes` row: `precise` 1, `location_type` not APPROXIMATE) and within 5 km of the post's pin (planar, 111 x 88.2 km per degree); else (2) the Craigslist pins. lat and lon always come from the same tier.

Keys are deterministic so annotations and trips survive any rebuild: `place_key` is a hash of the normalized street address (or of lat/lon rounded to 4 decimals when there is no address), `unit_key` adds the listing's own unit, else its floor plan, else a unit written in its address (a floor-plan row carries the building's address, which may name another listing's unit), and a listing is `(source, source_id)`.

### Config (human-edited, read at startup)

Your own config is `config/`, gitignored: real goals, commute targets, hand-listed community sites (data gathering, not code) and `discovered_sites.toml` (append-only; a `[[check]]` carries an `outcome`: `ok`, `rejected` = fetched and the street address is not on the page and nothing else vouches for it (`discover.vouched`: you added the URL; its host is this place's Google Places website on a host no other place uses, for a site not itself found from Places; or the building's distinctive name words are in the URL or title and its city is on the page; never an error page or an aggregator), the only outcome that drops a site, or a retryable `blocked`, `timeout`, `error`, `not_fetched`, `empty`; the latest check of a site wins, and so does the latest `[[found]]` row of a site id: a corrected URL is appended under the same id). `config.example/` is the committed template with the same files and anonymized values; it is used when `config/` does not exist (`settings.CONFIG_DIR`, override with `APTF_CONFIG`), and tests always use it. Paths below say `config/` for either.

- `config/goals.toml`: the seed goal definitions as a list of typed rules per goal (hard rules and nice-to-haves), plus verification thresholds. Rule types (`filter.rules.RULE_TYPES`, checked by `validate_rule` before any dashboard edit is stored): `max` / `min` (numeric field, value, strict?), `equals` (term, furnished, private_bath, kind, restriction: none / income / senior / veterans / student / employer / other), `size_options`, `dates_cover`, `commute_any` (a point, `{mode, max}` options, optional `in_net`), `near` (a point set, mode, max, optional `in_net`: counts in the net commute; unset = a must-have counts, a nice-to-have does not), `amenity` (name, match words, exclude words), `known` (field), `net_commute` (agg = total, mean, median or spread, max minutes: the goal's hard commute rules combined, see the web section), `not_operator` (name: an operator of `operators.toml`, below), `parking_cost` (spots; a nice-to-have that ranks). `equals` also takes `parking_included` and `is_apartment_building` (true / false); `max` / `min` take `price`, `price_month`, `price_per_bed`, `price_per_person`, `beds`, `baths`, `sqft`, `parking_spots`, `parking_usd_month`, `building_units` (`rules.NUMERIC_FIELDS`). The filter section says how each is judged. A goal may set `people` (an integer >= 1, the people sharing the rent of a group lease; editor header 'people sharing'): the numeric field `price_per_person` is then `price_month / people`, derived when a rule is judged (`rules.field_value`, never stored; a spare bedroom costs nothing extra, unlike `price_per_bed`). Under a goal without `people` a rule on it is needs_check, 'goal has no 'people''. The editor offers the 'price / person at most' preset and the field only when the goal sets `people`; cards, the drawer's plan table and fail chips under such a goal show '$X/person (÷n)' instead of '/bed'.
- `config/area.toml`: cities and bounding box.
- `config/points/*.csv`: one file per point set, columns `set,name,lat,lon` plus free columns. The single concept for anything a place can be near or travel to:
  - `commute_targets.csv`: Googleplex, Meta HQ, Downtown Palo Alto, Rengstorff industrial complex (1000 N Rengstorff Ave, Mountain View; a stand-in for a possible office).
  - `caltrain_stations.csv`: Redwood City, Menlo Park, Palo Alto, California Ave, San Antonio, Mountain View, Sunnyvale, Lawrence. Extra columns `service` (best tier that stops there: `express` or `local`), `patterns` and `other_transit`.
  - `grocery_chains.csv` (Walmart, Safeway, Whole Foods, Trader Joe's) and `grocery_any.csv` (every supermarket and grocery), generated once from OpenStreetMap by `scripts/osm_groceries.py` and committed.
  - `google_bus_stops.csv`: employer shuttle stops (filled in the user's live config); header only in `config.example/`, since a stop list is employer-specific.
- `config/point_sets.toml`: per set, which modes to compute and how many nearest points to route to. "Proximity" is a query (the minimum walk `trips` row for a set), not a table.
- `config/caltrain.toml`: station-to-station ride times per service pattern and headways, used by the commute module's train legs.
- `config/operators.toml` (the committed `config.example/operators.toml` when config/ has none; re-read when it changes): `[[operator]]` name, restriction `income` (affordable-housing nonprofits, housing authorities), `employer` (an employer's housing for its staff or affiliates; filled in like `income`) or `none` (market-rate REITs and managers), `domains` (own-site hosts, subdomains included) and `names` (the listing operator field, exact, any case; for the `not_operator` rule also the other lister names a listing carries, below). Read by the filter's who-may-rent pooling and by `not_operator` (below).
- `config/media.toml`: `[media] max_gb` (default 5 when the file is missing), the hard cap on `data/media/` for `aptf-media fetch`.

## Components

All code lives in one Python package `aptfinder` (`src/aptfinder/`), one subpackage per component, each with its own CLI entry point (`aptf-<component>`) and tests under `tests/`.

### store (`aptf-db`)
- Goal: own the schema: log tables, append-only triggers, derived views; append helpers.
- Inputs: a database path.
- Outputs: an initialized SQLite file; `append_*` functions; `seed` command writing demo observations to `data/seed.db`.
- Must not: update or delete log rows; hold business rules beyond key derivation and view definitions.
- **Write transactions are short.** SQLite has one writer at a time, so every writer keeps its transactions short: bulk appends (intake runs, `reparse`, the extract apply step, identity links) commit every `store.COMMIT_EVERY` (200) rows; `render` commits each site's rows before the next site's Claude call; `aptf-media fetch` commits each row before waiting for the next download; nothing holds a pending write across a network, Apify or Claude wait. Partial progress is visible (the logs are append-only), and a run that fails is still closed as `error`. Connections wait up to 5 minutes for the lock (`store.connect`, and the commute leg cache). `store.begin_write(conn, on_wait)` takes the lock explicitly and reports that it is waiting; the dashboard's compute job uses it to show "waiting for the database (another job is writing)".

### intake (`aptf-intake`)
- Goal: bring listings into `observations`, as wide as possible.
- Inputs: `config/area.toml`, source adapter configs, `APIFY_TOKEN`, spend cap.
- **Apify credit guard (`intake/apify.py` `guard`, `credit`).** Every Apify actor run, whatever starts it (`run_source` with every adapter and Airbnb's quadrant searches, `zillow-detail`, `render`, `portfolio`, `promote-sites --browser [--residential]`), goes through `apify.run_actor`, which first reads the account's real credit from `GET /v2/users/me/limits` (`limits.maxMonthlyUsageUsd - current.monthlyUsageUsd`), cuts the run's `maxTotalChargeUsd` to (credit left - `spend.reserve_usd`) and refuses (`SpendRefused`, no run started) when that is under $0.02 or the API gives no credit. Apify enforces `maxTotalChargeUsd` only for pay-per-event actors, so `run_actor` also aborts a run whose polled `usageTotalUsd` reaches its cap (platform-billed actors such as `apify/playwright-scraper`; checked every 10 s). That abort sees compute only: residential proxy traffic is not in `usageTotalUsd` until after the run (see the 2026-11-25 entry), so a residential browser run (`render --residential`, `promote-sites --browser --residential`, portfolio's residential operators) is checked up front instead: pages x `RESIDENTIAL_USD_PER_PAGE` ($0.029, an estimate from this cycle's charges) must fit the guard's cap, else the run is refused. Multi-run loops (`run_source`, `zillow-detail`) also keep `base.spend_room` for their own `--max-usd`, counting per actor run the larger of the real charge and the estimate. Media's residential-proxy downloads (not an actor) check the same guard before opening the proxy and cut the byte cap to the room at $8/GB; refused, the residential hosts are skipped (`counts.proxy_refused`). `aptf-intake credit` prints the guard's live numbers without running anything.
- **Real Apify charges are recorded with each run.** `runs.counts.apify_usd` is the sum of Apify's `usageTotalUsd` of the run's actor runs as read when each ended, and `counts.apify_runs` lists each (Apify run id, status, `usd`, `usage` breakdown); each is re-read from the Apify API just before our run closes (`apify.settle`), which may or may not catch the late residential-proxy charge (not yet seen on a proxied run). `runs.est_cost` keeps our estimate (charged events x FREE-tier event prices; render, portfolio and the browser check already used `usageTotalUsd` at finish) for comparison. Residential proxy traffic is billed after the run ends, so `apify_usd` can still be short for proxied runs (the dashboard calls it 'Apify-reported'); the live credit (dashboard header, pipeline panel: 'Apify credit: $X left (reserve $Y)', read through `/api/status` and `/api/pipeline`, cached 60 s) is the truth.
- Outputs: `runs` rows, one `observations` row per listing per run, raw files under `data/raw/`, mirrored media under `data/media/`.
- Must not: filter by goal; scrape any listing site directly from this machine (Apify only; the exceptions are the own-site precheck, a DNS lookup and one plain GET per site that reads a few KB and stores nothing of the page, and Prometheus's availability JSON, one request per Prometheus site, below); contact anyone; quietly accept empty or suspicious output (stop and ask the user).
- Tiers: aggregators discover places; direct enrichment reads a known place's own website (through an Apify web-scraping actor) for availability, per-unit pricing and floor plans, stored with `source = direct`; portfolio discovery follows an operator's "other properties" page. When direct and aggregator data disagree both are kept and direct is shown as current.
- **Areas** (`config/areas.toml`, `config.load_areas`): ZIP codes, cities and station boxes that the Caltrain stations' 25-minute walk zones (radius `radius_km`) touch, plus one `site` area per community website. Each adapter has an `area_kind` (zip, city, box or site) and covers every area of that kind: `aptf-intake run <source>` runs every area of its kind not covered yet, `--area` picks some. The coverage ledger (`intake.base.coverage`, `aptf-intake coverage`) is per (source, area), read from `runs.params.areas`. Steps that act on places (`discover-sites`, `promote-sites`, the own-site list, `compute --candidates`, the Pipeline's website and Google counts) take the places inside some station's walk zone (`areas.in_walk_zone`: within `radius_km` of a `[[box]]` station); `render` and `run direct` take every site in the list.
- No actor is assumed to work: each is chosen by running it for the area and comparing with the benchmark and manual checks. After each new source's first run, the user works a sample queue for it before it is relied on.
- **`reparse --changed-only` appends only what a parser change altered.** For a parser that now keeps a new field: a row is appended only for a listing that is active, whose latest observation came from this raw file, and whose parse differs from its newest intake observation (content hash). The run carries `params.changed_only`, so view `listings_base` does not count it as superseding the file's listings it skipped; it never adds a listing, never credits coverage (`--covers` is refused) and never brings back a retired one (a new row's run id would outrank the run that retired it). The cached text reading then re-applies to the new rows (free). Counts: `unchanged`, `inactive`, `other_file`, `not_held`.
- **A price of 0 or less is unknown.** Every parser's `Parsed` turns it into NULL, so it never becomes a $0 rent, $0 per bed or the place's minimum; goals report `needs_check` (price unknown). `reparse` tags each row with its intake area (`extra.area`) exactly as the original run did, so later complete area runs can still retire it.
- **Base rent is the price for every source; a fee-inclusive total is kept beside it** (`extra.total_monthly`, shown on the plan row's hover). Apartments.com (memo23): a plan whose `rentLabel` says 'Total Monthly Price' gives rent plus required monthly fees on the label and on its units; the fee is the building's one `rentals[].MinTotalMonthlyPrice - Rent` and is taken off (`lease.apartments_included_fee`); if the rentals disagree the total stays, marked `extra.price_includes_fees`. Zillow: when `rental.listPriceIncludesRequiredMonthlyFees` is true and the required fee (`totalRequiredMonthlyMinFee`, on `property` or `property.rental`) is one amount, it is taken off the bedroom rows' and single homes' prices (`lease.zillow_included_fee`). Own sites: render's reader returns base and total separately (decision log 2026-11-25).
- **Google Places (`google_places`, `intake/places.py`)**: the official Places API (New) Text Search, called directly (an API, not a listing site), per station box (`[[box]]`; the box is the search's `locationRestriction`, so results stay inside the walk zone, unlike a city name). One request per query in `[google_places] queries` (text `apartments` with `includedType` `apartment_complex`, then `apartment_building`, strict type filtering) and page (20 results, at most 3 pages = 60; a query that fills all 3 marks its box partial; reaching a cap keeps what was fetched and marks the run's boxes partial). Field mask: id, displayName, formattedAddress, location, websiteUri, nationalPhoneNumber, rating, userRatingCount, businessStatus, types, primaryType, googleMapsUri. One fetch covers every box of a run (batch), so every request is checked against the run's `max_calls` across all boxes and `places_monthly_call_cap` (config/google.toml) and logged in `api_calls` as provider `places`, apart from Routes (`google`). Each place is one `site_info` row (source_id the place id, url its Google Maps page, `extra.website` / phone / rating / business status / types); permanently closed places are skipped. The intake base gained two hooks for it: an adapter's `fetch` replaces the Apify actor run (no Apify credit check when `apify = False`), and `skip` leaves items out on purpose (counted `skipped`, not `unparsed`).
- **Zumper and PadMapper (`zumper`, `padmapper`, `intake/zumper.py`)**: one inventory (Zumper owns PadMapper; the same listing ids appear on both), searched per city (`[[city]]`: the sites' URLs are per city, `zumper.com/apartments-for-rent/<city>-ca`, PadMapper's `<city>-ca` slug). Zumper through `scrapemind/zumperCom-Scraper` with `getDetails`: a building comes with its floor plans, and each plan on the market (status 1, with a price or an offered unit) is one row, source_id `plan:<plan listing id>` (the item's own id is only one of its plans), (plan name, beds, baths incl. half baths, sqft, lowest price, available date, units offered), plus the building's own website (`homepage`) as `extra.website`; a single unit or house is one row, its baths from `min_all_bathrooms` ([full, half], so full + half/2; `min_bathrooms` is the full count only), with `extra.baths_full`/`baths_half` on every Zumper row that has them. PadMapper through `bovi/padmapper-scraper`, search results only: a single unit is a row; a building summary spanning several bedroom counts is a `site_info` row `building:<building id>` (ranges in extra), never an offer with an invented price. Furnished stays (`lease_type` 10, or a Blueground provider or homepage) are short-term, furnished, operator Blueground where named, so identity gives them their own stay place; a stay operator's link is never the building's website. Items are attributed to the city they state (searches spill into nearby cities). **PadMapper is skipped** (`skip = true` in its `sources.toml` section, since 2026-11-25): it adds nothing Zumper lacks, so `run padmapper` refuses and coverage and the pipeline leave it out; its stored rows stay. Any source can be skipped the same way.
- **RentCafe (`rentcafe`, `intake/rentcafe.py`)**: Yardi's listing site, searched per city (`[[city]]`, location `<city>, CA`, one actor run per city) through `benthepythondev/rentcafe-apartments-scraper` with property details: the actor pages through the whole city search (50 properties a page, until a short page) and opens each property for its floor plans; `siteTotal` (raw file only, not on rows, so a moving count never changes row hashes) is the site's count of rentals, i.e. units, not properties (Mountain View: 462 rentals in 72 properties). Each floor plan with a rent is one row, source_id `plan:<floorPlanId>` (name, beds, baths, sqft, lowest rent as a monthly price, `plan_price_max`, `plan_available` = units available now, `rent_text`); a property whose plans all say "Ask for pricing" is one `site_info` row `building:<propertyId>`, never an offer. Extra: property id, rent and bed ranges, total units, the current special, application and pet fees, cats/dogs, year built. Every item links its rentcafe.com page, an aggregator page (`AGGREGATOR_HOSTS`), so `extra.website` stays empty unless a link leads off rentcafe.com (a rentcafewebsite.com community site would be kept). The search spills into neighbouring cities; an item counts for the city it states. Not given by this actor: the card's "Base rent" vs "Total monthly leasing price" label and the units-available count (see the decision log).
- **Zillow detail pages (`aptf-intake zillow-detail`, `intake/zillow_detail.py`)**: an enrichment of Zillow listings already held, not a listing source. One page per zpid of the active Zillow listings without a page yet (a building's bedroom rows `<zpid>:Nbd` share one; `--refetch` takes all), through `delicious_zebu/zillow-property-details-scraper` (input `{"zpids": [...]}`), 150 zpids per actor run (a timeout loses one batch), the budget guard (`base.spend_room`, shared with `run_source`: run cap and Apify credit minus `spend.reserve_usd`) before each run, `--max-usd` required. Raw pages only, `data/raw/zillow_detail/run<id>_<k>.json`: no observations, so no listing, place or geocode; one `runs` row (component intake, source `zillow_detail`, `scope = targeted`), not in `ADAPTERS`, so never coverage and never retiring anything. Items are keyed by `zillow_id` (their `zpid` is empty); requested minus returned are `failed`. A building zpid returns one representative unit's page: its beds, baths, price, dates and deposit are never read for the building's bedroom rows, and its unit-level flags (furnished, laundry, appliances, cooling) are not sent for them. A single unit's row (source_id without ':') is that page's own unit: the text reading fills the beds, baths (full + half / 2, as the search parser), sqft and price its search card left empty from it (`zillow_detail.unit_facts`; price only on a FOR_RENT page and above 0, so 'Price on Request', which the page gives as 0, stays unknown; never the rent Zestimate). Such rows come from `apartmentComplex` search cards without `unitsGroup` (411 Forest Ave, 734 Cowper St), which carry no beds, baths or price at all; the card groups 2 to 17 matching homes (`matchingHomeCount`) and our row is its zpid's one unit (the same-address sibling zpids on the page are not rows), so the values are that unit's, not the building's. The run's `counts.home_status` maps each page's zpid to its `home_status` and `counts.last_event` gives a non-rental page's newest price-history event; an explicit non-rental status (anything but FOR_RENT) puts the listing in Verify (`verify.stale_queue`), or takes it off the market outright when the page says sold or its newest event is 'Listing removed' (view `withdrawals`, see verify).
- **Zillow building pages (`aptf-intake zillow-buildings`, `intake/zillow_building.py`)**: the floor plans of each building whose bedroom rows (`<zpid>:Nbd`) we hold, an enrichment like the detail pages (raw only, `data/raw/zillow_building/run<id>_<k>.json`, one targeted `runs` row of source `zillow_building`, never coverage, never retiring). One page per building through the same actor, `delicious_zebu/zillow-property-details-scraper`, input `{"Urls": [...]}` with a `/b/<address slug>/<lat>,<lon>_ll/` URL built from our row's coordinates (we hold no building URL: the search card's `listingUrl` is `/homedetails/<zpid>_zpid/`, which returns one representative unit's page); the record has `page_type` 'building' and `floor_plans[]` (name, beds, baths, sqft, min/max price, `units[]` with unit number, price, sqft, `availableFrom` epoch ms or '0' = now). A page is read only when it is our building's: its `zillow_id` is the row's zpid or the zpid is one of its plans' units (a neighbour's page counts as `mismatched`, logged, never read; the raw file keeps `requested` URL -> zpid). `--max-usd` required, budget guard before each batch of 150. Applied by the text reading (`extract zillow`, so the fill comes back after every new search observation of the row, as a single unit's page does): for a bedroom row, every plan with its bed count (`zillow_building.bed_facts`) goes to `extra.building_plans` (name, beds, baths, sqft, lowest price, unit count, earliest available); `baths` and `sqft` are filled only when every one of those plans has the same value (Casa Alberta 2 bd: 'Two Bedroom Two Bath' 2 ba and 'Townhouse' 1.5 ba, so baths stays unknown and the two options are kept), `available_from` = the earliest unit, `price` only if the row has none (the card's `minPrice` already is the lowest plan's); all marked `from` 'zillow building' in `extra.extracted.units`, and only empty fields are filled. Rows are not split into plan rows: the Zillow search keeps returning `<zpid>:Nbd`, so plan rows would sit beside it (the same units twice) and never be seen by a search to stay current.
- **Operator portfolios (`portfolio`, `intake/portfolio.py`)**: each big landlord's own city page (URL templates per operator in `[portfolio] operators`, `{slug}` = city), all operator x city pages in one `apify/playwright-scraper` run (clicks 'load more' style buttons, never apply/tour/contact). One generic reader, no per-site parser: Haiku lists the communities shown (name, street, city, link from the page's link list), and the answer is stored in the raw item, so `reparse` re-checks it for free. Kept only as far as the page shows it: name on the page; street on the page; link exactly on the page and on the operator's host (else no link, and no operator: Avalon's page also lists an Equity community). Each community is a `site_info` row, source_id its link without scheme or query (one row for a community seen from two pages), `extra.website` its community page (a lead for `promote-sites`). A blocked (403/429/captcha), missing or unread page is unparsed, warned and makes only its own city partial (`portfolio.failed_cities`, through the adapter hook `area_failures(items)`); the run's other cities keep their own state. `aptf-intake reparse portfolio <raw> --covers auto` recomputes each city's state from a saved raw file for free (partial with a failed page, else complete, empty when no page reached it).
- **Own-site render (`aptf-intake render`, `intake/render.py`)**: the same Haiku call also says who may rent the community (extract's `restriction` values, worded for a whole site: the limit must hold for the community, not a few BMR units, other properties of the operator or 'Section 8 accepted'), checked by `extract.check_restriction` against the pages sent (a limit needs its verbatim quote; none is fine, the pages are described), and stored on every plan row of the site as `extra.page_restriction` {value, evidence, url}. The same call reads the community's parking (extract's `parking` object, `check_parking` against the pages), stored as `extra.page_parking` {..., url} on every plan row; `render-restriction --parking` reads only parking again for sites already rendered (run component `render_parking`, parking-only schema, stored as `page_parking` on a new observation of each plan row). `aptf-intake render-restriction [--site ID] [--max-usd] [--dry-run]` reads it again for sites already rendered from the raw page text of their active `<site>:r:` rows' newest raw file (no Apify; `--workers` parallel Haiku calls, restriction-only schema): each plan row whose reading changed gets a new observation (its scraped data plus `page_restriction`, run component `render_restriction`; without `page_restriction` when the answer is now unknown, counted `cleared`, so a reading a stricter check rejects does not linger), then the cached extract answers are applied over them (free) so their own readings stay current. Rest of the render: each site in the list (Avalon excluded: its page data gives every unit; Prometheus excluded: read from its availability JSON, below) through `apify/playwright-scraper`. The page function clicks expanders, opens the community's floor-plans page and up to 5 per-plan unit lists per site ('View apartments', 'View units', 'Available units'): a link is opened as a page, a button without a link is clicked in place and the dialog it opens is read. Every page followed stays inside the start page's scope (origin + first path segment) and nothing that applies, tours, contacts, schedules or leases is ever clicked or opened. At most 7 pages a site (`maxRequestsPerCrawl`). Each page's text includes every iframe's text, frames showing a price first (plan widgets such as SightMap live in iframes, which `body.innerText` leaves out). A price written with cents ('$3,857.80 /mo') counts as on the page. The price is the base rent. When a page also shows a monthly total with required fees ('$3,857.80 /mo*' beside '$3,851 Base Rent'), the reader returns it as `total_monthly`, kept only if that number is on the page, and stored as `extra.total_monthly` and `extra.required_monthly_fees`. The drawer's $/month cell then shows '+fees' with the total on hover (`total_month`). Haiku reads a site's pages together, each cut to its own budget (start and floor-plans pages 12,000 characters, unit pages 6,000), so unit numbers, prices and dates reach it; a plan or unit counts only if its name, price and sqft are on the page. The page function also returns each page's images (src, alt, class); a plan's `floorplan_image` is one of them whose alt or file name names the plan (`plans.match_drawing`, exactly one), never a URL Haiku states. **Outcomes, failure vs not:** every render records per site, in the run's `counts.areas['<site>#render']`, an `outcome` beside the area `state`: `ok` (plans found; state `complete`), `no_plans` (rendered, nothing offered; state `empty`), or a transient failure with state `failed`, which retires nothing: `blocked` (HTTP 403/429 or the crawler's 'Request blocked'), `timeout`, `empty` (under 200 characters of text), `not_returned` (no item), `error` (any other request failure), `not_read` (Claude budget reached); with `http_status`, `error` (the scraper's message), pages and unit pages read and failed child pages. Failures come from the actor's failed-request items (`#error` / `#debug`, kept by fetching the dataset with `clean=false`), else from the run log, which the raw file keeps. A run over the whole site list (the default) picks transient failures first, then sites never rendered, then those older than 7 days; `ok` and `no_plans` are done until then. `--retry-failed` picks only the failures; `--site` always renders. Runs from before outcomes read their state (complete -> ok, empty -> no_plans, failed -> not_returned). **Precheck (`intake/precheck.py`, before every render and every `promote-sites` fetch, free):** per site a DNS lookup of the host and one plain GET from this machine (browser User-Agent, redirects followed, 12 s timeout, at most 16 KB of the body read, nothing stored). `dead`: the host does not resolve (`EAI_NONAME`/`EAI_NODATA`: NXDOMAIN, or a CNAME to nothing). `challenge`: a Cloudflare bot challenge to the plain GET (header `cf-mitigated: challenge`, or `server: cloudflare` with a 403/429/503 whose first bytes carry 'Just a moment...' / `cf-chl` / `challenge-platform`): reported by `aptf-intake precheck`, never recorded or skipped, because Apify's browser gets through (see the decision log). Anything else proceeds as before, including a temporary DNS failure, a timeout, a TLS error, a bare 403 and a malformed host (spaces in it). Only a `dead` site is not sent to Apify (`config.PRECHECK_SKIP`): render records it as its outcome (state `failed`, retires nothing; label 'site dead (DNS)'), `promote-sites` as a `[[check]]` with that outcome (not in `CHECK_RETRY`, never a rejection). Neither `--retry-failed` takes it; a plain `render` / `promote-sites` takes it again once the outcome is over 7 days old, and the fresh precheck decides; `render --site` takes it at once, still gated by the fresh precheck. `aptf-intake precheck [--site ID] [--dry-run]` checks every site in the list and records only `dead` (one `runs` row, `params.render` + `precheck`, scope `targeted`, cost 0; plus a `[[check]]` for a discovered site whose address check is not ok), so it never overwrites a real render outcome.
- **Finding plans for Verify's own-site list (`aptf-intake find-plans [--place KEY] [--name NAME] [--limit N] [--max-apify-usd] [--dry-run]`, `intake/findplans.py`), `--store` stores priced readings like render (below); `--no-search` (click agent from the known site), `--google-only`, `--reason R` (own-site list reason), `--shots` (screenshots and the vision fallback, off by default).** Targets (`findplans.targets`) are own-site list items. The known start page is a URL you added, else a site that loaded, else one not fetched yet, else one whose address check failed; never a listing's short-link host (livewres.com and the like) or a form.
  - Search first. Two searches for "<name> <city> floor plans": Google (`apify/google-search-scraper`, one run for all queries, $0.0025 a query) and Claude (`claude -p` with WebSearch only, Haiku, results reported in order, no Apify credit).
  - Picking a result (`pick_result`): never an aggregator or a form. It is accepted when it is on a host a known site of the place uses (on a manager's host whose name is not the building's, such as caprop.com, only a page whose path names it), or when its host, or host and path, carry every distinctive word of the name (`discover.name_tokens`, without the city's words). The rendered page must then name the building's city (`names_city`), else it is another building of that name.
  - Click agent. It starts from the Google pick, else Claude's, else the known site; a start page that fails falls back to the next one. Each hop renders the page and gives Haiku its text plus its links and buttons, and Haiku picks ONE step: read, follow a listed link, click a listed button or tab in place, or stop (rents not online, only a contact form). At most 3 hops.
  - Refusals (AGENTS.md rule 7): a button named like apply, tour, contact, schedule, log in, pay, resident or portal; a form submit button (page function); a link that is a form (`discover.is_form`). Every hop (URL, choice, reason, refusal) is in the raw file.
  - Rendering: `apify/playwright-scraper` (datacenter) with `AGENT_PAGE`, one actor run per round for every open site, cached by (URL, click). It waits up to 10 s for a price, reads every frame's text (plan widgets live in iframes), lists links and buttons, and takes a JPEG screenshot (first 6,000 px).
  - Reading: render's reader (`ask` + `check_plans` + `page_restriction`). When the text gives no priced plan, Haiku reads the screenshot in 1,500 px tiles (`claude -p --input-format stream-json` with image blocks). Those plans are marked `from_screenshot` (they cannot be checked against the text) and get the same price and sqft bounds.
  - Raw file: `data/raw/findplans/pilot<ts>.json`.
  - Storing (`store`, with `--store`). The first priced reading of a building (click agent, then Google's pick, then Claude's) is stored the way render stores: one intake run (source `direct`, `params.render`, `reader` 'find_plans', `scope` partial), plan rows `<site>:r:<plan or unit>` with area `<site>#render` (state complete, outcome ok, so the site's older render rows retire), the page's restriction (`page_restriction`) and fees in extra.
    - The site is the site-list entry whose URL is the page read (`config.site_key`). Otherwise it is a new `[[found]]` site with that page's URL (found_by 'find-plans', plus an ok `[[check]]`), kept beside a hand-listed site of the same place (`load_sites`). The next render then reads that page directly.
    - A manager's homepage (caprop.com) or a missing site is replaced that way, by appending; the site records are never edited. Buildings without a priced reading are not touched.
- **Prometheus availability JSON (`aptf-intake prometheus [--site ID]`, `intake/prometheus.py`)**: the sites in the list whose platform is `prometheus`, read directly from the machine it runs on (AGENTS.md rule 6 exception, 2026-11-25), one request per site, sequential, 2 s apart, a normal browser UA: `https://shopping.prometheusapartments-prod-west2.com/<id>/available-units?date=<today>`, a JSON list of the units offered (plan name, `rent` and `bestRent` for `bestTerm` months, sqft, beds, baths, unit and building number, ready date, deposit, hold status). `<id>` is the community page's `__NEXT_DATA__` -> `props.pageProps.apartmentPageData.id`, taken from the site's stored `direct` raw file (its `<site>:site` row), else from the page fetched once, and cached in `data/prometheus_ids.json`. Each unit is one row: source `direct`, source_id `<site>:r:<unit>` (render's scheme; `<building>-<unit>` when a building number is given), price `bestRent` (else `rent`) per month, `floor_plan`, unit, beds, baths, sqft, `available_from` the ready date, `operator` Prometheus, extra `platform` 'render' with `reader` 'prometheus_json' (so Verify's pending-render check and the render outcomes treat it as rendered), `area` `<site>#render`, rent, best rent and term, deposit, floor, plan and unit ids, `on_hold`, and `floorplan_image` from the site row's `plan_drawings` when a key equals the plan name (else `plans.match_drawing`). The run is logged like a render (component intake, source `direct`, `params.render`, `reader`, `scope` partial; `counts.areas['<site>#render']` with an outcome, units, plans and price range), so a list retires that site's older render rows (an empty list is `no_plans`, state empty, and retires them too), and a refused request (HTTP 403/429, or a JSON error such as Cloudflare's `challenge`) is `blocked` (state failed) and retires nothing; other failures are `error` or `timeout`, and so is a non-empty list that gives no rows (units without a unit number: a changed format, never read as 'nothing offered'). An entry that is a duplicate of another (below) is never asked. Raw responses: `data/raw/direct/run<id>_prometheus_json.json`. No Apify, no Claude, $0. The pipeline suggests `aptf-intake prometheus` when a Prometheus site was never read, failed, or is older than 7 days, and `render` only for the other sites.
- **One site, one entry (`config.duplicate_sites`, applied by `config.load_sites`)**: two entries of the site list (hand-listed plus discovered) are the same site when their URLs have the same `config.site_key` (`discover.clean_url`, then host without `www.` + path without its trailing slash, lowercased, + the query when there is one, since a redirect link names its building only in the query; fragment dropped) or, for Prometheus, the same community id (`data/prometheus_ids.json`). The canonical entry is the first in load order: `direct_sites.toml` before `discovered_sites.toml`, each in file order. `load_sites()` returns canonical entries only, so no reader (the `direct` site areas, `render`, `prometheus`, `precheck`, `promote-sites` retries) ever fetches or writes a duplicate; `load_sites(duplicates=True)` keeps them, marked `duplicate_of`, for the place lookups ("does this place have a site": `discover-sites`, `promote-sites`, the pipeline) and for retiring. `render.mark_duplicates`, run at the start of every `render` and `prometheus`, writes one run of its own (component intake, source `direct`, `params.render` + `duplicates`, scope partial, status ok, cost 0) whose outcome for each newly duplicate entry's `<entry>#render` area is `duplicate` with `duplicate_of`, state `empty`: that retires the entry's older render rows, whatever the reader's own run then does (a refused or failed fetch run retires nothing). Each entry is marked once (again only if its canonical changes). Its `<entry>:site` row (photos, plan drawings, no area) is left as it is. `discover-sites` records a URL whose key is already in the list or found before as a miss ('same site as <id>'), and `promote-sites` never proposes one. The pipeline's own-site list shows each duplicate as 'duplicate of <id>' and leaves it out of the counts and commands; the stage summary names them.
- **Text reading (`aptf-intake extract <source>|all`, `intake/extract.py`), a core phase.** Haiku (`claude -p`, no tools) reads the whole entry of every active listing of each source (`TEXT_SOURCES`: craigslist, furnishedfinder, airbnb, apartments_com, zillow, zumper, padmapper, rentcafe, direct, google_places, portfolio) that has text (a description of 40+ characters, an amenity list, Zillow's required monthly fees, or a name or title, as stored: a RentCafe building or an own-site plan with only a name is read for who may rent). The entry is title, name, description, amenity list and source extras as stored (pets, laundry, housing type, Zillow's `required_monthly_fees`, RentCafe's `rent_special`), plus allowlisted fields of the listing's raw item for sources whose parser stores less (Apartments.com: about text, lease terms, one-time and monthly fees, included utilities, parking, pet and parking fees, amenity groups; Zumper: full and supplemental description, pet policy, fees, deposits, parking, specials, tags); the raw item is found by re-running the source's parser over `raw_path` (no reviews, schools, transit or other buildings). Zillow: the listing's newest detail page, found by `extra.zpid` (`zillow_detail.load_pages`): description, what's special, and plain lines of fees by type (not the deposit), allowed pets, laundry, appliances, cooling, parking, community features, association amenities, pool, utilities, lease term, and 'Furnished: yes' only when true (false is on nearly every page, a default); placeholders ('Contact For Details') are dropped. For a building's bedroom row (source_id `<zpid>:Nbd` or `<zpid>:studio`) the page is one representative unit's, so laundry, appliances, cooling and 'Furnished: yes' are left out; the description, what's special and the building-level lines (fees, pets, parking, community features, amenities, pool, utilities, lease term) stay. `--address-missing` reads again only active rows whose address is still empty, asking even when an answer is cached (the fresh answer replaces the cached one, so a later cached re-apply keeps it); with `--cached-only` it applies the cache and asks nothing. The description is sent up to `MAX_TEXT` (Craigslist's parser keeps 6,000 characters, the other sources 3,000). `--id-like PATTERN` limits a run to source_ids matching an SQL LIKE pattern (e.g. `--reread --id-like '%:%'`: Zillow building rows only). A Zillow listing with a page counts as having text. Unit-specific values are left out, so a building's units send the same text and are asked once; capped at 5,000 characters, 10 entries per call, `--workers` calls (default 8) in parallel, each a separate `claude -p`; the database and the cache are written only by the main thread; new calls stop once `--max-usd` is spent or any call is blocked (answers already paid for are applied, then the blocker is reported). Schema (version 5): address (asked for wherever the text gives a house number and street: title, body, a 'Community Location' or office line, a signature; kept when its street line, the first comma or ' - ' part that starts with a house number, letter suffix '153a' and range '11720-11780' included, appears verbatim, and stored from that line on: 'Extended Stay America Suites - 3 Circle Star Way, San Carlos' -> '3 Circle Star Way, San Carlos', '848, 850, 852, 854 Jordan Ave' -> '854 Jordan Ave'), building name and website (verbatim), who may rent (`restriction`, below), parking (`parking`, below), and facts `{value, evidence}`: gym, pool, in-unit laundry, shared laundry, EV charging, air conditioning, dishwasher, cats allowed, dogs allowed, furnished, private bath, utilities included (which), minimum stay, lease months, application / admin / monthly fees. A fact counts only if its quote appears verbatim in the text sent (a fee's number must be in its quote); unknown stays null and absence is never inferred. The reading is a new observation of the listing: empty fields filled as before; positive facts added to `amenities` in plain words with the suffix '(read from text)' ('Gym / fitness center', 'Pool', 'In-unit laundry', 'Parking: garage', ...), so amenity rules and the place's pooled amenities use them; a listing with no amenity list and no positive fact keeps none (unknown); every checked fact with its quote in `extra.extracted.facts` (`extra.extracted.v` = 5). **Parking** (`extra.extracted.parking`, `extract.check_parking`, shared with render): `{offered, kinds, spots, cost, usd_month, evidence}`; offered yes (a space comes with the home or can be rented there, free or for a fee) / no (no parking, or street parking only) / null; kinds any of garage, carport, covered, assigned, uncovered, off_street, street, tandem, ev_charging, other; spots the stated number of spaces with the home; cost included (no extra charge) / paid (with `usd_month`, the lowest monthly price stated) / null (unspecified); evidence a list of verbatim quotes. Each quote must be in the text (normalized, 3+ characters) or it is dropped, and no quote left means unknown; a spot count or a price is kept only when a kept quote holds the number (digits, or one to six in words); a stated price with no cost makes it paid; kinds only `street` and every quote about the street -> offered no ('street parking available' is no space of one's own; 'Free parking on premises | Free street parking' stays yes); a notice that prices exclude 'fees such as utilities, parking' is no parking reading (null); an offered space becomes the amenity 'Parking: garage, assigned (read from text)' (none for street only). **Beds and baths** (`extra.extracted.units`, {field: {value, evidence?, from}}): only for a row whose source left beds or baths empty and that is one home or one plan (`units_wanted`: not a room, not a site_info row, never a Zillow building's bedroom row, whose page and text are the building's or one representative unit's), and only fields still empty: first a Zillow single unit's own page (`from` 'zillow page', structured), then the text (`from` 'text', lower trust: Haiku's `beds` / `baths` with a verbatim quote holding the number next to a bed or bath word: '1 Bed / 1 Bath', '4BR', 'two bedroom', 'Studio'; baths below 0.5 such as 'SRO/0BA' stay unknown). For sources where one listing is one home (`WHOLE_ENTRY`: craigslist, airbnb, furnishedfinder, zillow) the quote may come from anywhere in the entry; elsewhere (a building's plan rows: direct, rentcafe, ...) only from the row's own Title, Floor plan or Unit line, so a building's description never sets one plan's size. Such a row's entry also carries its floor plan and unit name, and its cache key is marked, so only these rows are asked again and every other row's text and cached answer stay the same (no schema version bump). `extract <source>|all --units-missing` reads just these rows. **Who may rent** (`extra.extracted.restriction`): `{value, evidence}` with value income, read only from explicit eligibility words (income limits, a % of AMI, income-restricted, low-income, subsidized, tax credit / LIHTC, voucher holders only, project-based Section 8 / PBV, a BMR program unit, 'affordable housing' as a program; an income quote counts only when it holds such words, `extract.restriction_rank`: rank 2 a limit, AMI, subsidy, voucher program or tax credit, rank 1 an affordable housing community / development, 'N affordable units' or BMR, rank 0 = no evidence, so 'affordable garden apartments' as a price word or a 'Below-Market Rates' menu label is unknown; Haiku returns the strongest quote and optionally a second, `evidence_2`, and the higher-ranked one leads), senior (55+, 62+), veterans (VASH), student, employer (employees only) or other (disability, supportive, transitional), kept only with a verbatim quote of 2+ normalized characters ('(55+)' -> '55'); `{value: 'none'}` (with `evidence` only when it quotes an explicit market-rate statement: 'market-rate', 'no income restrictions') when the entry sets no limit (accepting Section 8 or vouchers is none; so are a minimum income to qualify, 'income 3x rent', and house or booking rules: 21+, no children, vaccinated guests, reviews needed), kept only when the entry is `described` (40+ normalized characters beyond its Title, Name, Amenities and source-extra lines: a description, an about text, a detail page), since a name or plan name alone would never state a limit (`extract.check_restriction`, shared with render); a claimed limit whose quote is not in the text is dropped (unknown). `extract <source> --recheck-none` applies the cached answers again (free) to listings whose current reading says none from a text that is not described, so they become unknown; `extract <source> --recheck-restriction VALUE` does the same for listings whose current reading is VALUE (after a stricter check; cached answers come from the old prompt, so a reading can only stay or become unknown, never get a better quote). A RentCafe row under rentcafe.com/housing/ (`extra.vertical` housing) read as none stays unknown: that section holds income-restricted and market-rate buildings alike (Alta Torre, 1 bd $880, no text; 6tenEast, $3,841 to $7,267, a market-rate description; single houses), so it is a needs_check, never a pass or a fail. Answers are cached by schema version, model and text (`data/extract_cache.json`, merged under a lock, so parallel runs share it). A stronger model's reading survives a re-scrape (`extract.keep_stronger`): when a listing's newest reading is by a stronger model than the run's (a Sonnet reading newer than any Haiku one) and that model's cached answer is for the very same text (same schema, same entry), the listing keeps that answer and model instead of being read again; a changed text, or a newer reading by the run's model, ends it. **Hook:** `aptf-intake run <text source>` reads the run's rows right after it (each new scraped row supersedes the listing's reading; unchanged text is cached, free), `--no-extract` skips, `--workers` / `--extract-max-usd` apply; `reparse` applies cached readings only (no calls, so a reparse stays free). **Free follow-up (same hook, `after_intake` -> `after_steps`):** after any `run` or `reparse` that wrote rows, of any source, and after the reading (even when the reading is blocked): geocode the address strings not in `data/geocode_cache.json` (`identity.addresses.geocode`, its run and monthly caps; skipped with a note when `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` is unset; never on `reparse`, which stays call-free), rebuild identity (an `identity` run; the only rebuild of a CLI `run` or `reparse`, which with `--no-followup` leave identity for the next one), then straight-line estimates (`trips.estimate_missing`, a `commute`/`estimate` run) for the routable places with no estimate under the current point ids, the Pipeline panel's rule; places that have estimates are not routed again. One summary line, e.g. `after intake: geocoded 12 (cached 340), identity rebuilt (16 links), estimates for 9 new places (279 trips)`. `--no-followup` skips the three steps.
- **Baths an own site leaves out (`plans.inherit_baths`; `python -m aptfinder.planread`).** Applied at read time by the filter (`goal_inputs`, after `inherit_prices`) and the drawer, never stored on the row, so the user's hand value always wins: only an active `direct` plan row (not `site_info`) with known beds and unknown baths after the user's corrections is filled. First **borrowed**: another source's active listing at the same place with the same beds and kind of offer (`plans.offer_kind`) and sqft within 3% of the row's, or the same plan name when that name is more than a bed count ('Studio', '2 BR', 'One Bedroom' and Zillow's '3bd' name no plan); taken only when every such listing gives the same baths (`baths_from` {how 'borrowed', source, source_id, n}); a Zillow building's bedroom row ('3bd') names no plan itself, but the plans its building page lists for that bed count (`extra.building_plans`: name, beds, baths, sqft) do, so an own-site plan of the same distinctive name and beds takes that named plan's baths; when they disagree the row stays unknown and the disagreement is reported (dry run). Else **read from the row's own drawing**: `planread` sends each stored drawing (`extra.floorplan_image`, else an own-site `plan_drawings` label naming the plan; the file from the photo store) of a still-unknown row with known beds to Haiku once (`claude -p`, no tools, the JPEG as a base64 image block over `--input-format stream-json`, no file name, so a URL such as 'plan-2d-1052sqft-2x2' cannot leak in), asking is it a floor plan, bedrooms, full and half baths, confident, and what it saw; the answers are kept in the run's `counts.readings` keyed by the stored file's sha256 (a `runs` row, component `planread`, est_cost the Claude spend; read again only under a new prompt `VERSION`), and applied when it is a floor plan, confident, its bedrooms equal the row's, and full baths are 1 to beds + 2 (baths = full + half / 2) (`baths_from` {how 'drawing', seen, file}). Both are lower trust: the size rule's reason says 'baths from <source>' or 'baths read from the floor plan drawing', and the drawer marks the value (≈ borrowed, ▣ drawing; hover says from where or what Haiku saw). `--dry-run` counts the direct rows without baths, the borrowed, the disagreements, and the drawings to read, with the rows; `--max-usd` (default 0.5) caps the spend.
- Untested leads carried over from the original brainstorm (`handoff.md`, Apify store listings, never run):

  | Source | Actor to try | Listed price | Caveats noted then |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Zillow, Apartments.com, Realtor.com, Zumper, Redfin | `tri_angle/real-estate-aggregator` | from $1.50 / 1k | one call, deduped output; verify rental coverage per provider |
  | Property-manager sites (RentCafe, Entrata, RealPage, AppFolio, Buildium) | `housingfeed/rental-listings-api` | unknown | its pages disagree on whether Zillow and Apartments.com are included; low usage |
  | Craigslist (sfbay) | `lentic_clockss/craigslist-housing-scraper` | $2 / 1k | |
  | Airbnb | `tri_angle/airbnb-scraper` | $1.25 / 1k | Apify-maintained; had an empty-price bug; fallback `tri_angle/airbnb-fast-scraper`. Used 2026-11-24, replaced 2026-11-25 by `kaix/airbnb-listing-scraper` (see the decision log) |
  | Furnished Finder | `rigelbytes/furnishedfinder` or `datascrapers/furnishedfinder` | about $1.20 / 1k | community-built; site behind Cloudflare, may need residential proxy |

  For Airbnb, query with real check-in and check-out dates at least 28 days apart so the returned price is the monthly-discounted one; Airbnb quotes a stay of 28+ nights per month ("monthly"), so `price_basis = month`, with the query dates recorded on the observation. Blueground has no known actor yet.

  **Airbnb box coverage (`intake/airbnb.py` `fetch`).** Each station box is one kaix coordinate search (centre, radius half the diagonal). kaix logs each search page ("Search: found N listings", 18 a page, at most 15 pages = 270); a search whose last page was short and that returned fewer than `max_items` ended on its own. A search that did not (cap reached, all pages full, or no page lines in the log) is split into 4 quadrants, each searched the same way, down to `MAX_SPLIT_DEPTH` = 2 (at most 1 + 4 + 16 searches); results are merged by listing id into one raw file and still attributed to boxes by coordinates (`area_of`). The box is complete when every leaf search ended on its own, partial when a leaf at the last depth was still capped, a search failed, or the run's money ran out (`ActorRun.complete`, which replaces the item-cap test in `run_source`). Absence from a complete Airbnb run still never retires a listing (liveness): the search returns only stays free on the queried dates, ranked.

### identity (`aptf-identity`)
- Goal: decide which listings are the same place and unit.
- Inputs: `latest_obs`, `current_links`.
- Outputs: appended `links` rows (rules: `addr_unit` = normalized address plus unit; `geo_near_address` = a coordinate-only listing within 80 m of an address-keyed building (a property name or 3+ listings), never for Airbnb, whose pins are offset; `geo_beds_price` = lat/lon rounded to 4 decimals plus beds plus price otherwise).
- Obvious duplicate buildings (`identity/same_site.py`, author `auto-merge:same-site-100m`): after the rules above, two rule places whose own website is the same building page (`config.site_key`: host and path, query kept) and that sit under 100 m apart (the closest pair of their listings whose address Google placed precisely, Airbnb pins left out; else the places' centres) become one place: the merged place's listings get rule links to the kept place, each keeping its unit ('<merged>/<unit>' -> '<kept>/<unit>', as the drawer's merge). Own website = a site-list entry's or a site search's (address check not failed) place, a `direct` site scraped there, or the website a listing names. Never evidence: an aggregator host (`discover.is_aggregator`), a bare homepage (path '/' or empty: a manager's homepage like zell.com, caprop.com, heritagerealtysanmateo.com covers many buildings, and a building's own domain homepage is left out with it), an operator's portfolio or city page (every path segment a list word or a city: '/apartments/ca/mountain-view'), a URL more than 6 places share. Left out: pairs you answered 'not the same building', third-party stay places (`~`), places you split off (`u_`). Pairs join into groups; a group keeps the place it kept last run, else the canonical site-list entry's place, else the one with more listings. Recomputed from the rule decisions every run (inside `identity.run`, so after geocoding and on new data), so it is idempotent; it is a rule link, so a user link (split, drawer merge) still wins, and answering 'not the same building' on the pair (annotation place_pair / 'a|b' / same_building = no) puts both places back on the next run.
- Address ranges (`identity/ranges.py`, author `auto-merge:address-range`), applied before same-site: a place whose address is a house-number range on one street ('1222-1244 University Dr': no spaces, low < high; a spaced hyphen or a comma is a list, read as its first number) absorbs every rule place on the same street, city and ZIP (from the listing, the geocode cache or the address text; unknown ZIP never merges) whose single house number falls inside the range, when the two share a property name (lowercased, punctuation and 'the', 'apartments', 'apts', 'homes', 'townhomes', 'residences' dropped) or an operator. A range alone never merges. The range place is kept; an absorbed listing's unit is its house number ('<range>/1226', '<range>/1226-b' when it names unit B), so townhouses stay separate units and the sources listing one townhouse share one. Left out: `~` and `u_` places, pairs you answered 'not the same building', and a place you merged or split by hand (a current user link points at it or at one of its listings: moving its rule listings would leave the hand-linked ones behind); a number inside two ranges joins the narrower. `aptf-identity ranges` prints what it would merge and the near misses (inside a range, no shared name or operator, or ZIP missing or different) without writing.
- Geocoding (`identity/addresses.py`): `aptf-identity geocode` reads each new address string with Google (cache `data/geocode_cache.json` for the parts, a `geocodes` row for the position, metered in `api_calls`, capped by `geocoding_monthly_call_cap`); `aptf-identity positions [--dry-run]` geocodes again, for its position only, each Craigslist address at a place no other source places that the cache placed precisely but has no `geocodes` row (cached before 2026-11-25). Positions never change links.
- Must not: overrule a `user` link; append a row that repeats the current decision.

### commute (`aptf-commute`)
- Goal: minutes and legs from coordinate A to coordinate B by a mode. Fully independent: no imports from the rest of `aptfinder`, no knowledge of apartments, goals or the main schema.
- Inputs: two `(lat, lon)` pairs, a mode, a `LegSource`, a SQLite path for its cache, station list and train table (passed in by the caller).
- Outputs: `Trip(minutes, distance_m, legs, provider, approximate)`.
- `LegSource` answers "minutes from A to B by walk, bike, drive or transit". Implementations: `GoogleRoutesLegSource` (Routes API, `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY`; `matrix(origins, dests, mode)` calls Compute Route Matrix, walk, bike, drive and transit, at most 625 elements a request, 100 for transit (Google's limit; `Commute.prefetch` splits transit requests at 100); transit sends `departureTime` = `next_departure(depart)`, a future weekday time, never 'now'; transit matrix elements carry no steps), `StraightLineLegSource` (haversine times a detour factor at a fixed speed; free, labelled `estimate`), and a fake for tests.
- `Commute.prefetch(legs)`: fills the cache for many single legs with the source's `matrix`, one request per destination and mode (up to 625 origins); later `leg` / `trip` calls are cache hits. Matrix legs are cached under provider `google` (the same router as computeRoutes).
- Cache: the module owns one table, `commute_legs`, in the database path it is given, keyed on origin and destination rounded to 4 decimals, mode, departure assumption and provider. Every single leg goes through it; composed modes are built from cached legs, so station-to-workplace legs are fetched once ever and each new apartment costs only its own legs. Callers never manage cache state.
- Modes: `walk`, `bike`, `drive` (no traffic), `transit`, `bike_train_bike` (bike to each of the nearest k stations, in-train time from `config/caltrain.toml` plus half the headway as wait, bike from the far station; fastest kept), `bike_bus_bike` (Google transit route with walking legs re-timed as bike legs; labelled approximate). Both composed modes also consider biking the whole way, so they are never slower than plain bike.
- `nearest(origin, points, mode, k)`: shortlist by straight line, route to the top k.
- Wait at a station is half the peak gap between trains across all patterns (`peak_trains_per_hour`); `bike_bus_bike` adds the transfer/wait time Google's route includes beyond its step durations.
- Must not: read or write any table but `commute_legs`.
- **Glue (`aptfinder/trips.py`, CLI `aptf-commute`)** is the only code that joins commute with the store: `route` for an ad-hoc A to B, `compute` to append `trips` for places per `config/point_sets.toml`. The free estimate provider covers every place: `estimate_missing` (after every intake run, and in the served dashboard at start and whenever `point_sets.toml` or a `config/points/` file changes) routes the places missing an estimate row for some (point, mode) the current config asks of them (each set's k nearest points by each of its `modes`, `missing_estimates`; the Pipeline panel's estimates stage counts the same), so a point added to a set reaches the places it is now nearest to; a paid provider respects each set's `prefilter_km`, per-mode `max_km` and `auto` (point name to the modes Google routes by itself; missing means every mode) and routes the full `modes` table only on demand and a `--max-calls` cap per run, with calls and estimated cost logged in `runs`. A trip row is appended only when none exists for that place, point, mode and provider or its minutes changed. `sample` (CLI; the dashboard's Pipeline panel does the same, `aptfinder/sampling.py`) routes, for (destination, mode) pairs, a plan over a scope of places with Google: `cover` (greedy set cover: repeatedly the place that brings the most not-yet-covered places within R m of a Google-routed place; places already routed count as covering; for a set, 'routed' means the place's fastest point of the set, what the list shows, is its own Google trip, so a place whose Google trip goes to a stop that is no longer its nearest is routed again), `random` (pct % of the scope, the same places for every pair) or `refine` (over the places that can still pass a goal apart from commute rules (`sampling.candidate_goals`, the `routing_candidates` logic, in every scope) and only for goals with a commute rule on the pair: a KNN range that straddles that goal's limit, or is wider than `REFINE_MAX_SPREAD_MIN` = 2 min; estimate-only places are counted for the dry run's 'use cover' line, not picked). The candidates scope (CLI `sample`, the dashboard's 'search candidates') is every place that can still pass a goal apart from commute rules, in a walk zone or not, and its cover takes per pair only the candidates of a goal with a commute rule on that pair (`sampling.goal_cover_plan`: transit to Downtown Palo Alto covers `temp`'s stays, drive to Meta HQ `lease`'s homes). The legs a plan needs (`trips.planned_legs`: the same code with a recording source over an in-memory copy of the places; reads the cache, writes nothing) go through Compute Route Matrix (`trips.MeteredMatrix`: one `api_calls` row per element, provider `google_matrix`, `matrix_monthly_element_cap` in `config/google.toml`, paced under 3,000 elements a minute) into the leg cache, then `compute_all` appends the trips from it (`trips.route_plan`, one `commute` run row per pair). Stale trips (a merge or a pin rule moved the place more than 30 m from a trip's origin): `compute_for_place` appends again when the latest row is stale even if the minutes are equal, `missing_estimates` counts only current estimates (so moved places are estimated again for free), and `aptf-commute reroute-stale [--dry-run] [--max-elements N]` routes the stale Google (point, mode) pairs again through `route_plan` (`stale_plan`). `aptf-commute adopt-origins` was the one-time step for rows routed before origins were kept (below).

### filter (`aptf-filter`)
- Goal: evaluate each goal over the derived views and `trips`.
- Inputs: `listings`, `places`, `trips`, the effective goal set (`config/goals.toml` overlaid by goal annotations, `filter.goals`), per-source accuracy from verify. `goal_inputs` reads everything once; `evaluate_goal` judges one goal over it (`evaluate_all` runs it per goal; the dashboard's draft preview runs it on one unsaved goal).
- Outputs: per listing and goal, `pass`, `fail` (with reasons) or `needs_check` (naming the missing or untrusted field: a value unknown, disputed, or an estimate / KNN range that cannot tell; a known value just over a limit, a Google 21.1 min against 21 or $2,612 a person against $2,500, is a fail, never a check: the limits already allow the margin). Per place and goal one more outcome, **`no_listings`** (`filter.place_outcome`; not a rejection, its own bucket, sorted after needs_check): a *plan* is a deciding row with beds or a price known (a RentCafe, Google Places or Zillow building row with neither offers nothing); an *offer* is a plan with a price that its source does not mark unavailable (`filter.unavailable_label`, structured fields only: Apartments.com `extra.availability` '0 Available units' and plan name 'No availability', an own site's `extra.available_label` 'Unavailable on selected date', 'Call for details', 'Contact for Availability', 'WAITLIST CLOSED'). Some offer does not fail -> the best offer decides (pass or needs_check, as below). Else some plan does not fail (beds/baths pass or unknown, no other rule fails) -> `no_listings`, `why` `no_price` ('no price for a fitting plan (4 fit)') or `unavailable` (every fitting plan so marked: 'fitting plan unavailable ("Unavailable on selected date")'). Else plans that all fail -> fail. No plan at all (a site_info-only place, a building row alone) -> `no_listings`, `why` `none`, 'no offers known'. Every place has a result for every goal (`goal_inputs` `places`). An own site's price-less plan rows (`goal_inputs` `unpriced`) still are no offers, but count here as plans: one that fits makes the place no listings, none fitting makes it a fail. Where any deciding row is active, only active rows count. A place's outcome is otherwise the best outcome among its offers (its active listings first: a listing that has left the market decides only a place with no active listing), except that **the own website wins**: at a place whose own site currently prices an offer (an active `direct` row with a price, not `site_info`; `plans.own_site_places`), only those own-site rows decide the place, and other sources' listings there keep their result (`decides: false`), stay stored and shown as 'also listed', and nominate nothing for paid routing.
- Who may rent (`restriction`, an `equals` field): your answer in Verify (annotation place / `restriction`, comment the value, evidence 'marked in Verify' (you)) wins; else a limit any listing or `site_info` row at a place states (extract's reading, e.g. the RentCafe building row 'PBV - Belovida (VASH)', or an own site's pages read by render, `extra.page_restriction`, source 'direct page') holds for every listing there, like pooled amenities (`filter.row_restriction`: a row's limit from either reading first, then either's none); otherwise a listing's own reading ('none'); nothing read at all (not read yet, a name alone, a RentCafe /housing/ row with nothing stated) is **assumed none** for the goals (`filter.ASSUMED_NONE`, evidence 'no limit stated — assumed none', source assumed; the user's decision: no limit unless one is stated), so `equals restriction none` passes; only a quoted limit, an affordable-housing operator or your Verify answer restricts. The stored readings keep unknown (extract's 'none needs real text' still decides what is stored); the assumption is made at read time only. **Operators** (`config.operator_restriction`, `config/operators.toml`): a place where a listing gives its own site on an operator's domain (a direct or portfolio row's URL, or any `extra.website`: Google Places, extract; host or subdomain) or names the operator in its operator field takes that operator's reading, below any quoted limit: an affordable-housing nonprofit or housing authority (MidPen, Alta, Charities, First Community, Eden, Mercy, BRIDGE, Housing Choices, EAH, the San Mateo and Santa Clara housing authorities) -> income, evidence 'operator: MidPen Housing (affordable-housing nonprofit)', source operator, also over a listing's none, unless a page there says market-rate in so many words (a none with its quote); a market-rate REIT or manager (AvalonBay, Prometheus, Essex, Equity, Greystar, Irvine, UDR, ...) -> none, evidence 'operator: AvalonBay (market-rate)', only where nothing was read.
