The card badge (`place_restriction`) shows a limit only; the drawer says 'no limit stated — assumed none', with its basis when one exists (a page's market-rate quote, 'operator: AvalonBay (market-rate)'). A failure names the quote and its source ('restriction = none: is veterans, "(VASH)" (rentcafe)'); `fact` carries it as `ev`.
- Parking (`parking_included`, an `equals` field true / false; `parking_spots` and `parking_usd_month`, numeric fields for `min` / `max`), from extract's `parking` reading and an own site's pages (render, `extra.page_parking`, source 'direct page'). Per reading (`filter.parking_values`): offered no -> included false, spots the stated number or 0, price unknown; offered yes -> included true when the cost is included (price 0), false when paid (price `usd_month`, unknown if not stated), unknown when the cost is unspecified; spots as stated, else unknown; offered unknown -> all unknown. Each field comes from the first reading that knows it (`filter.parking_fields`): the row's own readings (extract's, then its site's pages), then the building statements at its place (`filter.row_parking`): an own site's pages, and extract's readings of building-level sources (Apartments.com, RentCafe, own-site rows, Google Places, portfolio, Zillow building rows `<zpid>:Nbd`); a Craigslist, Airbnb, Furnished Finder, Zumper or PadMapper post, or a Zillow unit, speaks for its own home only. Unknown -> needs_check; a failure names the quotes and their source (`parking_included = True: is False, "Garage parking $150/month" (apartments_com)`), carried as `ev`.
- Parking cost (`parking_cost` rule, `{spots}`; editor 'parking cost for N spots'; meant as a nice-to-have, it only ranks): what `spots` spaces add to the rent a month, from ONE parking reading, never fields mixed from two (`rules.parking_cost`): the first of the row's ordered readings (the same order as `parking_fields`: its own, then its place's building statements) that says no parking, included or paid; a reading with the cost unstated is skipped. When none of them does, the one reading the place's card shows (`filter.place_parking` / `best_parking`: the most informative reading of any listing there, unit posts included) is read the same way, its why ending 'from another listing here' (`rules.row_parking_cost`, shared by the rule, its fact and the place result); the rule fields (`parking_included` and the rest) do not use it. Included and stating at least `spots` spaces -> $0 (pass); paid with a monthly price -> `spots` x that price (each space at the stated price; a stated limit on spaces is not read) (fail, with the cost); no parking -> fail ('no parking'); included with fewer or no stated spaces, paid without a price, nothing stated -> needs_check naming what is missing. Each place result of a goal with such a rule carries `parking_cost` {usd, spots, none, why, ev}; the card shows '🅿 +$150/mo for 2 spots' / '$0 for 2 spots' / '? for 2 spots' under that goal (the reading on hover), the drawer beside the goal's status and in its nice-to-have line, and the list sorts on it ('parking cost (low to high)': known costs, then unknown, then no parking).
- Units in the building (`building_units`, a numeric field for `min` / `max`; mainly tells an apartment building from a house), one value per place from its listings' best evidence (`filter.building.place_building_units`, read at query time, nothing stored), the same on every listing there: (1) a stated total: RentCafe `extra.units_total` (0 = not given), else a count in a listing's own title or description by a strict pattern ('unit' next to a building noun: 'a 44-unit community', 'apartment building with 12 units', 'only two units in the building'); (2) a house type -> 1 (single-family or townhome: Zillow kind house / townhome, Apartments.com SingleFamily / Townhouse, Craigslist house / townhouse, Furnished Finder House / Room - House, Airbnb '… home'; Craigslist duplex -> 2), unless (3) says more, and unknown (needs_check, the card names both) when another row at the place, or the same Zillow card's `apartmentComplex` group, says apartment building (64 places on 2026-11-25, nearly all Zillow townhomes inside an apartment complex card); (3) a floor: the most distinct units or floor plans (unit / floor_plan labels) one source lists there, rooms and site_info rows excluded, or the most homes a Zillow `apartmentComplex` search card at the place groups (`matchingHomeCount`, kept as `extra.complex_home_count`: the homes matching the search, so a lower bound, never the complex's total; Zillow gives no total; the max over the place's cards, never their sum), when 2 or more. A floor is a lower bound (`building_units_floor`): a `min` rule passes when it already reaches the limit, a `max` fails when it already exceeds it, anything else is needs_check, never fail. A type that only says apartment (Google Places apartment_building / complex, Zillow apartmentComplex, Zumper / PadMapper type 4, an Apartments.com, RentCafe, own-site or portfolio community, Craigslist apartment / condo, Airbnb rental unit / condo, Furnished Finder Apartment / Condo) gives no number: unknown, needs_check, the card names the type. Evidence and source (`building_units_evidence`) go into the rule's reason and the card's hover. Card and drawer: '🏢 167 units (rentcafe)', '🏢 ≥ 7 units (apartments_com)' (floor), '🏠 single-family (zillow)', '🏢 apartment building, units ? (google_places)'. Editor preset 'units in building at least' = `min building_units 5`.
- Apartment building (`is_apartment_building`, an `equals` field, true / false), from the same evidence (`place_building_units`' `apartment_building`, read at query time): true when the value or its floor reaches 5 units, or a building-level type says apartment building (Zillow `apartmentComplex`, Google Places apartment_building / apartment_complex / condominium_complex, Zumper / PadMapper type 4, a RentCafe, Apartments.com community, own-site or portfolio listing) and no listing at the place gives a house type; false when a house type decided the value (single-family, townhome, duplex); unknown (needs_check) otherwise: a house type against apartment evidence, a floor of 2 to 4 on a Zillow townhome card, unit-level types only (a Craigslist, Airbnb or Furnished Finder 'apartment'), a stated 2 to 4 units. The reason names the evidence (`is_apartment_building_evidence`). Editor preset 'apartment building' = `equals is_apartment_building true`.
- Dates (`dates_cover` rule, `{start, end}`): a listing passes when its available-from / available-to cover the range; unknown dates are needs_check. A stated minimum stay longer than the stay (end - start) fails whatever the dates (`filter.min_stay`, structured fields only: `extra.min_stay_days`, Furnished Finder `minimum_stay_days`, Zumper `min_lease_days`, Apartments.com `min_lease_months` x 30; 'minimum stay 180 days (minimum_stay_days), the stay is 35').
- Who rents it out (`not_operator` rule, `{name}`; editor 'not rented out by', a select of the operators in `config/operators.toml`, `GET /api/goals` `operators`): per listing, never pooled per place (`filter.listing_operators`, read time, nothing stored; a stay operator reposts single units of large complexes such as The Crossings). A listing is that operator's when a lister name it carries matches the operator's `names` (exact, any case) or its own-site URL one of its `domains`: the operator field; Apartments.com `extra.management_company`; Zumper `extra.brokerage`; a PadMapper row's Zumper twin (the same listing id: one company, one listing); a Zillow unit's detail page `attribution_info` brokerName / agentName (`zillow_detail.page_listers`, raw pages, cached until a raw file changes; a building's bedroom rows `<zpid>:Nbd` are not read, their page is one representative unit's). It fails with how it was named ('not Blueground: is Blueground, Zillow page lister 'Blueground Furnished Apartments''); with nothing naming the operator it passes, not needs_check: an exclusion has no missing input (most offers name no operator), and the fact it reads is evidence, never an assumption.
- Must not: write to the database; drop anything.

### verify (`aptf-verify`)
- Goal: measure data accuracy instead of assuming it.
- Sample queue: seeded random sample drawn evenly across sources (round robin, not proportional to volume); computed trips sampled the same way by provider and mode. Straight-line estimate trips are never sampled. A listing leaves the queue once checked and returns if a checked field's value changes. Accuracy counts one vote per (listing, field, checked value).
- Conflict queue: units with more than one source disagreeing on price, beds, baths, sqft or availability (two availability dates on or before today agree: both say 'available now'), compared over **active** listings only (a retired plan never makes a conflict), and never at a place whose own website prices an offer (the own site is the truth there; disagreements among other sources, cross-source or text, are moot). Shown first. A conflict at a place whose own site has not been rendered yet (a `direct` row exists, no `extra.platform = 'render'` row, site not Avalon: `verify.pending_render`) carries `pending_render` and a reason and goes last, after the text conflicts: the render will likely settle it. `verify.all_conflicts` gives that order.
- Too-cheap listings (`verify.cheap_queue`): an active, priced, monthly lease listing (not `term = short`, not `kind = room`, beds known) whose price per bedroom is below `[verify] min_price_per_bed` (default $1,000; a studio counts as 1 bedroom) is a one-listing conflict tagged `cheap`, with the reason 'price per bedroom $320 < $1,000: check for per-room or misread price', lowest $/bed first, after the text conflicts and before the pending ones. Furnished long leases are included. It shows price (to check) and beds (optional: a wrong beds corrects $/bed through the override); it leaves once the price has a current check (right: the price is real; wrong: the true monthly rent overrides it), or once the place has a `restriction` annotation: the item's third answer, buttons 'income-restricted' and 'other restriction' (`POST /api/annotations` place / restriction / income or other), says the low rent is real because the place limits who may rent; the goals' `restriction` rule reads it (above), so under `equals restriction none` the place fails. A limit read by Haiku does not drop the item by itself (under a goal with that rule, the goal filter hides it as settled). Counted with the conflicts.
- No longer for rent (`verify.stale_queue`): an active Zillow listing whose newest detail page (see intake) states a non-rental `home_status` (FOR_SALE, SOLD, OFF_MARKET, ...) is a one-listing conflict tagged `stale` ('Zillow's page now says for sale, not for rent (latest: 2026-11-24 Price change $6,800): still offered?', the page's newest price-history event), after the too-cheap ones, on the price: ✓ still offered at it, or the item's **no longer for rent** button. It leaves once the price has a current check, a newer page says FOR_RENT, or the listing is off the market; a page with no status flags nothing.
- Off the market (view `withdrawals`, read by `listings_base.active`): the newest withdrawal evidence of a listing decides. Gone: the user's **no longer for rent** mark (Verify stale items, and a small button per active listing in the place drawer; `POST /api/listings/gone`), an appended `checks` row on the listing's newest observation with field `active`, stored_value `"offered"`, verdict `wrong`, true_value `"gone"` (queue `conflict` from Verify, `adhoc` from the drawer); or a Zillow detail page that says SOLD / RECENTLY_SOLD, or any other non-rental status whose newest price-history event is 'Listing removed' (retired without a Verify item). Offered: the user's undo (the same row with stored `"gone"`, true `"offered"`), a FOR_RENT detail page, or a fresh scrape that saw the listing (an `intake` run that is not a reparse; extract and reparse runs re-append old data and prove nothing). Gone evidence counts only when newer than the listing's newest fresh observation. An ambiguous page (FOR_SALE with a recent 'Listed for rent', or no event) is neither and stays a Verify item. `listings` carries `gone_at`, `gone_by` (`user` or `zillow_detail`) and `gone_why` (the page's event); the drawer shows 'you marked it gone on <date> — undo' or 'Zillow's page: <event> — still for rent', both appending the offered row. An `active` check is not a field reading: `current_checks`, `accuracy` and the sample queue skip it.
- Duplicate-building list (`verify.duplicate_queue`): pairs of places that are probably one building, each with its reasons, strongest first (own website 3, address 2, name 2, summed; then nearest): **similar address** (`identity.addresses.suspected_duplicates`: same street number, street names 85%+ alike, within 150 m); **same own website** (a building's own page only: never a bare homepage, a portfolio or city page or an aggregator host, `identity.same_site.building_site_key`, so a manager's zell.com named at two buildings is no evidence; host and path, lowercase, no www., query or trailing slash: the site list or a site search found for the place (a failed address check rules one out), a `direct` site scraped at both, or the website a listing names; within 600 m; a URL shared by more than 6 places is a manager's site and ignored); **same name** (property name without 'the', 'apartments' and the like, not starting with a digit, within 200 m). Third-party stay places (`~`) and places you split off (`u_`) are left out. Bucketed by street number, URL and name, never all pairs (0.7 s on 2,718 places, plus the cached places view). A pair leaves by itself after a merge (one place is gone) or when you answer 'not the same building' (annotation `place_pair` / `<a>|<b>` sorted / `same_building` = no).
- Own-site list (`verify.own_site_queue`): named buildings (`is_named_building`) inside a station walk zone (`areas.in_walk_zone`) whose own sites give no usable offer (no active priced `direct` row), one plain reason each, the first that applies: no website known; a site is a contact/tour/lead form; a site's address check failed; a site was read but gave no plans or prices; a site's domain is dead (DNS, from the precheck); every site failed to load (blocked, timeout, empty, not returned); a website known but not checked yet. Each item lists every URL known for the building (`own_site_urls`: the site list, sites found by search or named by listings, URLs you added; listings' links to one host collapsed into one row) with its last render or address check outcome and date. An item leaves by itself once a site gives priced offers, or when you dismiss it (annotation `own_site`, until you add another URL) or say it has no website (annotation `website` = none). Adding a URL appends an annotation `website`; every such URL becomes a site of its own (`promote-sites`), beside any site the place already has, and shows at once as 'pending check'.
- Sonnet reconciler (`aptf-verify reconcile [--goal G] [--limit N] [--skip K] [--max-usd X] [--datacenter] [--dry-run] [--out F]`, `verify/reconcile.py`): open conflicts, goal-relevant first (`goal_relevance` under any goal, or `--goal`'s), then by place. Not handled: address text conflicts (a machine never overrides an address), stale Zillow items (no machine gone marks), and any field where a listing already has a current user check. For each conflict Sonnet (`claude -p --model sonnet`, no tools, subscription) reads every listing's stored fields, their values over time (`obs_history`) and its text as `extract` reads it, and decides each disputed field (a too-cheap listing also `kind` = room) with a verbatim quote and a confidence. Fields still open that a current page could settle (price, beds, baths, sqft, availability) get their listings' pages through Apify (`apify/playwright-scraper`, one page per URL, text only: no clicks, links or forms; residential by default; capped by `--max-usd` and by credit left minus $0.30; raw run under `data/raw/reconcile/`), and Sonnet decides again; Zillow and Apartments.com pages are never sent (`BLOCKED_HOSTS`: both answered 403 through the datacenter and the residential proxy in the pilot), own sites and RentCafe read through the datacenter proxy, Zumper through residential. Code accepts an answer only when: confidence high; the quote is verbatim in the evidence block it names (text or page, never the stored-fields line); the quote writes the value (the number; the count beside a bed / bath word; a date's month or 'now'); a text conflict's value is one of its two readings (a price may be a third); and on a page with three or more prices the quote names the unit (beds, plan or unit). A too-cheap item's price is accepted only with a high-confidence answer on what is offered (`kind`: a room in shared housing or the whole unit), since a real price closes the item. A machine never corrects an own-site (`direct`) row (there the disagreement is more likely two plans grouped as one unit). Two availability dates on or before today are the same fact. `--retract ID...` withdraws a reconciler check: a newer `reconcile:` row on the same observation and field with stored value null, which matches no current value, so `current_checks` holds no valid check and the conflict reopens; on a field the listing left empty the null row is itself the current check, a cannot_tell, which removes the override (the retracted row stays; refused for user checks). A cross conflict is never won by a source quoting itself (its own text or page restating its stored value proves nothing about the other source): refused for beds, baths and sqft; for a price only a current page counts, and only when every losing listing was last seen a week or more ago. Accepted answers are appended as `checks` rows (author `reconcile:sonnet`, queue `conflict`, note JSON: quote, evidence, URL, model, confidence, reason, run), one per listing (correct where it states the value, wrong with the true value elsewhere), so the conflict leaves Verify and the value overrides as a hand check would; a later user check wins (newest row). `accuracy` and the sample queue read only non-`reconcile:` checks. A `runs` row (component `verify`, source `reconcile`) holds the counts and the Apify estimate. **Stored pages** (`verify/stored.py`, on by default, `--no-stored` to leave out): every raw page we already hold for each listing of the conflict is an evidence block `S<n>` with its fetch date (the raw file's run, its own `fetched_at`, or a date in its name; never the listing's last observation, since rows are re-observed from old files): the listing's raw item in its `raw_path` for every source with a parser (Apartments.com, Zumper, PadMapper, RentCafe, Craigslist, Airbnb, Furnished Finder, ...; found by re-running the parser), Zillow's newest detail page and building page (a building's bedroom row gets only the detail page's building lines and the building page's plans), an own site's rendered pages (`site_text`) or its Prometheus availability JSON. A raw item is written as plain lines, one per field and one per list element ('rentals[8]: Beds: 2; BedsText: 2 Beds; Rent: 3895; UnitNumber: 2586-008; ...'), the lines naming the listing's unit or plan first, at most 14,000 characters a block; keys that state nothing about the listing are left out (photos, links, ids, reviews, schools, nearby, similar or other listings, price history, Zestimates, agents, feature-code lists). The quote gate is unchanged, and an S block counts as its own listing's evidence (a cross conflict is never won by a listing's stored page restating its stored value; only a current page `P` can win a price that way). The check's note names the stored page (`stored`: label and fetch date). **One listing on request** (`--listing SOURCE:ID --field F [--field G]`, `reconcile.reconcile_listing`): Sonnet reads chosen fields of one listing under the same acceptance, for a fact the user saw that no conflict raised; an accepted answer is a `reconcile:sonnet` check in queue `adhoc`. Sonnet is told that a month with no day ('beginning March') is the 1st of the next such month from today, as 'available now' is today.
- `aptf-verify samples --source <s> --n 5 --seed N`: n random rows of one source (seeded, over its rows sorted by source_id, so the same seed picks the same rows on any copy), each with the page to open (Google Maps place, Zumper/PadMapper listing, operator community page) and every stored field, `(missing)` named, for checking by hand against screenshots.
- Outputs: `checks` rows from the dashboard; accuracy per source and field with sample size and a Wilson 95% interval; cross-source agreement rate. A check whose stored value is empty (the user filling in what the source never stated, e.g. the drawer's 'set ba') is no vote on the source's accuracy.
- A source whose measured accuracy on a field is below the threshold in `goals.toml` (with at least the minimum sample) gets that field treated as `needs_check` by the filter.

### media (`aptf-media`, `aptfinder/media.py`)
- Goal: our own copy of every listing photo and floor-plan drawing, because image URLs change or vanish and some hosts refuse hotlinking. Downloads only image files at URLs observations already carry (`data.media`, `extra.images` as strings or `{url|src}`, drawings `extra.floorplan_image` / `extra.plan_image` / the URLs of own-site `extra.plan_drawings`), directly from the machine it runs on (AGENTS.md rule 6 exception). Run on the box: `nix run .#media -- fetch` (resumable: URLs stored OK are skipped; failures are retried only with `--retry-failed`; `--limit N` for a pilot), `nix run .#media -- status`.
- Store: each image re-encoded to JPEG, photos at most 800 px on the long side at quality 75, drawings 1,280 px at 80 so their text stays readable, never upscaled (EXIF orientation applied, transparency on white). Content-addressed: `data/media/<sha256[:2]>/<sha256>.jpg`, the name being the hash of the stored bytes.
- Deduplicated in the write path, from the first download: downloads run in threads (at most 4 per host, 0.25 s apart, 429/5xx/network errors retried with backoff; a host that refuses 20 in a row with 401/403 is skipped for the rest of the run), and one serial writer decides for each: the same bytes already stored -> `same_as` itself; else a stored file of the same place (the URL's place, or its listing before it has one) and kind within `PHOTO_MAX_DISTANCE` = 6 pHash bits (drawings: 0, only against drawings) -> `same_as` that file; else write. Whichever copy arrived first is the stored one; a larger later copy is not re-written (its original size is in its row, so the better source is derivable).
- Cap: before each write the running total of `stored_bytes` is checked against `max_gb`; at the cap the run stops and logs "media store full (5.0 GB)" (`runs.counts.full`, shown by the pipeline's Photos stage). So URLs go in priority order: active listings first, then places that pass or need a check on a goal, then drawings, then the first 12 photos of each listing, then the rest.
- Each run is a `runs` row (component `media`) with counts per outcome and host.
- The image libraries are checked before the run starts (`media.check_decoders`: import Pillow, imagehash and cairosvg and encode a tiny PNG and SVG in the main thread): if one does not load, `fetch` exits with the library's error and writes no run and no `media` row. An `ImportError` during a download also aborts the run (closed `aborted`), never a row 'not an image'. Both entry points work on NixOS: `nix run .#media` (the Nix build's own libraries) and `nix develop -c uv run aptf-media` (the dev shell puts libstdc++, zlib and cairo on `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` on NixOS only, for uv's PyPI wheels of numpy and cairocffi).
- Hosts in `[media] curl_hosts` (Furnished Finder's `staticproperties.furnishedfinder.com`) are downloaded with the `curl` binary (`--fail`, 30 s timeout, same UA and Referer, no proxy), because they answer 403 to Python's HTTP client and 200 to curl from the same machine; everything after the download (writer, dedupe, cap, log) is the same. SVG drawings (Prometheus) are rasterised with cairosvg (`unsafe=False`: no entities, no external fetches) at 1,280 px and stored as JPEG like any drawing. Drawings dedupe on identical stored bytes only.
- Hosts that refuse this machine (`[media] residential_hosts`, empty today; it did not help Furnished Finder) go through Apify's US residential proxy (`groups-RESIDENTIAL,country-US`; the proxy password is read from the Apify API with `APIFY_TOKEN` at run time, never printed or stored). It bills by traffic (about $8 per GB), so bytes through it are counted (`runs.counts.proxy_bytes`) and capped per run (`residential_max_mb`, default 300), and a smaller variant the host serves is tried first (Furnished Finder: `-650-570` instead of the `-full` original). The `media` row keeps the URL the source gave. `--host H` limits a run to one host; with `--retry-failed` it retries that host's earlier 403s (new rows).

### web (`aptf-web`)
- Goal: the dashboard. FastAPI bound to `127.0.0.1`, JSON API plus one static page (plain JavaScript and Leaflet, no build step).
- Map: Esri World Imagery satellite with a label overlay, toggle to OpenStreetMap. A service worker caches tiles cache-first. Google tiles are not used (their terms forbid caching).
- Goal switcher (a dropdown in the header: All goals plus every goal by name); pins coloured pass / fail (dimmed) / needs-check / no listings (muted slate, small, `--none`); point sets as toggleable layers; station pins show service tier and other transit.
- Card list sortable and filterable by price, price per bed, beds/baths, net commute, rating, status. Under a goal, price sorts read the goal's offer (the price its card shows); under a goal with `people` the 'price / bed' sort is labelled and sorts as 'price / person' (that price ÷ people, as on the card), also the status sort's tiebreak. A card shows a dashed badge ('income-restricted', 'seniors only', 'veterans only', 'students only', 'employees only', 'restricted') when any listing at the place states a limit on who may rent (`filter.place_restriction`, also on the drawer's title), its quote and source on hover; red or amber like other facts when the selected goal's restriction rule fails or needs a check on it. A card's commute facts are the selected goal's commute rules (must-haves and nice-to-haves, each 'drive to Meta HQ 17.7 min (est.)', coloured by the goal's fails) and its net commute; under All, none. Parking shows as '🅿 1 covered, $75/mo', '🅿 garage, cost unspecified', '🅿 street parking only' (`filter.place_parking`: the most informative reading of any listing at the place, cost or no-parking stated first, then a spot count, an own site's pages on a tie; also on the drawer under who may rent), the quotes and source on hover, red or amber when the goal's parking rule fails or needs a check; nothing when no listing says anything about parking (a needs_check then shows as a '🅿 ?' fact).
- Detail drawer: photos and floor plans (each plan row shows one drawing as a thumbnail that opens full size: `plans.plan_image`, the own site's `floorplan_image` first, then an own-site `plan_drawings` label that names the plan when exactly one does, then any other source's; hosts that refuse hotlinking never; where the own site prices a plan, other sources show as 'also listed at $X on <source>'; a plan only other sources list at such a place is marked 'not on own site'; the goal chip says 'judged on the own website'), price and availability history, commute matrix (each time coloured by its source with the source named on hover: Google Maps blue, KNN range violet ('17–19', its neighbours' minutes and distances on hover), straight-line estimate grey with '~'; a legend line under the table; cards and the nearest-points list colour times the same way, and a failing fact keeps its red or amber), nearest points per set, all source URLs, raw JSON, per-attribute comments and 1 to 5 ratings (append to `annotations`), each fact's source and hand-check state, on-demand routing (`POST /api/trips/{key}`: Google by default; with no `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` that is an error, never a silent estimate, and the button offers 'Recompute (estimate)' explicitly; the outcome, 'N new trips, M Google calls' or the error, stays under the button and the drawer reloads with the new trips at once). Each listing's bd / ba cell has a **set ba** button: the baths typed in become a hand check on that listing (`POST /api/checks`, queue `adhoc`, verdict `wrong` with the value, or `correct` when the source already says it), which overrides the source and any borrowed or drawing value while the source's own value is unchanged.
- Photos (`gather_photos`: drawer strip, card cover, map hover): a photo with a stored copy is served from it as `media/<sha>.jpg` (relative, so it works under the base path; `GET /media/{sha}.jpg`, registered before the `/media` static mount that still serves seed files, `Cache-Control: immutable`), and photos sharing one stored file (near-duplicates the store mapped together), or whose files are within the same 6 bits, show once; photos not downloaded yet fall back to the source URL, hotlink-blocked ones last. Floor-plan drawings likewise come from the stored copy when there is one (`local_plan_images`), and a drawing on a hotlink-blocked host (Apartments.com) is picked by `plans.plan_image` only when we hold its copy.
- Status strip from `runs` and `checks`.
- Who may rent (drawer, under Goals): a select of the restriction rule's own values (`/api/goals` `equals_fields.restriction`: no limit, income, senior, veterans, student, employer, other) that appends the same annotation Verify writes (place / restriction, comment the value); the server refuses any other value for that annotation (400 naming the allowed ones), so a note typed in the drawer's notes block under 'restriction' cannot hold free text the rule would misread; it beats every source's reading in the goals' `restriction` rule. Beside it: your answer, or what the sources read (value, quote, source).
- Pipeline panel (header button, `GET /api/pipeline`, `aptfinder/pipeline.py`): every stage in order (configure, intake per source x area, identity, text reading (read / to read per text source, under the current schema), website finding, own-site scrapes, commute estimates, station -> target bike legs, Google routing, verify, filter) with a state (done / partial / todo / stale = older than 7 days / blocked), counts, last run, cost and the `nix run` command for the rest; intake counts every area of each source's kind, and the website, own-site and Google stages count places inside a station walk zone. Then the intake matrix (one table per area kind, a row per area), website states of named buildings and every own site with its last scrape, its last render outcome ('blocked (HTTP 403) on 11-24, will retry') and, for a discovered site, its last address check; the own-site stage counts render outcomes, names how many the precheck stopped as dead domains, and offers `render --retry-failed`. Computed on read from the logs and config; nothing stored. The drawer's Pipeline section (`place_status`, in `/api/places/{key}` as `pipeline`) gives each source's last sighting with a stale chip, the own-website state, each own site's last render and address check, whether Haiku read the text, and estimate vs Google commute. The KNN grid (Commute times: Google samples, KNN for the rest) says under each row and the bike+train and transit columns what one place costs (or, for transit, that it has no straight-line estimate; bike+train and transit are commute-target columns only) in Route Matrix elements, from config (`/api/knn/cells` gives each set's `k` and point count, `k_stations`, the matrix cap and free tier); the dry run shows places × routes per place against the uncached elements. Under the grid, an accuracy table per ticked cell over the chosen scope (`POST /api/knn/accuracy`, also in the dry run's answer): % of places shown with their own Google route, with a KNN range, estimate only; and KNN accuracy by leave-one-out over every place with its own Google route for the cell (n, median, p95 and p99 minutes outside the range padded by KNN_PAD_MIN, p99 only from 100 scored places (else '–'), % inside it; `filter.knn.loo_errors`, cached per cell like other dashboard results). At the top of the panel, a 'do I still need to sample' table (`POST /api/knn/status`, `sampling.commute_status`, cached per data version and scope): per ticked cell and a total row, for the current search results and for every place in a walk zone, the p90 / p99 expected error over the scope's places (own Google route 0; a KNN place spreads over the cell's leave-one-out errors of ranges on its side of 2 min wide, `filter.knn.loo_samples`; an estimate-only place over the cell's measured |estimate - Google|), the places still undecided because of the cell (`sampling.hinges`: no deciding listing passes, and one is needs_check on must-have commute rules only; the cell is an option of such a rule that is needs_check while none of the rule's options passes), and a verdict: done (0 undecided and p99 <= `DONE_P99_MIN` = 2 min), run cover or refine (the cheaper by uncached elements of those that pick a place), settled (no sample picks one) or unknown (nothing measured). The total row names, per goal, places waiting on a commute no ticked cell covers. The dry run shows undecided now -> after per cell, in total and per goal (after: if each routed place's time settles its cell; an upper bound). A '?' button by the title opens a 'How commute times work' dialog (native `<dialog>`, Esc or a click outside closes it) in plain language: what ticking does, cover / random % / refine, the status table, the scopes, how to read the dry run, billing and the typed confirm; its numbers come from the same response (`knn_k`, `radius_m`, `typed_over`, caps).
- Verify mode: conflicts ten to a screen (`GET /api/verify/batch?kind=conflict&skip=&n=&goal=&zone=`), filtered by 'Relevant to goal: [All conflicts | each goal]' (default: the header goal; the choice is remembered per browser) and 'only within the station walk zones' (`zone=walk`, `areas.in_walk_zone` of the place), with 'Showing X of N conflicts: Y hidden as irrelevant to <goal> (A no rule reads the conflicting fields; B every reading fails a rule; C every reading gives the same result); Z outside the walk zones' (`all_total`, `hidden` in the batch; the filters are cached per goal and zone; `/api/status` and the Verify button count every conflict). A goal keeps a conflict (`verify.goal_relevance`) when choosing a different candidate value for its disputed fields could change the goal's status (pass, needs_check, fail) of one of its listings. Only the hard rules that read a disputed field matter (`verify.rule_fields`: price rules read price, beds and price per bed; beds/baths rules beds and baths; dates rules availability; equals/max/min/known their field; commute and amenity rules none; none of them: hidden as irrelevant). The candidates of a field are the values the conflict's listings state, plus the text's value for a text conflict; each listing is judged on every combination of them (at most `verify.MAX_READINGS` = 64, else shown), those rules re-evaluated on its goal_inputs row with price per bed recomputed and its source's low-accuracy fields as in `evaluate_goal`, the other rules taken from its stored result. A listing whose status differs between readings keeps the conflict; otherwise it is hidden as settled when one rule fails on every reading, else as 'every reading gives the same result'. A too-cheap or stale listing (one reading, its alternative an unknown price or no offer) keeps the earlier test: shown when a price rule reads it and no rule fails on its reading; with no price rule the general test hides it. So both prices over a $/bed limit, a 2-bed under a 3-bed goal, a place over the goal's commute limits, or 3bd/2ba vs 3bd/2.5ba under '3bd with 2+ baths' are hidden; needs_check never hides. One block per conflict (place label linking to its drawer, a row per disagreeing field, a column per source with the value and correct / wrong / cannot tell), headed 'N conflicts: A to check (C priced too low per bedroom), P waiting for an own-site render'; a too-cheap listing carries the tag 'too cheap?' and a line with price, beds, $/bed and the reason, and only its price buttons count towards 'checked' when paging; pending ones carry the tag 'own-site render pending: will update this'. Previous / Next 10 (Next skips past the page minus the conflicts fully checked on it, since those leave the queue). Verdicts already given on a partly checked conflict show as pressed (`checked` per observation in the batch). Then the possible duplicate buildings, twenty to a screen (`GET /api/verify/duplicates?skip=&n=`; also linked from the conflict screen): each pair as A and B with label (opens the drawer), address, listing count, sources, distance and reasons, and three buttons: 'Merge A into B', 'Merge B into A' (the existing merge endpoint; the bigger place is the highlighted target) and 'Not the same building'; a checkbox per pair and a sticky bar ('select all on this page', 'N selected: Merge each smaller into bigger | Not the same building | Clear') answer the ticked pairs one by one through `POST /api/verify/duplicates/batch` with progress ('merging 3/12…'), each pair folding in place like a single answer (a failure unfolds with its error and stays ticked). Then the own-site list, twenty to a screen (`GET /api/verify/own_sites?skip=&n=`): each building with its reason, every URL with where it came from and its last outcome, an input that adds one more URL, and 'Dismiss: no plans online' (and 'No website' when none is known). Then the sample, one at a time (`/api/verify/next`). The Verify button counts actionable conflicts and pending ones separately (`/api/status` `queues.conflict`, `queues.conflict_pending`), then duplicates (`queues.duplicates`), own sites and the sample. Answers are in place: a value's button is pressed at once and saved behind it (a failed save un-presses it with the error next to it), a fully checked conflict dims and focus moves to the next open item, an answered duplicate pair or own-site building folds to a one-line stub; nothing refetches or redraws the screen except Next / Previous / Refresh / a filter / a jump to another list (which wait for answers in flight, keep the old screen dimmed until the new one arrives, and drop a late reply to an older request); closing Verify (x, Escape, a place link) only hides it, and the Verify button shows the same screen at the same scroll; the list and map reload once on close if anything was answered.
- Must not: be exposed beyond localhost without Cloudflare Access or a secret base path (it has write endpoints).
- Base path: `aptf-web --base-path /X` (default `APTF_BASE_PATH` from `.env`) serves the whole app under `/X/` only; every other path is 404 and FastAPI's `/docs` and `/openapi.json` are off. The frontend uses relative URLs so it works under any prefix.
- Tunnel: `nix run .#tunnel` runs `aptf-web` plus `cloudflared tunnel run` with `CLOUDFLARED_TOKEN` from `.env` (a remotely managed tunnel: the hostname and service `http://localhost:8765` are set in the Cloudflare dashboard).
- Redacted view: `aptf-web --redact` swaps the strings in `config/redact.toml` (`[replace]`, real -> shown, longest first) in every `/api/` response and refuses every non-GET request, for screenshots of real data (`scripts/screenshots.py`).
- Run: `aptf-web` (real data) or `aptf-web --seed` (demo data in `data/seed.db`, rebuilt with `aptf-db seed --fresh`), then open http://127.0.0.1:8765.
- API: `GET /api/meta|points|places|places/{key}|status|pipeline|verify/next?seed=&skip=|verify/batch?kind=conflict&skip=&n=&goal=&zone=|verify/duplicates?skip=&n=`; `POST /api/annotations|checks|links|trips/{key}|verify/duplicates/batch` (a list of `{a, b, into}`: `into` one of the pair merges the other into it, no `into` is 'not the same building'; in order, one transaction, each item's error returned while the rest commit) (an annotation of type `goal` is refused there); goals `GET|POST /api/goals` (list with the editor's rule metadata; create, id from the name unless given), `PUT|DELETE /api/goals/{id}` (an edit or a tombstone), `POST /api/goals/preview` (an unsaved goal judged on stored data, writes nothing); every goal write is validated (`filter.goals.validate_goal`: rule shapes, numeric fields, known points, sets and modes; a bad rule is a 400 naming it); the Pipeline panel's background sampling job `GET /api/compute/status` (also what `scripts/deploy.sh` waits on; after a restart that cut a dashboard job it reports `interrupted`) and its run log `GET /api/compute/log`, `POST /api/knn/accuracy`; the KNN cells `GET|POST /api/knn/cells` and Google sampling `POST /api/sample/dry_run|run` (the Pipeline panel's grid). Every write goes through `store.append_*`. A check's `stored_value` is read from the database, never taken from the client. A split appends a `user` link to a new place key `u_<hash>`.
- Speed: whole-database results (best trips with KNN, goal results, the places and listings views, the built place list and its JSON, Verify queues, the Pipeline overview, KNN status and accuracy) are computed once per data version in `create_app`'s `cached` and shared, never rebuilt per request; JSON is gzipped. A place in `/api/places` carries `minutes` for commute targets (and any point a goal's commute rule names) and `nearest` per set and mode; the drawer has the rest.
- Nothing blocks a request (`aptf-web` runs `create_app(background=True)`): each thread has its own SQLite connection (WAL: reads never wait for a write, a write waits only for another write); one background worker rebuilds the whole-database results and a request never computes one: it answers from the last snapshot and the response says `X-Updating: <results>` with `X-Snapshot-Age` while the worker rebuilds (stale-while-revalidate). Only before a result's first snapshot (just after a start, when the list, header counts, own-site and duplicate queues and Pipeline are queued first) does a request wait, for that result only. A watcher checks the data version every 3 s and rebuilds what a page read in the last 10 minutes, so an edit shows up unasked. Writes take short transactions and never wait for a rebuild; the global lock guards only check-then-write sequences and the job state. The page shows 'updating…' by the status strip and fetches the list (and an open drawer) again 5 s later until it answers fresh. A CSS spinner (both themes; it always holds its space, so nothing moves; never takes clicks) shows: by the goal editor's name and at the end of the count line while a goal preview or save is in flight (after 150 ms, so quick answers don't flicker; the list dims to 60% meanwhile; the latest edit wins), on the count line and the 'updating…' badge while the list is flagged `X-Updating`, and beside 'updating…' on the KNN status table (re-asked every 5 s until fresh) and the Pipeline panel (polled every 5 s while open; when fresh the spinner becomes a 'fresh results: show' button, not an unasked redraw that would drop a half-filled compute form). A goal edit (save, new goal, delete) is its own version, apart from the other edits: only the goal-dependent results go out of date (every name but `GOAL_FREE`: trips with KNN, `goal_inputs`, the places and listings views, `place_rows`, the Verify queues ...), and the save itself re-judges the goals on a second one-thread lane (`goal_refresh`) over the last snapshots of the goal-free inputs, taken as they are even when new scrapes made them out of date, then swaps in new goal results, list and its JSON (only goals whose definition changed are judged again; the list is `place_rows`, the goal-free part of each card, plus `with_goals`). The save answers once that is done (up to 20 s), so the page's next `/api/places` is fresh: 1.3-1.6 s on a copy of the box's data instead of a full rebuild. Each swapped snapshot keeps its predecessor's version with the new goal version, so what it was out of date on stays so and the worker rebuilds it as usual; a snapshot built against an older goal version never replaces a newer one (a rebuild that started before the save). The goal preview reads the same cached `goal_inputs`, which a goal edit leaves current. The start queues `goal_inputs` and `place_rows` after the other first snapshots, so a save soon after a restart (list loaded from disk) also takes this path; `goal_inputs` and `place_rows` are not written to disk. A user's own route, KNN cell change or sampling run marks every snapshot out of date (`invalidate`); the request that routed waits (holding no lock) for the rebuilt trips and goal results, so the drawer it reloads shows them. The worker's queue puts first a result a request waits for (no snapshot yet). The list, the header counts and queues, the Pipeline panel, the KNN status table and every whole-database input of the drawer are written to `data/cache/<db name>/` (pickles, at most every 5 minutes each) and loaded at start: with the same code and data they are current, otherwise served flagged while the worker rebuilds, so a restart or deploy answers the list and a drawer click at once. Without `background` (tests, scripts) a request computes what is out of date itself, as before. `tests/test_nonblocking.py`: during a 3 s rebuild the list and a write each answer within 300 ms.
- Service worker: map tiles and CDN assets cache-first; same-origin requests (page, API, media) network-first with the cache as the offline fallback, so a reload with the network off shows the last data.
- The filter panel is the selected goal's editor (one mechanism, no separate list filters). Its rows are the goal's rules: must-have rules and nice-to-haves, each a small form by type (field and ≤ < ≥ > value; beds/baths options; term, furnished, private bath, kind; dates; a commute row picks a commute target or the nearest point of a set, a mode and minutes, stored as `commute_any` or `near`, '+ or' adds another mode; an amenity preset from the list's amenity flags, words included, or a custom name and words; a field that must be known). Every change is a draft judged on the server (`POST /api/goals/preview`, debounced) and shown in the list at once, and it stays shown while the draft differs from the saved goal: a reload of the list (the 'updating…' re-poll, a compute run) re-applies the draft's last preview before it renders and judges the draft again quietly, never rendering the saved goal's results in between; 'Save' stores the goal for everyone, 'Save as new goal' stores a copy (also how a goal is duplicated), 'Revert' drops the draft, 'Delete goal' appends a tombstone, '+ new goal' starts an empty one (every place passes). The goal switch (a header `<select>`) lists 'All goals' plus every goal by its label and is rebuilt, keeping the selection, after a goal is created, renamed or deleted (a deleted selected goal falls back to All); All edits nothing and shows each place's best status. The redacted view shows the editor read-only. View controls, not goal rules, kept per browser in localStorage (`aptf.view`): search, sort, net, which statuses to show (pass, needs check, fail; an unknown value is needs check, so 'keep unknowns' is ticking needs check), inactive, map toggles. Which offer price a card shows follows the goal's `term` rule (short: stays, long: leases, none: both), not its id. Above the list, the count line gives the selected goal's totals by status over every place (All: each place's best status), each number in its status colour, e.g. '2,715 places · 207 pass · 71 check · 2,437 fail', then '· showing N' when the status ticks, search or the inactive box hides some. Number inputs are sized per kind in `ch` plus room for the spinner (beds and baths, minutes 3 digits; money 6; the compute confirm 6). While a draft differs from its saved goal, its preview is compared with the saved results place by place (client side, no second request): pass or needs check under the saved goal and fail or no listings under the draft is **cut** (blue, `--cut`), the reverse is **added** (pink, `--added`). Both get a dashed card border, a pin in their colour and a line naming the rule: 'cut by: <the draft's failing reason of an edited rule>' or 'added by (failed under the saved goal): <the saved result's failing reason of a rule the draft changed or dropped>' (the rule's index is each fail's `i`; with no such reason, the reasons the other side did not give). Cut and added places are always in the list and on the map, whatever the status ticks and the inactive box (search still applies), so the summary's counts are the cards shown. Under the status sort a cut place keeps its saved status's position. Under the count line (always in view; the editor scrolls): 'this edit cuts N places (show only these) · adds M (show only these)', each link filtering the list to that kind. A draft back to the saved goal, a save, a revert or a goal switch drops all of it at once.
- Net commute: each place's result for a goal carries `commute`, one `[minutes or null, estimate, trip]` per counted commute rule of the goal (`filter.net_rules`: the must-haves unless unticked, then the nice-to-haves ticked, by the rule's `in_net` and the editor's 'count in net commute' box on every commute and near row; `commute_off` holds the unticked must-haves' trips, hover only; one per roommate; `rules.commute_trips`, read by `commute_terms` and the rule: a `commute_any` term is its fastest option with a time, a `near` term the nearest point of the set by that mode, the same trips the rules check: Google when routed, else the estimate). The Net selector (view control) combines them as total, mean, median or spread (population standard deviation, low = everyone's commute is alike); the default is mean; the card shows e.g. 'net commute mean 8 min · spread ±7 (3 trips, est.)', and 'net commute (low to high)' sorts by it: complete places, then those with an unknown term (mean, median and spread of the known trips), then a total missing a time, then those with no time. Unknown terms are never 0: a total needs every counted trip's time, else the card says 'total needs check (n of m trips without a time)'; mean, median and spread say 'of k known (n unknown)'. All shows no net commute. A `net_commute` rule (agg, ≤ max) makes it a requirement; any unknown term makes it needs_check, an estimate near the limit too. Hovering the net commute (card fact, the drawer's `net_commute` reason) lists the trips behind the number from those same `trip` dicts, one line each: mode to destination, whole minutes, source (Google, KNN range a–b and the neighbours' median it uses, straight-line estimate), the goal rule it came from, a skipped term with why, and an unticked must-have as 'not counted: unticked'; the drawer also shows the list as an expandable 'net commute: n trips' line.
- Status ticks and the view controls also govern which pins are drawn; pass, needs check and fail are shown by default, with fails dimmed; 'no listings' has its own tick, off by default, and the count line ends '· N no listings'. A no-listings place shows with its tick whether or not it has an active listing (most have none, so the inactive tick does not hide them). Its goal chip is outlined in `--none`; the drawer's first goal line says why ('– no offers known', '– no price for a fitting plan (1 fits)', '– fitting plan unavailable ("…")'), then the fitting plan's own reasons.
- Cards show which attribute a place fails on: each goal result in `/api/places` carries `fails`, one `{s, i, f, v, lim?, pt?/set?, modes?}` per non-pass rule (`i`: the rule's index in the goal's must-haves) in `reasons` order (`filter.rules.fact`: the field or beds / baths / available / commute / near / amenity it judges and the deciding listing's value). The card colours that fact red (fail) or amber (needs_check): price spans (temp's price on the stay price, other goals' on the lease price; the deciding listing's own price follows in brackets when it differs from the card's minimum), beds, baths, the selected commute and each commute row of the selected goal (shown on the card with its time) with the same point and a mode the rule allows. An attribute the card does not show gets a small outlined chip ("term long", "private bath ?"). Only the selected goal; All shows every goal's. A commute row with no time is amber when its rule is undecided.
- Old saved list filters (annotation view / list / filters, a browser's `aptf.listFilters`) are no longer offered: goals come from `goals.toml` and the editor. The stored annotations stay (append-only) and old localStorage is ignored.

### eval (`aptf-eval`)
- Goal: the discovery benchmark for goal 2.
- Inputs: `eval/benchmark.csv` (copy of the user's sheet, snapshot of Nov 23, 2026), `places`.
- Outputs: `aptf-eval recall` prints found and missed per tier (Zillow listings, community sites, ruled out). A miss is reported with a reason: taken down since the snapshot (not counted) or not found (counted); the user decides when unclear. The sheet's "Walk to Caltrain" and "Drive to Meta HQ" columns also check the commute module (within about 2 minutes).

LLM steps (all through headless `claude -p`, the user's subscription, no API key; each reading's accuracy is measured through `checks`): Haiku reads listing text (`extract`; `--model sonnet` for a stronger re-read), own-site pages (`render`, `find-plans`, `portfolio`) and plan baths (`planread`); Sonnet searches for buildings' sites (`discover-sites`) and decides open conflicts (`aptf-verify reconcile`). The original plan had no LLM step; text reading became a core phase on 2026-11-24 (decision log).

## Stack

Built and run with Nix (`flake.nix`: the package, one `nix run .#<name>` app per CLI, the tunnel app, a dev shell with uv, cloudflared and Claude Code; `claude-code` is on the CLI's PATH for `extract`, `discover-sites`, `render`, `find-plans`, `planread` and `reconcile`, the one unfree package allowed); `apify-client` and its `impit` dependency come from the wheels `uv.lock` pins. Tests run with `uv run pytest` in the dev shell.

Python 3.12 (pinned through `uv`; the system Python is 3.10), `apify-client`, FastAPI plus uvicorn, `sqlite3` from the standard library, `pydantic` for row and config shapes, `httpx` for Google Routes, `pytest`. Front end: plain JavaScript and Leaflet from a CDN, no build step. Secrets in a gitignored `.env`. A spend cap per run, with estimated cost logged in `runs`.

## Order of work

1. Repo setup and this document.
2. Skeleton, log schema with triggers, derived views, config loaders, CLI entry points.
3. Point set files: commute targets, Caltrain stations (user confirms), grocery sets from OpenStreetMap.
4. Commute module with tests, then a hand check against Google Maps and the benchmark columns.
5. Identity and filter, with `needs_check`.
6. Dashboard, including verify mode, on seeded example observations (with a deliberate cross-source conflict).
7. Stop and show the user the dashboard.
8. Intake: Airbnb, Furnished Finder and Blueground first, then lease aggregators, direct enrichment, portfolio discovery. User works a sample queue per new source. Run `aptf-eval recall`.
