### Simplification TODOs (suggested 2026-11-24, not done; the user prefers simpler design)

1. **Shrink `discover-sites`.** It keeps its own append-only file (`config/discovered_sites.toml`: found, miss, check), `load_sites()` merges it with the hand list, and `direct.parse` re-checks addresses for discovered sites. Simpler: search, fetch and check the address once, then print proposed `[[site]]` lines for the user to paste into `config/direct_sites.toml`. One site list, every entry approved by the user; removes the extra file, the merge and most of `intake/discover.py`.
2. **One price per card.** Third-party stays are now their own places, so a building's minimum is its lease minimum (its own furnished option always costs more) and a stay place's minimum is its stay price. Drop `min_lease_month`, `min_lease_per_bed`, `min_stay_month` from the `places` view and `priceFacts` / goal-dependent price sorting from `app.js`.
3. **Route stay places like any place.** Remove `filter._Trips` (stay places reading the building's trips), the candidate merge in `trips.py` and the stay exclusion in `compute_all`. Cost: a few extra Google calls inside the free tier (a stay place's pin usually sits slightly off its building's, so most are real calls, not cache hits).
4. ~~**Remove `bike_bus_bike`** until the Google bus stop list exists~~ (moot since 2026-11-25: the private config has a stop list).
5. **Remove legacy-run coverage handling** (`LEGACY_CITY_COVERS`, `LEGACY_PARTIAL`, legacy box overlap in `intake/base.coverage`): either re-run the Mountain View city searches through the area ledger (about $1 to 2) or mark those areas done by hand once.
6. **Optional: drop the service worker** (offline cache of tiles and pages): little use on this machine, and it can serve stale pages.

Kept on purpose: the verify queue, the area ledger, the photo filters, the three offer kinds in `plans.plan_key`.

## Decision log

Each entry: date, decision, why, what it replaced.

- **2026-11-25: The goal switch is a dropdown.** User: "have this be a dropdown". Goals are user-editable now, so the header's segmented buttons (All | temp | lease | uh, by id) would grow without bound; the switch is a `<select>` with 'All goals' plus each goal by its label. Changing it does what a button click did (`selectGoal`: the goal, the stay sort default, the list, the open drawer, the editor); declining the 'drop unsaved changes' prompt puts the select back. Browser check on a copy of live, fail unticked: All 2,611 of 2,699 places, temp 364, lease 296, uh 2,611 (no rules). Replaced: one button per goal, labelled by id.
- **2026-11-25: Zillow amenities are the card's feature words only; required monthly fees are stored.** User: "use the description" (Zillow). The search card's `listCardRecommendation.flexFieldRecommendations` were all stored as amenities, and across the three raw files (905 items) they are mostly badges: time on Zillow ('227 days ago', 503), 'Apply instantly' (267), 'Waitlisted' (99), '3D Tour' (48), 'N available units' (32), offers ('$1,000 Off', '1 Month Free', 12), 'Income restricted' (11), 'Senior housing' (1); only `homeInsight` (29: 'Swimming pool', 'Designer finishes', 'Three resort-style swimming pools') names features. Now amenities = `homeInsight` strings plus the card's `zovInsight` ('Clubhouse', 'Fitness center', 'Pet washing station'), allowlisted by content type so a new badge never counts; a card with badges only has no amenity list (unknown). Badges are not kept (Waitlisted, Income restricted and Senior housing exist in raw if wanted later). The required monthly fees (`rental.totalRequiredMonthlyMinFee`/`MaxFee`) move from the extract raw allowlist into `extra.required_monthly_fees` ('$40', '$50 to $75') and count as text to read, because 17 listings had a monthly fee reading and no other text once the badges are gone; Zillow is no longer a raw-text source for extract. Rehearsed on a copy of live (reparse of runs 4, 21, 156 in that order): active Zillow 1,123 unchanged, goal statuses unchanged (temp 8/375/7,935, lease 389/121/7,808 pass/check/fail), amenity lists 746 (junk) -> 84 (real), every Zillow reading lapses (its text changed); a re-read is 112 listings, 72 texts, 8 Haiku calls (about $0.01). Replaced: every card badge stored as an amenity; fees read from the raw item.
- **2026-11-25: A price of 0 is unknown (NULL), never $0; reparse keeps the area tag.** 18 places showed $0/mo and $0/bed and sorted first by price (Coronado, Overlook at Walnut Creek, Levare, 201 Marshall, ...). Cause: Craigslist's actor returns `priceUsd: 0` for posts that state no price (34 active listings, mostly property-manager ads such as Greystar's "Call Now" posts, whose text says "Studios starting at $2499" at most), and FurnishedFinder returned `"$0.00/month"` for Sonesta ES Suites Sunnyvale; `money()` kept the 0, and the text reading copied it forward. Not Haiku, and no listing had a price between $0 and $300. Now `Parsed` turns any price of 0 or less into NULL for every parser (`money()` itself is unchanged, since it also reads beds, where 0 is a studio); the views already take MIN over non-null prices, the dashboard shows "?" and sorts unknown last, and goals report `needs_check` (price unknown). Also, `reparse` dropped `extra.area`, so reparsed rows (e.g. run 185's 42) could never be retired by a later complete area run; it now tags each row as `run_source` does. Repair: free reparses of the raw files behind the affected listings plus every file that is now the current one for a listing in them (1 FurnishedFinder, 10 Craigslist), in an order that keeps each listing's current file last (the parallel runs 171 to 178 make a strict order impossible; 2 listings move to a same-minute file of another area). Rehearsed on a copy: 35 prices 0 -> NULL, $0 places 18 -> 0, active counts unchanged, 42 rows regain their area, and the reparse applied cached text readings to 385 Craigslist listings that had none (unread 446 -> 61), which merges a few duplicate places (2,720 -> 2,711; e.g. 2333 Eastridge on Zillow and Craigslist). Replaced: a source's 0 stored as a $0 rent; untagged reparse rows.

- **2026-11-23: Local SQLite plus files, no Google Sheet.** Everything runs on the user's PC; a sheet adds sync and quota problems. Replaced: the Sheet idea and `handoff.md`'s plain SQLite-with-upserts model.
- **2026-11-23: Logs are the truth, entities are derived views.** Append-only logs keep every observation and decision with its time and author; views mean no second copy to drift. Replaced: mutable `listings` table with `price_history` side table.
- **2026-11-23: Gather wide, filter late; unknown never rejects; filters report, not drop.** Scraping is the expensive step, so it must not depend on thresholds that change. Replaced: SQL hard filters before ranking.
- **2026-11-23: Two goals (`temp`, `lease`) over one candidate list.** The search split into a furnished stay for one person and a group lease. Replaced: single criteria file.
- **2026-11-23: Lease size rule is 3bd with 2+ baths or 4bd with 3+ baths.** Confirmed with the user; matches the benchmark, which counts 3bd/2.5ba. Replaced: "4bd with 2.5+ baths".
- **2026-11-23: One point-set concept** (CSV per set) for commute targets, stations, groceries and bus stops; proximity is a query over `trips`. Replaced: hardcoded station list with a walking threshold.
- **2026-11-23: Commute is an independent module owning its leg cache.** Lets composed modes share legs (station-to-office legs fetched once) and keeps it testable alone.
- **2026-11-23: Google Routes API for routing, behind `LegSource`; straight-line estimate as the free fallback.** Paid routing is lazy; the estimate gives every place a labelled rough number.
- **2026-11-23: Satellite tiles from Esri, cached by a service worker; no Google tiles.** Google's terms forbid caching.
- **2026-11-23: One full observation row per listing per run.** User's choice; makes liveness ("still listed") a plain fact for recall and the status strip. `content_hash` covers normalized parsed fields plus query dates, not raw payloads. Replaced: write only on content change.
- **2026-11-23: All listing sources go through Apify, property websites included.** User's choice; nothing scrapes a listing site from this machine. Replaced: `handoff.md`'s ban on direct Zillow/Apartments.com/Realtor.com only.
- **2026-11-23: `runs` rows may be closed out once.** A run must be visible while in progress and survive a crash; a single UPDATE while `finished_at IS NULL` is allowed, enforced by trigger. Replaced: blanket no-UPDATE on every log table.
- **2026-11-23: A `wrong` check overrides only while the source still shows the checked value.** Otherwise a stale correction would mask a real price change.
- **2026-11-23: User links beat rule links regardless of age; identity appends only on change.** Re-running identity must never undo a manual merge or split, and the log should not grow on every rerun.
- **2026-11-23: Goal outcomes computed at read time, not stored.** Thresholds live in TOML, which a SQL view cannot read; computing on read keeps "no second copy". Replaced: `goal_matches` as a view.
- **2026-11-23: Seed data in `data/seed.db`.** Demo observations must never mix with real ones.
- **2026-11-23: No LLM step for now.** Deterministic parsing first; a model is proposed only if free-text parsing demands it, measured through `checks`. Replaced: LLM extraction and ranking from `handoff.md`.
- **2026-11-23: Manual verification built in from the first run** (sample and conflict queues, accuracy with intervals). Confidence in scraped data is measured, not assumed.
- **2026-11-23: `handoff.md` deleted, replaced by this file.** Its candidate actor table and the Airbnb 28-day tip were moved into the intake section as untested leads.
- **2026-11-23: Python 3.12 via `uv`, FastAPI, plain JS plus Leaflet.** One language for every component; no front-end build step.
- **2026-11-23: Caltrain set is 8 stations, Redwood City to Lawrence.** User's choice. Atherton closed 2020; Stanford is event-only and left out; Lawrence added because it is the nearest station for east Sunnyvale (the benchmark uses it). Ride times and tiers from the Caltrain GTFS feed of 13-Nov-2026 (valid to 2027-01-31; recheck after). Limited trains stop everywhere in this segment at Local timings, so the train table has two patterns: local and express. Replaced: "7 stations, Redwood City to Sunnyvale".
- **2026-11-23: Rengstorff industrial complex target at 1000 N Rengstorff Ave, Mountain View, marked unverified.** User's choice; only aggregator sites give the street address.
- **2026-11-23: Commute glue lives in `aptfinder/trips.py`; `aptf-commute` points there.** Keeps the commute package free of project imports while still giving it a CLI. Paid routing gets per-mode straight-line limits (`max_km`) and a call cap, because routing every place to every target by every mode costs about 20 calls per place.
- **2026-11-23: Benchmark geocoded once with Nominatim into `eval/benchmark_geocoded.csv` (committed).** Free, and the commute check and recall fallback need coordinates. First commute check with the free estimate: walk to Caltrain mean error 1.7 min (13 of 22 within 2 min), drive to Meta mean error 9.6 min (straight line ignores freeways). Google check pending: the supplied key returned PERMISSION_DENIED for the Routes API.
- **2026-11-23: Estimate trips decide a commute rule only far from the threshold.** A straight-line estimate passes at or under 0.67 times the limit, fails at or over 1.5 times, and is `needs_check` in between; Google trips are preferred whenever present. A rough number must not reject a place that real routing might pass.
- **2026-11-23: Place outcome = best listing outcome (pass, then needs_check, then fail; ties broken by nice-to-have count).** A building with one qualifying unit qualifies.
- **2026-11-23: User ratings of an amenity attribute override the scraped amenity list** (rating 3 or more counts as having it). The user's direct knowledge beats the listing text.
- **2026-11-23: Dashboard checked in headless Chrome (Playwright, system Chrome) instead of the DevTools MCP**, which failed to attach. Verified: satellite tiles load, goal switching, drawer, rating and comment append, verify mode shows the seeded conflict first, reload with the network off renders all places and cached tiles, no horizontal scroll at 390 px, no console errors.
- **2026-11-23: Check state keyed by listing and field, not by observation id (`current_checks` view).** With one observation per run, keying on the observation id would have reset every check, conflict resolution and ✓ mark on the next run and double counted re-checks. Replaced: queues and drawer marks keyed on the latest `obs_id`.
- **2026-11-23: Estimate trips are excluded from the sample queue.** They are labelled guesses; hand-checking them measures nothing (the benchmark already shows their drive error).
- **2026-11-24: Temp goal runs Dec 1, 2026 to Jan 1, 2027, with a soft budget.** User: "about $4k, cheaper is better". Hard rule `price_month <= 4000`, temp view sorts by price. Replaced: Dec 1 to Jan 1, no budget.
- **2026-11-24: Staged rollout by city** (Mountain View, then plus Menlo Park, then the rest), with `config/sources.toml` spend cap $1 per run on top of Apify's own `maxTotalChargeUsd`. The Apify account is on the free plan ($5 a month). Staged runs are `scope = partial` and never mark listings inactive; only a whole-area run does.
- **2026-11-24: Cost is computed from charged events times the actor's event prices.** Apify's `usageTotalUsd` reported $0.0001 for a run that charged 18 listing events at $0.004.
- **2026-11-24: Listing photos are not mirrored to `data/media/` for now.** Downloading from listing sites' image servers would be a direct fetch from this machine; image URLs are kept in `data.extra` and the browser loads them. Replaced: mirroring every photo.
- **2026-11-24: Airbnb via `tri_angle/airbnb-scraper`** ($0.004 per listing on the free tier). Trial of 20 and first run of 240 on Mountain View: every item parsed, with coordinates, a monthly price, room type and bath summary. Finding: a city query returns a wide region (65 of 240 in Mountain View) and hit the cap, so city queries are a poor fit for Airbnb.
- **2026-11-24: Furnished Finder via `shahidirfan/furnished-finder-scraper`** ($0.001 per listing). Trial of 20 and Mountain View run of 77: every item parsed, with coordinates, monthly rent, beds, baths and availability date. City pages are properly scoped. Rooms do not state whether the bath is private, so `private_bath` stays unknown for them (`needs_check`).
- **2026-11-24: Blueground actor `moving_beacon-owner1/blueground-scraper` does not work.** Listing mode returns only title and URL; detail mode reports "No embedded listings found. Blueground may have changed its Next.js data". Not used; raised with the user.
- **2026-11-24: Airbnb searches one map box around downtown Palo Alto** (`box` in `config/sources.toml`). User's choice, because city queries spill over the South Bay. The search URL must carry a place slug (`/s/Palo-Alto--CA/homes?...`); a bare `/s/homes` returned 0 items. The box held about 130 available stays for the dates; past that Airbnb widened the search (128 of 240 inside), so `max_items` is 150.
- **2026-11-24: Lease sources.** Zillow via `clearpath/zillow-zip-code-search-scraper` (same output as the bulk actor at half the price per result); buildings become one listing per bedroom count with baths unknown. Apartments.com via `memo23/apify-apartments-cheerio`: `epctex/apartments-scraper-api` caps free-plan users at 10 items, and `fatihtahta/apartments-com-scraper` refuses any run capped under $1. memo23 needs 1 GB of memory (256 MB ran out after 16 of 43 pages); single units, condos and houses come back without floor plans and are parsed from `rentals`. Craigslist via `solidcode/craigslist-scraper` by postal code and radius; no street address, so identified by coordinates.
- **2026-11-24: Some Blueground inventory arrives through Apartments.com** (furnished units with IDs like `#SFO279`). The generic Apify scrapers (`apify/cheerio-scraper`) and `tri_angle/real-estate-aggregator` need full account permissions, which only the user can grant in the Apify console.
- **2026-11-24: `aptf-intake reparse <source> <raw file>`** re-ingests a saved raw file with the current parser as a new targeted run, so parser fixes never require paying for a scrape again.
- **2026-11-24: Blueground dropped as a direct source.** Its Bay Area market page lists only San Francisco neighborhoods, and the embedded listing data has no Peninsula listings, so there is nothing to collect in the search area. Its few Peninsula units arrive through Apartments.com.
- **2026-11-24: Working rule: name blockers, don't detour** (`AGENTS.md`). User's instruction after an exploratory Blueground scraping detour.
- **2026-11-24: Step 4 hand check passed with Google Routes.** Against the benchmark's Google Maps columns: drive to Meta HQ mean error 1.0 min (17 of 19 within 2 min); walk to nearest Caltrain mean error 1.9 min (17 of 22). Outliers: Shadowbrook (+8.8: the check routes only to the straight-line-nearest station; trip computation uses k = 2), Spruce (+6.1), Park Place (+3.6), The Shadows (-3.2); likely geocode or station-point placement, not yet confirmed. The earlier 403 was the Routes API not being enabled on the project, then a few minutes of propagation.
- **2026-11-24: Derived views rewritten for speed: joins and window functions, no per-row subqueries.** `listings_base` looked up each of 14 checkable fields' overrides with its own correlated subquery, each re-deriving `latest_obs` from all observations: 73 s for 1,946 listings, so the dashboard showed nothing. Overrides are now aggregated once per listing into a JSON object (`json_group_object`) and joined; `places` picks its address, city, name and operator with `FIRST_VALUE` windows over one materialized pass of `listings`. All views build in under 0.2 s; results unchanged (tests pass). Still views, not tables.
- **2026-11-24: Intake runs on areas, with a coverage ledger.** User: start from areas reachable from Caltrain, smallest efficient units, track what's left. `scripts/plan_areas.py` writes `config/areas.toml`: each station's 25-minute walk zone (1.54 km), reverse-geocoded on a 500 m grid into ZIP codes (Zillow, Craigslist), places (Furnished Finder, Apartments.com) and a bounding box per station (Airbnb), grouped into chunks worked in order: Mountain View + San Antonio, Palo Alto + California Ave + Menlo Park, Redwood City, Sunnyvale + Lawrence. A run batches its areas into one actor run (start fee paid once) and records the area ids in `runs.params`; `aptf-intake coverage` derives done and TODO per chunk, area and source from `runs`, crediting this morning's whole-city and box queries. No new table. Some generated ZIPs look like PO boxes or odd OSM tags (94035, 94039, 94088, 94807, 94036) and are expected to return nothing. Replaced: city-by-city runs.
- **2026-11-24: SQLite connections wait up to 60 s for a lock** instead of failing, so the dashboard and an intake run can share the database.
- **2026-11-24: Identity rule `geo_near_address`.** Craigslist has no street address, so its complexes (Novo 61 posts, Highland Gardens 54, Ladera 50) formed separate places from the same buildings on Apartments.com and Zillow. A coordinate-only listing now joins the nearest address-keyed building within 80 m. Not single houses (closer than 80 m apart) and not Airbnb (offset pins). Real data: multi-source places 46 to 59.
- **2026-11-24: Real-data checks.** Benchmark recall in scraped areas: Zillow 7/7, communities 4/4 (all Mountain View rows); every miss is in a ZIP not scraped yet, which `aptf-eval recall` now reports separately. Airbnb: 113 listings seen in both runs, none moved coordinates or changed place.
- **2026-11-24: Each run records a state per area, and only complete areas count as covered.** `runs.counts.areas.<id> = {items, state}` with state `complete` (finished under its cap), `partial` (cap hit, or the actor failed after returning items), `empty` (finished with nothing) or `not_reached`. The ledger shows PARTIAL and the next `run` picks those areas up again; legacy Craigslist r5 (hit its 300 cap) is partial. Sources with a negligible start fee (Craigslist, Furnished Finder, Airbnb) run one actor run per area so attribution is exact; Zillow ($0.05 start) and Apartments.com ($0.02) batch their ZIPs and attribute items by ZIP, with the item cap scaled by the number of areas. Each observation carries its area in `data.extra.area`. Replaced: one cap per batch, every area credited.
- **2026-11-24: Liveness per area.** A listing is inactive when a newer run of its source completed its area without it (or a whole-area run missed it). Partial and failed reruns change nothing. Replaced: whole-area runs only, under which nothing could ever go inactive once intake moved to areas.
- **2026-11-24: Apify budget guard.** Before every actor run, intake reads the account's remaining monthly credit and caps the run at (credit left minus `spend.reserve_usd`, $0.30), stopping with areas marked `not_reached` instead of running the account dry.
- **2026-11-24: Google Routes metering.** New append-only log `api_calls` (ts, provider, mode, ok) in the main database; every paid call from any caller (CLI, dashboard, eval) goes through `trips.MeteredSource`, which checks the run cap and the monthly caps in `config/google.toml` before calling and logs the call after. Caps: 5,000 calls a month in total (half the Essentials free tier of 10,000 at $5 per 1,000, read from Google's SKU page 2026-11-24) and 900 bicycle calls, because Enterprise ($15 per 1,000, 1,000 free) covers "two-wheeled vehicle routing" and the page does not say whether BICYCLE counts. The 43 calls of the step-4 hand check were backfilled. Usage shows in the status strip and `aptf-commute usage`.
- **2026-11-24: Detail drawer leads with photos and floor plans; units are collapsed below.** User: for a building like Avalon, floor plans matter more than a list of units, for every apartment. The server groups a place's active listings by beds, baths and plan name across sources (`group_plans`); Zillow's beds-only building summaries join the matching plan. Columns: plan, bd/ba, sqft and price ranges, $/bed, units available, earliest date, sources, plan image. Units and listings sit in a collapsed table with hand-check marks.
- **2026-11-24: Photos are linked, not mirrored; Apartments.com photos are blocked.** Gallery = image URLs from every listing (Apartments.com gallery up to 16 and plan images, Craigslist up to 8, one each from Zillow, Furnished Finder, Airbnb). Chrome refuses every images1.apartments.com URL (`ERR_BLOCKED_BY_ORB`: the CDN answers hotlinked requests with a non-image), so those go last and broken images are removed. Craigslist photos load. Blocker: showing Apartments.com photos needs mirroring through Apify into `data/media/`.
- **2026-11-24: TODO areas in the dashboard.** Each place is tagged with its chunk (first chunk with a station within the 1.54 km walk radius; `aptfinder/areas.py`, computed on read). Places outside `active` chunks get a TODO chip, are dimmed, and can be hidden; the map shows every station's walk circle (solid when active, dashed when TODO). Real data: 271 of 674 places in the active Mountain View chunk.
- **2026-11-24: A reparse supersedes listings it no longer produces.** A listing whose latest observation came from a raw file that was later reparsed without producing it is inactive (old-parser artefacts like Landsby's "sqft 1" plan).
- **2026-11-24: Paid routing only for what the goals read, for places that can still pass.** `aptf-commute compute --candidates`: active-chunk places where some listing passes or needs a check on every non-commute rule of some goal, routed only for those goals' commute rules (temp: bike/walk/transit to downtown Palo Alto; lease: walk to the 2 nearest stations). Groceries and bus stops are estimate-only (`paid = false`). `--dry-run` counts the uncached calls first. Mountain View: 176 places, 372 calls (the full table would have been 3,147). The full table per place stays one click away (Route with Google).
- **2026-11-24: Apartments.com runs per city, not per ZIP.** The `{city}-ca-{zip}` URL returned "No listings were found" (one $0.001 test on 94306); city pages are proven. Buildings are attributed to their area by `listingCity`. An actor's no-results marker makes the area `empty`; a run where every item is unparseable makes it `failed` and is never credited (including run 17, which had been recorded as complete).
- **2026-11-24: Direct enrichment of community websites works through Apify's generic `cheerio-scraper`** (one page per site, about $0.0001), sites listed in `config/direct_sites.toml` (each an intake area of kind `site`), parsers per platform in `intake/direct.py`, source `direct`. Tested on the benchmark's Mountain View communities: **Avalon** (Fusion page state) gives every available unit, and reproduces the sheet exactly: four 3BR C1 units, 1,056 sqft, $5,320 to $5,449, available Nov 25, Dec 1, Dec 29, Dec 30. **The Dean** and **Park Place** (Prometheus, `__NEXT_DATA__`) give plan names with sqft, beds and baths from the plan drawing file names, plan drawings and a photo gallery, but no prices: The Dean Plan 3A 1,196 sqft matches the sheet. **Landsby** (a floor plan app's JSON) gives 77 plans with sizes but no rent or availability; 3A.1 and 3A.2 at 1,248 and 1,244 sqft match the sheet. Blockers: Prometheus and Landsby prices and availability load with JavaScript after the page (would need a browser-rendering actor); the aggregators carry no property-website URL, so sites are listed by hand. All four land on the existing aggregator places through their street address (Landsby's page has none, so `address` in the config is the fallback).
- **2026-11-24: Floor plans group by beds, baths and size (to 10 sqft) across sources**, with the community site's plan name preferred. Avalon's C1 now merges Apartments.com, Zillow and the direct units.
- **2026-11-24: The Apartments.com parser also reads the earlier epctex actor's format**, so its raw file (run 3) could be reparsed with the sqft fix. Runs 3 and 6 were reparsed, superseding old-parser rows.
- **2026-11-24: Mountain View chunk intake (night run).** Zillow 94306/94022/94303 complete ($0.14, 139 new listings); Apartments.com Los Altos + Palo Alto complete ($0.10, 152 new units/plans); Furnished Finder Los Altos + Palo Alto complete ($0.02, 97 new); Craigslist 94022 and 94303 complete, 94306 partial ($0.21, 135 new). Bug found and fixed: `--max-items` reached Apify's run cap but not the actor's own per-search limit.
- **2026-11-24: Blocker: Airbnb map-box search only works for boxes around the place named in the URL.** The Mountain View and San Antonio station boxes returned 1 and 8 of 80 results inside the box and nothing new ($0.64, both areas left PARTIAL). The URL needs a place slug and the only one used is Palo-Alto--CA; the Palo Alto box worked because it surrounds Palo Alto. Likely fix (needs a small trial): use the box's own city in the slug. (Tried, see below.) Lesson: trial a new query shape on a few items before a full run.
- **2026-11-24: Furnished detection fixed; Blueground units are furnished short-term stays.** `"furnished" in description` matched "unfurnished" (55 Apartments.com listings were wrongly furnished). Now: unfurnished means False, furnished means True, both (Blueground's "looking for an unfurnished lease? ... upgrade to furnished living") means unknown. Units whose text carries Blueground's id pattern `(ID #SFO1141)` or name are operator Blueground, furnished, and short-term when the minimum lease is 3 months or less: 61 such units in the scraped areas (several inside Avalon Mountain View), all now temp candidates. Apartments.com raw files reparsed (free).
- **2026-11-24: One plan identity for filter and dashboard (`aptfinder/plans.py`).** Same place + beds + baths + size (to 10 sqft) + kind of offer (lease or furnished stay). In the drawer, when the community's own site prices a plan, its units, prices and dates are the row ("own site", other sources "also on"), and furnished stays by an operator inside the building (Blueground at Avalon) are separate rows. In the filter, a listing without a price takes the same plan's lowest price from another source (`price_from` named in the reason), so a price-less community-site plan no longer holds a place at needs-check while a priced copy fails. Avalon C1 now reads 4 units, $5,320 to $5,449, earliest Nov 25, exactly the benchmark; The Dean's lease result went from needs-check to fail ($8,570 for 3 bedrooms).
- **2026-11-24: Airbnb listings are attributed to a station box by their coordinates** (`area_of`), not by the search that returned them: box searches spill outside the box, and an untagged listing can never be retired by a later complete run of a box it isn't in. Tonight's station-box runs were reparsed to drop their tags. Junk areas removed from `config/areas.toml` (PO-box or mistagged ZIPs 94035, 94039, 94088, 94807; OSM neighbourhood names Rains Houses, Menlo Oaks).
- **2026-11-23: Rengstorff industrial complex replaced by "Rengstorff industrial complex", 1000 N Rengstorff Ave, Mountain View.** User's choice: a stand-in for an office that may go in the industrial area south of the Googleplex. Replaced: the Rengstorff industrial complex target.
- **2026-11-24: Temp goal requires `term = short`.** User's choice: the stay is about a month, so an ordinary 12-month lease does not fit even when furnished. 180 of the temp needs-check places were plain leases with unknown furnishing; in the Mountain View chunk temp went from 4 pass / 26 needs-check to 4 / 3. Replaced: temp rules that did not look at lease term.
- **2026-11-24: Building photos come from the community's own website (`photos` platform), not mirrored Apartments.com images.** User's choice. The direct page function now also returns every image candidate on the page (og:image, `img`/`source` with the largest `srcset` entry, CSS background images, with alt, class and size); `parse` keeps photos and drops logos, icons, social and stock images, videos, SVG/GIF, and anything under 300 px (size attributes, Cloudinary `w_`/`h_` transforms, `250x25` in a file name), one copy per file name; a site's optional `photo_match` regex keeps only that community's images on operator pages (UDR mixes in company-wide ones). `srcset` is split on comma plus space because CDN URLs carry commas. Each such site yields one row of `kind = site_info` (photos and link, on the site's address) that is not an offer: the filter, plan grouping, verify queue and place listing counts skip it (checked: goal counts unchanged). The browser loads images from the site's CDN; nothing is downloaded on this machine. Sites: Sevens, The Village Residences, Verve, Birch Creek, Fayette Arms (URLs from the old epctex Apartments.com items, the only aggregator output that carried them); 4 to 10 photos each, all load in Chrome; $0.0025 for the five. The drawer gallery now puts the community's own photos first. Replaced: mirroring Apartments.com photos through Apify.
- **2026-11-24: Airbnb box searches take a per-box place slug (`slug` in `config/areas.toml`, default Palo-Alto--CA).** Trial on the Mountain View box with Mountain-View--CA: 13 of 20 results inside the box (the Palo Alto slug gave 1 of 80), $0.07. The full run then returned 137 items of which only 33 were inside the box (24%), 25 new listings, $0.55, state complete (under the 150 cap). So the slug helps but does not hold results to the box; the rest are attributed by coordinates to other boxes or nowhere. Mountain View box listings change no temp result: they fail the commute rule to downtown Palo Alto. The San Antonio box is still partial. Replaced: one hardcoded Palo-Alto--CA slug.
- **2026-11-24: Airbnb box runs never count as complete.** Airbnb's map search returns a ranked subset: run 61 on the Mountain View box returned 33 of the 51 listings known inside it (and 104 outside). A run of a ranked source (`RANKED_SOURCES` in `intake/base.py`) marks its areas partial, and absence from it never retires a listing (liveness view). No listing had been retired by run 61. Airbnb boxes stay on the "left" list; rerunning them refreshes, it does not finish. Replaced: complete when under the item cap.
- **2026-11-24: Community websites are found by a local headless Claude Code that may only web-search (`aptf-intake discover-sites`).** User's choice over Apify's Google Search actor: picking the right site from results needs judgement. Amends "No LLM step for now" for this one step. `claude -p` runs with WebSearch as its only tool (search is not a listing fetch; the page itself is still fetched only through Apify, rule 6), in a temp directory, without the Apify and Google keys in its environment, and trimmed (`--strict-mcp-config --setting-sources "" --disable-slash-commands --no-session-persistence --system-prompt ...`): about $0.024 per building instead of $0.25 (trials). A call counts only with a structured answer, no permission denials and at least one search; anything else stops the command as a blocker and records nothing (an untrimmed trial reported success while its search was denied and guessed a URL). URLs lose query strings and tour/apply/contact paths (rule 7); aggregator and social hosts are refused. Results are appended to `config/discovered_sites.toml` (`[[found]]`, `[[miss]]` so reruns skip, `[[check]]`); `load_sites()` merges them with the hand list (hand wins). Each found page is fetched through Apify and accepted only if it shows the building's street number and street name (`direct.address_on_page`), checked again in `direct.parse` for every discovered site; a failure is a `[[check]] ok = false` and the site is dropped. Known operator hosts get their full parser (avaloncommunities.com -> `avalon`), so those rows are real offers. Each Claude call is a `runs` row (component `discover`) with its cost. The page function now also returns the page text and the request's site tag (matching survives redirects). Replaced: hand-listing every site.
- **2026-11-24: Drawer and cards show only the selected goal; website under the name; photo source overlay; raw data inside the units table; demo database deleted.** User's requests. With a goal selected (temp or lease), cards and the drawer show only that goal's result and reasons (All shows both); switching goals re-renders an open drawer. The building's website (community-site rows first, else a website an aggregator gave) sits under the name. Every photo carries a small bottom-right label with the host of the page it came from, linking to that page (`page`, `site` in `/api/places/{key}` photos). The separate "Raw data" section (first 12 listings' JSON) is gone: each row of "Units & listings" has a `raw` button that opens that listing's parsed fields and source payload under the row, so one table covers every listing. `data/seed.db` and the demo server on port 8765 were removed (`aptf-web --seed` still rebuilds it for tests or demos). Replaced: both goals everywhere, photos unlabelled, raw data as a separate list.
- **2026-11-24: Leases, furnished leases and furnished stays are three separate offers.** User: split the furnished rentals at Avalon Mountain View from its plain apartments. Findings: (1) Avalon's own page gives every unit two prices, `startingAtPricesUnfurnished` and `startingAtPricesFurnished` (its "Furnished+" program, `furnishStatus = OnDemand`; e.g. unit 130: $3,804 vs $5,054 on 15 months); the furnished price is now its own row per unit (`<site>:uf:<unit>`, furnished, long term). - **2026-11-24: `aptf-intake extract` (Claude Haiku over listing text) and `aptf-intake promote-sites` (websites listings already name).** User: use aggregators to find each building's own website, and read facts that only sit in prose. `extract craigslist|furnishedfinder` sends the title and description of active listings not read yet to headless `claude -p --model haiku` with no tools, ten per call, in a fixed JSON schema (address, building name, website, furnished, private bath, minimum stay, utilities). An address counts only if its street part appears verbatim in the text, a building name only if it appears, a website only if it appears and passes `clean_url`; only empty fields are filled. The result is a new observation of the same listing (`extra.extracted` names the filled fields; run component `extract`), so identity and the dashboard use it and the next scrape supersedes it; answers are cached by text in `data/extract_cache.json`, so re-reading after a scrape costs nothing. `promote-sites --chunk X` turns `extra.website` values (Apartments.com's field, URLs read from Craigslist) into `[[found]]` sites (`found_by = <source> listing`, one per place, the URL most of its listings give) and runs the existing Apify fetch and address check, which also scrapes the accepted ones. A place whose only proposals came from listings and failed the check can still be searched by `discover-sites`. Why Haiku through `claude -p` and not an Apify LLM actor: nothing needs fetching, the subscription pays, and runs are logged. Replaced: facts in prose ignored; aggregator websites unused.
- **2026-11-24: Claude Code is a flake dependency.** User's request: the installed `claude` on the NixOS box was outdated. The package wrapper puts nixpkgs' `claude-code` (2.1.329 at the locked revision) on PATH, allowed by an unfree predicate naming only it; it uses the machine's existing login. Replaced: a separate `nix shell nixpkgs/nixos-unstable#claude-code`.
- **2026-11-24: Redirect links are followed, not cut down.** User: click through and keep where the link lands. Craigslist posts link through redirect hosts that name the building in the query string (`apt-rental.com?n=highland_gardens&...`); cutting the query left a bare home page. `extract` now keeps a link whole (`clean_url(keep_query=True)`), `promote-sites` fetches it through Apify (the scraper follows redirects and returns the loaded URL), and an accepted check records `final_url` and its platform; `load_sites` then scrapes the final URL and keeps the original as `link`. `extract --reread` reapplies cached answers to listings already read (free), starting from the scraped observation under the extracted one. Replaced: query strings dropped from every link.
- **2026-11-24: Text that contradicts its source goes to Verify.** User: a conflict between what a source stated and what its own text says should be kept as a second reading and shown in the conflicts tab. `extract` keeps the source's value and records the text's in `extra.extracted.conflicts` (address by street line, `store.keys.same_street`, see 2026-11-25; furnished, private bath); `verify.text_conflict_queue` lists each such listing as a one-listing conflict with a '<source> (text)' column, after the cross-source conflicts. Known cost of this: drive times are about 10 min off (straight line ignores freeways), transit cannot be estimated (rules reading only transit stay `needs_check`), and estimates between 0.67x and 1.5x of a limit stay `needs_check`. Haiku stays for reading text (`extract`), Sonnet for searching. `discover.candidates` now skips a building only if it was searched by the same or a stronger model (each `[[found]]`/`[[miss]]` records `model`; older rows take it from their run). You answer with a URL or 'No website', stored as a place annotation `website` (append-only); `promote-sites` treats a typed URL as the strongest lead (`found_by = you (Verify)`). The Prometheus parser made a plan out of every floor plan drawing in the page data (`floorplanSvg`, beds and sqft read from file names like `..._1100SQFT_3B2BR_NORTH-3A.svg`); the data keeps drawings of plans not offered (old and rent-control plans), and the offered plans load by JavaScript. - **2026-11-24: Pipeline status panel in the dashboard.** User: a full table of which steps have run, which have not and which could run now, from intake to Google routing, and which aggregator areas and building websites were synced more than a week ago. It must be live, so it is computed on each request from `runs`, `observations`, `trips`, `commute_legs`, `api_calls`, `checks`, `annotations`, the coverage ledger and the config (`pipeline.overview`, about 1.5 s on ~1,000 places), and stores nothing. Rules: headline states count only active chunks (else every stage reads partial forever); identity's 'left' is what its own rule would still place (the unplaceable stay visible, not todo); text extraction counts what `extract` would read (active, 40+ characters of text); estimates mirror `compute_all`'s place set; trips count only under current point ids; the no-website count uses the queue's definition of a named building (`verify.is_named_building`, now shared). `routing_candidates` takes an `evaluate_all` result already in hand. - **2026-11-24: `aptf-intake render`: own sites rendered in a browser, plans and prices read by Haiku.** User: get the plans and prices that load by JavaScript, clicking 'show more' and the like. Each site in the list (Avalon excluded: its page data gives every unit) is fetched through Apify's `apify/playwright-scraper`, which waits for the page, clicks expanders ('view more floor plans', 'load all'; never apply, tour, contact, schedule, sign-in), scrolls, and opens the site's own floor-plans page once. Trial 2026-11-24: Park Place showed 5 plans, then 7 after clicking 'VIEW MORE FLOOR PLANS' (North 2CR at $6,145 among them, no 3-bedroom); a real browser got past The Laurel's 403; Essex (The Montclaire) answered 429 even to the browser. Haiku (`claude -p`, no tools) lists plans in a fixed schema; a plan counts only if its name is on the page, its price and sqft only if those numbers are on the page. Also: Avalon units keep the full unit id as the site shows it ('00D-130' from `unitId`) and link to the unit's own page. Replaced: plans and prices that load by JavaScript were invisible.
- **2026-11-24: Verify shows conflicts ten to a screen; retired listings never conflict; conflicts awaiting an own-site render go last.** User: a conflict at one building compared an aggregator's sqft with an own-site plan the site no longer offers, and the building's pending render would settle it anyway. `conflict_queue` and `agreement` now read only active listings (and skip `site_info` rows). A conflict at a place with an own site that has not been rendered (no `extra.platform = 'render'` direct row, not an Avalon site) is marked `pending_render` with a reason and ordered after the actionable and text conflicts; text conflicts are unaffected by rendering. New `GET /api/verify/batch` returns up to n conflicts with their observations, fields and place label plus totals; `/api/verify/next` keeps its order and gains `conflicts=false` for the flow after the batch screens. The dashboard shows ten compact blocks per screen with Previous / Next 10 (verdicts already given show as pressed, so a conflict checked across two visits counts as done when paging), then the one-at-a-time flow; the Verify button and the Pipeline panel count pending conflicts separately. Doubtful by design: a site whose render failed or found nothing, or that left the site list, stays pending until it renders. Replaced: conflicts one at a time, retired rows compared, no notion of a pending render.
- **2026-11-24: An address without a comma before the city gets the same key.** User spotted two 'Highland Gardens' places: Haiku copied a Craigslist address as written ('234 Escuela Avenue Mountain View, CA 94040'), and `parse_address` split street from city only at commas, so the city stayed glued to the street and the building got a second key (Park Place and Novo had the same duplicate). Now, when the street line runs on past a street-type word (st, ave, rd, way, real, ...; at least one name word before it), the rest is taken as the city. 'Mountain View Ave' keeps its type word last and is unchanged; 'Avenue A' keeps 'Avenue' as the name. A similarity scan for remaining near-duplicates (same street number, similar street name, close coordinates) follows. Replaced: comma-only split.
- **2026-11-24: Dashboard speed: whole-database results are cached by data version; raw data loads on demand.** User: opening a building took 1-3 s. Each drawer recomputed goal results and best trips for every place, read the `places` and `listings` views (computed over everything), recomputed website status, and read each listing's whole raw file (up to 8 MB) to ship 200 KB per listing. Now `create_app` keeps those results until `data_version` changes (max row id of every log table, the last run's finish time, config file mtimes), so they stay exact; raw data comes from `GET /api/raw?source=&source_id=` when the 'raw' button opens, and is that listing's own item (it used to be the head of the whole file). Local timings: drawer 1.5 s -> 0.4 s, response 120-540 KB instead of several MB. Replaced: recomputing per click; raw files in every drawer.
- **2026-11-24: Similarity scan for duplicate places; the address's own city wins.** User asked for a similarity comparison. A scan of the live places (same street number, street names at least 85% similar, within 150 m, different keys, third-party stay places excluded since they are separate on purpose) found 4 pairs left after the comma fix, each a parser gap: a listing's own city field (a Craigslist area label) overrode the city written in the address; a unit in its own comma slot ('1870 California St, 11, Mountain View') was read as the city; and a one-number range ('881-881 E El Camino Real'). `parse_address` now takes the address's city first and the listing's city only as a fallback, reads a short number slot after the street as the unit, and collapses N-N to N (real ranges such as 4180-4190 stay). Replaced: listing city field first.
- **2026-11-24: Google Places intake (`aptf-intake run google_places`), per station box; blocked on first call.** User: start a Places feed of apartment communities. Per station box rather than per city because Text Search takes a rectangle `locationRestriction` (results stay in the walk zone) and caps a query at 60 results, which a 3 km box is less likely to exceed than a city. Strict `includedType` (`apartment_complex`, then `apartment_building`) instead of free text, so shops and agents never come back. Pricing read 2026-11-24 (developers.google.com/maps/billing-and-pricing/pricing and places/web-service/data-fields): `id` and `nextPageToken` are Essentials (IDs only, free); displayName, formattedAddress, location, businessStatus, types, primaryType, googleMapsUri are Pro ($32 / 1,000, 5,000 free a month); websiteUri, nationalPhoneNumber, rating, userRatingCount are Enterprise ($35 / 1,000, 1,000 free a month), so every call bills as Text Search Enterprise. Caps: `places_monthly_call_cap = 900` (under the free 1,000), `max_calls = 30` per run; metered in `api_calls` as provider `places`, separate from Routes. Rows are buildings (`site_info`), never offers. Not checked yet: Google's terms on storing Places content other than place ids (to read before the first real run). **Blocker:** the first trial call (one request, MV box) answered `HTTP 403 PERMISSION_DENIED`, reason `ACCESS_DENIED`, service `places.googleapis.com`: the key's project does not have Places API (New) enabled, or the key's API restrictions exclude it. Unblock: enable "Places API (New)" in the key's Google Cloud project and allow it on the key. No other call was made. Replaced: nothing (new source).
- **2026-11-24: Zumper and PadMapper intake, per city; `aptf-verify samples`.** User: add Zumper and PadMapper and give random samples to check against the sites. Actors from the Apify store (stats 2026-11-24, 30-day runs): Zumper `scrapemind/zumperCom-Scraper` (rating 5.0 from 2 reviews, 60 of 64 runs succeeded, $0.00099 per item, $0.00005 per start; only one with a detail option among the cheap ones; `jongoose/zumper-scraper`, 162/162, $0.001, gives search results only), PadMapper `bovi/padmapper-scraper` (12/12, $0.0024 per item; no reviews on any PadMapper actor). A 5-item probe of each showed the same listing ids on both sites (Zumper owns PadMapper) and that search results are buildings with ranges; Zumper's detail option adds the floor plans and the building's homepage. Through Apify's datacenter proxy Zumper served a page without its data ("could not find `__PRELOADED_STATE__`"), so the Zumper input sets no proxy. Trial (Mountain View city, copy of the database): Zumper 62 listables (the actor reports 62 in total for the city), 122 offered plans and units at 62 places, $0.057; 37 places existed, 25 new (some are Blueground stay places and trailer or room rentals), website on 8 buildings; 52 offers match a Zillow listing on beds and price, 19 Apartments.com, 2 Craigslist. PadMapper 60 items (cap hit), 44 offers and 16 building summaries at 57 places, $0.144; every PadMapper listing id was also in the Zumper run; no website. Reverse: of our active Mountain View listings, Zumper's buildings cover 25 of 161 Zillow places, 7 of 65 Apartments.com buildings, 5 of 40 Craigslist places, so neither site holds most of what we have. Each building row from both runs is shown with its page by `aptf-verify samples --source <s> --n 5 --seed N` (seeded sample over rows sorted by source_id, so a seed picks the same rows on any copy holding the same listings). Replaced: the untested `tri_angle/real-estate-aggregator` lead for Zumper.
