Operators: Equity Residential, Essex, Irvine Company, Greystar, UDR, Prometheus, AvalonBay, city page URLs found by web search (search is not a listing fetch; the pages themselves only through Apify). Haiku over the rendered text and link list, the same verbatim checks as `render`, answer kept in the raw item so `reparse` needs no Claude. Trial (Mountain View, copy of the database, $0.013 Apify + Haiku): UDR 2, Greystar 7, AvalonBay 5 communities (14 rows, 13 with their community page, 11 at existing places, 3 new: 599 Castro (560 Hope St), Central Park at Whisman Station (100 N Whisman Rd.), Sevens (777 W Middlefield, which Apartments.com has as 777 W Middlefield Rd: an address-form miss in identity, not a new building). **Blocker:** Equity (403), Irvine Company (403), Prometheus (403) and Essex (429) refuse Apify's default (datacenter) proxy even in the browser actor; they would need Apify's residential proxy group (more cost per page) or another route (user's decision). Replaced: "portfolio discovery follows an operator's 'other properties' page" (never built).
- **2026-11-24: Address parts read by Haiku, cached per address string; duplicate scan in the Pipeline panel.** User: the hand-written parser kept missing formats ('rolling our own'). Tested first: `usaddress`/`scourgify` read most formats but split 'El Camino Real' wrongly (street 'El Camino Real Mountain', or city 'Camino Real Sunnyvale'), the most common street here; the Google geocoder would be authoritative but metered. Chosen: `aptf-identity normalize` sends each new distinct address string to Haiku (`claude -p`, no tools, ~50 per call) for number, direction, street name, type, unit, city, ZIP. Checks: the number must appear in the original, every street-name word must appear (abbreviations expanded on both sides), the city is kept only if written; a failure keeps the parser's reading. Cached in `data/address_cache.json`; identity reads the cache on every run. The key formula is unchanged ('street|city'), so clean addresses keep their keys and ratings stay attached. The Pipeline panel gains a stage 'Addresses read and duplicate buildings' (`identity.addresses.suspected_duplicates`: same number, street names 85%+ alike, within 150 m, stay places excluded); `aptf-identity duplicates` lists them. Replaced: the parser as the only reader.
- **2026-11-24: Fixes to the three new sources before their first live run.** Review of the trials: (1) Zumper plan rows were keyed `<item id>:fp:<plan id>`, but the item id is just one of the building's plans (all 31 buildings in the trial), so a plan leaving the market would have renamed every row of the building; now `plan:<plan id>` and summaries `building:<building id>`. (2) Places ran one fetch per box, so `max_calls` was per box; now one fetch per run (batch) with the counter spanning every box, per-box truncation (`ActorRun.truncated_areas`), and a cap reached mid-run keeps what was paid for. (3) A Haiku failure on one portfolio page is kept on that page (`communities_error`, unparsed, run partial) instead of losing the paid pages. (4) `reparse` no longer needs the source's section in `sources.toml`. (5) `aptf-verify samples` prints lat/lon; Zumper rows keep the listing's phone. Trial rows were reparsed on the copy (free). Replaced: the first versions of the same day.
- **2026-11-24: Google's geocoder is the first reader of an address for building identity.** User: Haiku parts plus the parser still left sources disagreeing on facts (Zumper's search city 'Mountain View' for a Los Altos ZIP; 'Infinity Wy'). `aptf-identity geocode` sends each address string not geocoded yet to the Geocoding API (official API; Essentials: 10,000 free a month, then $5 per 1,000, checked 2026-11-24), logs every call in `api_calls` as provider 'geocoding' and stops at `geocoding_monthly_call_cap` (4,900, the user's 'under 5k'). A result counts only if it is street-level (street_address, premise, subpremise) and its house number is the one written in the address; then the key uses Google's street and locality with the unchanged formula, so clean addresses keep their keys. Cached in `data/geocode_cache.json`; identity prefers Google, then the Haiku parts, then the parser. Replaced: text-only readings as the authority.
- **2026-11-24: Haiku address reader removed; Google geocoder then the parser.** User: replace the Haiku reader with geocoding. Building identity now reads an address with Google's geocoded answer when it placed the address precisely, else `store.keys.parse_address`. `aptf-identity normalize` and `data/address_cache.json` are gone; the Pipeline stage counts addresses geocoded and suspected duplicates. Replaced: Haiku parts as the middle reader.
- **2026-11-24: Render follows only links inside the community's own pages.** A verify sample showed Central Park at Whisman Station's render reading Encasa (Sunnyvale): on an operator's site (greystar.com) the 'availability' link it followed led to another building. The floor-plans link must now start with the start page's origin plus its first path segment. Replaced: any same-site link.
- **2026-11-24: Own website wins.** User (approved): where a building's own site prices offers, judge its goals on those. `evaluate_all` restricts a place's result to its active priced own-site rows when it has any (`plans.own_site_places` / `is_own_priced`); every listing still gets a result (`decides: false` for the others), nothing is deleted, aggregators keep being collected, and `routing_candidates` skips non-deciding rows. Verify drops conflicts at such places (own site vs aggregator: the own site is the truth; among aggregators or text ones: moot). The drawer shows each other source's lowest price per plan ('also listed at $X on zillow') and marks plans only aggregators list there. Measured on the local copy (older than the live database, no rendered sites yet): one place has own-site prices (Avalon Mountain View, 30 priced units; its 3 Zillow rows stop deciding), no goal status flips (temp 0/175/805, lease 52/37/891 pass/check/fail before and after), conflicts 6 cross-source (1 pending render) and 0 text, unchanged. Doubtful by design: an aggregator's offer of a different kind at the same place (e.g. a furnished unit listed on Zillow, while the own site prices only unfurnished leases) no longer decides that place either. Replaced: a place's best listing from any source decides.
- **2026-11-24: `reparse --covers complete|partial` credits a trial's areas in the coverage ledger.** User: make the Zumper, PadMapper and portfolio trials part of the main data. Those trials ran on a copy of the database, so their runs never reached the live ledger, and a full run would have paid for Mountain View again; a plain reparse still never counts as coverage (its original run already does). Also, user: bulk Google routing of the lease candidates is cancelled (not run). Replaced: reparse that could not record coverage.
- **2026-11-24: Render follows per-plan unit lists.** User (approved, about $0.003 a page): Highland Gardens and The Marc show plans with a 'VIEW APARTMENTS' button leading to unit lists with unit numbers, prices and dates. The page function now follows up to 5 such links per site (or clicks a link-less button in place and reads its dialog), only inside the start page's scope, never apply/tour/contact/schedule/lease-now; the floor-plans page hands down what is left of the 5. `maxRequestsPerCrawl` is 7 pages a site; each page has its own text budget for Haiku (replacing one 24,000-character cut that would have dropped unit pages). US numeric dates ('12/1/2026', UDR's unit lists) are read as available dates. Trial on a copy of the local database (2026-11-24, two runs, 7 sites, 13 pages, $0.042 Apify + $0.010 Haiku): Verve's 'VIEW APARTMENTS' opened its apartments-pricing page: 5 units with numbers (Apartment 122, Two Bedroom B2L, 2/2, 1,319 sqft, $6,034, now; Apartment 205, One Bedroom A1C, 787 sqft, $4,230, 1/29/2027; ...); Birch Creek the same, 7 units (Apartment 139, 1 bd, 800 sqft, $3,297, now; ...); Park Place 7 plans and The Dean 13 plans (Prometheus pages have no unit links). Highland Gardens and The Marc are not in this machine's (older) site lists, so they were not tried here. Doubtful: Village Residences says '15 available' but lists them only in a map/list widget the text does not show: read as no plans. Replaced: one floor-plans page per site.
- **2026-11-24: Render and site-check outcomes record failure vs not; failures are retried.** User: 'clearly record failure vs not'. Before, a render saw only the pages that came back (Apify's dataset was fetched with `clean=true`, which drops the scrapers' failed-request items) and wrote 'failed' for a 403, a timeout and an empty page alike; and `promote-sites` wrote a page that was not fetched (HTTP 403) as `[[check]] ok = false`, which `load_sites` read as a rejection, dropping the site for good. Now each render site gets an outcome (see intake, own-site render) and the next chunk run retries the transient ones first; `fetch_and_check` writes an `outcome` (`ok`, `rejected` only when the fetched page lacks the address, else `blocked`/`timeout`/`error`/`not_fetched` with the HTTP status and message read from the run log, or `empty`), `load_sites` drops only a site whose latest check is `rejected` (old 'page not fetched' rows now read as retryable), and `promote-sites` fetches retryable sites again (`--retry-failed`: only those). The Pipeline panel and the drawer show each site's last render and check with the reason. Real run on a copy (2026-11-24): Sevens timed out (`page.goto: net::ERR_TIMED_OUT`, 3 tries) and was picked first by the next run, which rendered it (no plans offered); Village Residences needed 2 retries to load. Replaced: one 'failed' state and 'page not fetched' as a rejection.
- **2026-11-24: A contact or tour form is never a building's site.** Station 245 and Woodside Place Apartments were accepted as their own sites: `promote-sites` took their Craigslist short links (`station245.prospectportal.com/su/6ks5m6`, `amc.touraptnow.com/rip8j3`), which redirect to a lead form (`.../mountain-view/station-245/guest-card/`, `amc.touraptnow.com/emailus.cfm?...`). A lead form shows the building's address, so the address check passed and the form became the scraped URL. Now a page whose path is a tour, apply, contact or lead form (`discover.ACTION_PATH`, plus `guest-card` and `email`) fails the check (`landed on a contact or tour form`), and `load_sites` drops a discovered site whose URL or recorded `final_url` is one, so checks stored before this rule are reread without a refetch. Not cut back to a home page: Woodside's would be the vendor's root, not the building. Replaced: any page showing the address passed.
- **2026-11-24: One Verify list of buildings whose own site gave nothing usable; any number of URLs per building.** User: 'list all places with invalid info (no floor plans etc found) ... allow specifying a url there to check. (A URL not THE URL ...; there can be multiple sources for the same information)'. `own_site_queue` replaces `no_website_queue` (folded in as the reason 'no website known') and the one-at-a-time no-website screen; the Verify button and the Pipeline panel's verify stage count it (`queues.own_site`, `counts.own_site`). A URL you add was the place's single website before (`promote` proposed it only for a place without a site, and `load_sites` dropped a found site at a place the hand list already has); now each is its own `[[found]]` site and kept. On a copy of the live database (2026-11-24): 93 of 116 named buildings in active chunks: 57 no website, 14 read but no plans or prices (Landsby: plans without prices), 12 every site failed (The Montclaire, Aviana: address check never fetched the page), 5 address check failed (Palmetto, Palo Alto Plaza, Hidden Garden), 3 not checked yet, 2 forms (Station 245's guest card, Woodside Place's email-us form). Replaced: the no-website queue and one website per place from Verify.
- **2026-11-25: Dashboard cache survives running intake; the own-site list reads `listings` once.** User: the pipeline tab stopped loading. With 2,529 places (up from 992 after the all-cities scrape) and intake writing every few seconds, every request found the data version moved and rebuilt every whole-database result under the one lock; `/api/pipeline` was not cached at all and took 97 s, 91 s of it in `own_site_urls`, which queried the `listings` view twice per building (282 buildings, each query a full view build). Now: `own_site_inputs` reads both facts for all places in one query each (queue 91 s -> 1.1 s, overview 97 s -> 5.7 s); the cache key splits into the user's edits (links, checks, annotations, config: recompute at once) and scraped rows (observations, trips, runs: recompute at most every 120 s, `SCRAPE_REFRESH_S`); the pipeline overview, conflicts, own-site list, agreement, sample count, row counts and the conflict screen's place lookups go through the cache; the lock is re-entrant. Warm on the live copy: status, pipeline, Verify screens about 0.01 s, map 1.6 s, drawer 1.1 s. Replaced: recompute on any new row; one view query per building.
- **2026-11-25: Run details live in the Pipeline panel, not the header.** User: the per-source strip belongs in Pipeline. The header keeps the place and listing counts and a red "N recent errors" link that opens Pipeline; the panel starts with "Latest runs" (each source's last run: time, status, seen, new, changed), Apify spend, Google usage, hand-check accuracy and the recent errors written out. Replaced: every source's last run, spend and errors in the header strip.
- **2026-11-25: Residential proxy for sites that block Apify's datacenter proxy.** User: use some residential proxy credit (tabled 2026-11-24). `apify.browser_proxy(residential)` gives a web/playwright-scraper input either the default datacenter proxy or Apify's `RESIDENTIAL` group with US exits, plus a pre-navigation hook that aborts images, fonts and video (residential is billed by traffic). Portfolio: an operator entry with `proxy = "residential"` (Equity Residential, Essex, Irvine Company, Prometheus: 403/429 on the datacenter proxy) goes into a second actor run of the same `fetch`, merged into one result, each item tagged `proxy`. Render: `aptf-intake render --residential` (with `--retry-failed` or `--site`) for own sites whose last render was blocked; the run's params record the proxy. Spend is bounded by pages (`maxRequestsPerCrawl`), since `maxTotalChargeUsd` does not bind this actor. Replaced: those operators' pages left blocked.
- **2026-11-25: Search-areas map in the Pipeline panel.** User: show what each area means, one colour per kind. `GET /api/areas` (`aptfinder/area_map.py`) gives every intake area with the sources that search its kind and a shape: a station box is its rectangle plus the walk circle it was drawn around (blue); a ZIP (orange) or city (green) is the convex hull of the listings its searches returned (`extra.area`), or, for areas scraped before rows were tagged, of the listings whose address names it (dashed). Stray pins (beyond 3x the median distance from the centre, or 8 km) are not drawn; an area whose returns are mostly far apart is listed as "search ignored the location" instead of outlined. That flagged Craigslist's 95089 (Alviso) search: 296 listings from San Francisco to Petaluma, i.e. the radius was not applied. Replaced: no picture of what an area covers.
- **2026-11-25: No chunks; each source covers every area of its own kind.** User: 'tile the chunks by station for the things that take in station, or zip code for the apps that take in zip code, or city for the apps that take in only city, chunks do not need to be global they can be per source.' All four chunks were active, so chunks only set the order of work. `config/areas.toml` loses `chunks`, `active`, `chunk_stations` and each area's `chunks`/`chunk` (the boxes keep `station`, the ZIPs `stations`); `Area.chunks` is gone; an old-shape file still loads (the keys are ignored). `aptf-intake run <source>` without `--area` runs every not-yet-covered area of the source's kind; `--chunk` is removed from `run`, `render`, `promote-sites` and `discover-sites`. `render` takes every site in the list (every site carried one of the four chunks, so the same set); `discover-sites`, `promote-sites`, the own-site list, `compute --candidates` and the Pipeline's website and Google counts take places inside a station walk zone (`areas.in_walk_zone`, the 1.54 km radius around each `[[box]]` station), which with all chunks active is exactly the set they used. That filter stays because on the live copy 1,530 of 2,532 places (269 of 574 named buildings) lie outside every walk zone (ZIP and city searches spill over), and searching or rendering them would roughly double paid work. Dashboard: no TODO chip or dimming; one filter checkbox 'outside walk zones' (shown by default, the place payload's `in_zone`); station circles all drawn alike. Pipeline: the intake matrix is one table per area kind, a row per area, no chunk column; suggested commands carry no `--chunk`. Coverage reads `runs.params.areas` as before: on a copy of the live database the ledger (state and run per source and area, 320 pairs: 285 done, 17 partial, 18 todo) is identical before and after, with the old and the new areas.toml. Replaced: global chunks, `active`, `areas.chunk_of` / `is_active`, the TODO chip and `--chunk`.
- **2026-11-25: Commute limits in the list filters, remembered per browser.** User: add any number of filters such as time to Meta, time to Google, transit to Palo Alto, then sort by price. "+ commute limit" adds a row (target, mode, at most N minutes); every limit must hold; each card shows its time for each limit. Places with no time for a limit's pair (not routed; transit and bike+bus have no straight-line estimate) are kept unless "keep places without a time" is off, the dashboard's form of "unknown never rejects". Stored in `localStorage` (`aptf.commuteLimits`), read in try/catch so the page works without it; this is a per-viewer convenience, not shared state. Replaced: one target and mode at a time.
- **2026-11-25: Amenities belong to the building; an amenity rule can match several words.** User: Laurel Grove has a fitness center but did not count as having a gym. Two causes: the rule matched only the word in `name` ("gym", not "Fitness Center"), and since own website wins, the place was judged on its own-site plan rows, which carry no amenity list. Now `evaluate_all` gives every listing at a place the union of all its sources' amenity lists, and an amenity rule takes `match = [...]` (gym: "gym", "fitness"); the reason names the amenity found ("has gym: Fitness Center"). Live copy: lease places with a gym 225, none listed 1,531, unknown 659. Replaced: per-listing amenities matched on the rule's name only.
- **2026-11-25: Apify API reads retry hiccups.** A Craigslist run crashed on one empty status response (JSONDecodeError) while its actor kept running, so its results were never read. `apify._get_json` retries a 5xx, empty or non-JSON answer up to 6 times with backoff for status polls and dataset reads. Replaced: a single GET per poll.
- **2026-11-25: `promote-sites --browser [--residential]`: the address check in a real browser.** The cheap check fetch (cheerio) could not load 88 of the websites listings name (71 "fetch failed", 17 not returned): many community sites refuse it. `fetch_and_check(browser=True)` loads one page per site in the playwright actor (`BROWSER_CHECK_PAGE`: loaded URL, page text, ld+json), with the residential proxy if asked, and applies the same address and form checks. The run is an intake run of `direct` with `sites` rather than `areas` in its params, so it is not coverage, and its raw file is `run<N>_browsercheck.json`. Typical use: `promote-sites --retry-failed --browser`, then `--residential` for what is still blocked. Replaced: only the cheap fetch for checks.
- **2026-11-25: Zumper single units count half baths.** User: 1090 Mcgregor Way (Palo Alto house) reads 2 baths, Zumper's page says 2.5. Raw item: `min_bathrooms` 2, `min_all_bathrooms` [2, 1], `detail_entity_data_half_bathrooms` 1, no floor plans; the single-unit path read `min_bathrooms`, the full count only (plan rows already added the plan's `half_bathrooms`). Now a listing without plans takes full + half/2 from `min_all_bathrooms`/`max_all_bathrooms`, the lease.py Zillow convention, and keeps `baths_full`/`baths_half` in extra (plan rows too). Affected on the live database: 36 of 606 Zumper listings (29 in run 145, 7 in the Mountain View trial), every one a half bath dropped (e.g. 440 Churchill Ave 7 for 7.5). PadMapper's actor gives integer `baths_min`/`baths_max` only, with no half count: the 7 Mountain View trial listings that Zumper shows with a half bath read the full count there too (not fixable from its output). Replaced: `min_bathrooms` for single units.
- **2026-11-25: Identity's rule links count as scrapes, not edits; every dashboard error names its request and HTTP status.** User: the live page showed a red "Failed to load:" with nothing after the colon. Server log: the 18:40 load got `/api/places` (200 at 18:40:32) but `/api/status` never completed; it was cut at 18:42:33 (125 s), and a second browser's load at 18:44:26 was cut at the same 125 s mark. No GET returned an error from uvicorn, so the failure was an error page from Cloudflare's edge after the origin stalled. The empty text: `api()` showed `statusText`, which HTTP/2 never carries, or a JSON `detail`, which an edge HTML page lacks. The stall: the build live then (965672c) rebuilt every whole-database cache on each scraped row while seven intake runs wrote; the request threads queued behind one lock used 14 CPU-minutes, and the restart at 18:53 needed SIGKILL. fa0748d (deployed 18:54) already throttles scraped-row refreshes, but `data_version` still counted every `links` row as a user edit, and identity appends rule links during intake (130 in one minute measured live), so each one still forced a full rebuild (7 to 11 s per endpoint on the box). Now only `author = 'user'` links count as edits; rule links join the scrape key. `api()` errors read `<path>: HTTP <status>[: detail]` and a network failure `<path>: <error>`, and a failed `/api/status` shows in the header strip instead of failing the page. Replaced: all links in the edit key; `statusText`-only error messages; boot failing on the status counts.
- **2026-11-25: A raw block in the drawer scrolls inside itself.** User: the drawer's raw JSON ran past its right edge. The `<pre>` sits in a `<td colspan=9>`, and a table cell grows to its widest line, so opening a raw row widened the listings table from 679 to 1,180 px (measured in Chrome on Laurel Grove) and the whole table scrolled instead of the code. `.raw-row pre.raw` now has `width: 0; min-width: 100%`: it adds nothing to the cell's width, fills it, and scrolls both ways (table stays 679 px; the pre scrolls 1,168 px of lines in 667). Replaced: a pre sized by its content.
- **2026-11-25: List filters saved for everyone.** User: "it should be saved for everyone if I click save". 'Save for everyone' stores sort, target, mode, statuses, min beds, max $/bed, inactive, outside walk zones, keep-without-a-time and the commute limits as one annotations row (view / list / filters, JSON in `comment`) through `append_annotation`: append-only, no new table, latest row wins, refused in the redacted view like every write. Load order: this browser's working copy if it has unsaved changes, else the saved set, else the page defaults. The working copy is written only when you change a filter and deleted on save and on 'reset to saved', so 'a local copy exists' means exactly 'unsaved changes', and a browser without changes picks up each new save. The old per-browser `aptf.commuteLimits` key is folded into the working copy once, then removed. A save is a new annotations row, so every cached result recomputes once (7 to 11 s live); acceptable for a rare click. Limit rows now wrap at narrow widths (the remove button overflowed at 320 px). Replaced: commute limits in localStorage only, other list filters not kept.
- **2026-11-25: Commute limits become generic requirements: a commute target or the nearest point of any set.** User: "in the commute requirements make it a generic requirements so we can have for example proximity to google bus walking". A row is now (point, mode, at most N minutes), where the point is one commute target (`commute_targets:Meta HQ`) or `set:<name>`, the nearest point of a set in `config/point_sets.toml` ("walk to nearest Google bus ≤ 10 min"). `/api/places` carries a compact `nearest` map per place, `"<set>|<mode>": {minutes, provider, point}` from `filter.nearest_by_set` over `best_trips` (commute targets stay in `minutes`, one by one), cached per data version like the other whole-database results; on the live copy the payload grew from 10.9 to 11.9 MB. Cards show each requirement's time and which point ("walk to nearest Google bus 2.9 min (<stop name>) (est.)"); unknown still never rejects unless 'keep places without a time' is off. Rows saved or stored with the old `target` field read as that point. Replaced: limits over commute targets only.
- **2026-11-25: SQLite waits up to 5 minutes for the write lock; Craigslist's 94036 and 95089 searches ignore location.** With eight intake runs in parallel, the Craigslist 94027 run failed with "database is locked" after 60 s (each run rebuilds identity after writing); its raw file was intact and was loaded with `reparse --covers complete`. The connection timeout is now 300 s. The search-areas map showed that Craigslist's searches for 94036 (a PO-box ZIP) and 95089 (Alviso) returned 300 and 296 listings from across the Bay Area, none inside the ZIP: the radius is not applied there. Both are recorded as done; do not rerun them for Craigslist. Replaced: a 60 s lock wait.
- **2026-11-25: Pipeline panel: compute one attribute for chosen places, dry run first.** User: "add a thing to pipeline view to trigger calculation of particular attributes for such as driving to meta hq". The panel picks (point or `set:<name>`, mode, provider: estimate by default or Google) and the places: those the list shows (stay places route as their building) or every place in a walk zone. `POST /api/compute/dry_run` gives the places, the uncached calls Google would make (the CLI's `compute --dry-run` logic, now `trips.dry_run`: straight-line answers on a scratch copy of the database, nothing routed, paid or written), their cost beyond the free tier and any monthly cap they would pass; only then does Run appear. `POST /api/compute/run` starts one background thread on its own connection (`compute_all(..., only=(point, mode))`, the one new parameter, which routes that pair whether or not the set's `modes` lists it, the way the drawer's on-demand routing does), logs a `commute` run, and clears the dashboard cache when it ends so the new trips show at once; `GET /api/compute/status` reports progress. A Google run must carry the dry run's call count (`confirm_calls`), which becomes its `max_calls`, so it never spends more than the dry run showed; `MeteredSource` still enforces the monthly caps, a set with `paid = false` is still never routed by Google, and the redacted view refuses both POSTs. On the live copy: estimate bike to the nearest Caltrain for the 451 listed places appended 902 trips; a Google dry run for drive to Meta HQ on 20 places would make 20 calls, $0.00 (565 of 5,000 used this month). Bulk Google routing stays off; this is the deliberate per-attribute path. Replaced: attributes computed only by `aptf-commute compute` or per place in the drawer.
- **2026-11-25: Correction: the empty "Failed to load:" was a deploy restart cutting `/api/places`; GETs retry across a restart.** The entry above blamed a stalled `/api/status` at 18:40, but the user's page showed 2,558 places, a count the database reached after 19:00. The matching load is 19:45:48: `/api/meta` 200, `/api/status` 200 at 19:45:54 (the header's '2558 places'), `/api/places` never answered; at 19:45:57 a deploy restart stopped uvicorn mid-request ("Waiting for background tasks to complete") and killed cloudflared, so the edge answered with an error page whose status (likely 502 or 530) is inferred, not logged. Cards stayed empty, the banner had no text. Deploys restart the service often (28 times on 11-24), so `api()` now retries a GET twice, 3 and 6 s apart, on a network failure or an edge/gateway status (502, 503, 504, 520 to 524, 530) before failing with `<path>: HTTP <status>`. The links-cache and message changes above still stand. Replaced: one attempt per request.
- **2026-11-25: Website check runs never retire listings.** The browser address checks of discovered sites (`discover.py`, runs 179 and 183 on 11-25) were logged as `intake`/`direct` runs without a `scope`, so they counted as full runs: `source_last_full_run` for `direct` became run 183 and every earlier own-site row went inactive (live: 28 of 1,194 `direct` listings active, so Avalon's units, Prometheus and every render plan dropped out of the drawers, the filter's 'own website wins' and the conflict rule). Check runs now record `scope: targeted`, and the view also skips any run with `params.check` (runs rows are append-only, so the two old runs keep their params). On a copy of live: 960 of 1,194 `direct` listings active again; no other source changed. Replaced: a check run counted as a full run.
- **2026-11-25: Text reading: every text source, the whole entry, building and unit facts with quotes, parallel calls, run after each intake.** User: "can we not haiku the whole entry" (Laurel Grove's 'Fitness Center' did not count as a gym until a `match` list was added), "run a bunch of parallelized threads calling out to haiku", "make it a core pipeline phase". `extract` now covers craigslist, furnishedfinder, airbnb, apartments_com, zillow, zumper and padmapper; reads title, name, description, amenities and allowlisted raw fields (Apartments.com and Zumper keep their about text, fees, utilities, parking and pet policy only in the raw item, so `extract` re-parses `raw_path` to find it; changing the parsers and reparsing was rejected because reparsing an older raw file makes its old prices current); schema v2 adds gym, pool, laundry, parking, EV charging, air conditioning, dishwasher, cats, dogs, utilities, lease months and fees, each with a verbatim quote; positive facts go into `amenities` in plain words so the existing amenity rules and pooling see them. Calls run 8 at a time (thread pool, each its own `claude -p`, prompt on stdin), the main thread writes; one blocked call stops new ones. `aptf-intake run` of a text source reads its rows afterwards (`--no-extract` skips) and `reparse` applies cached readings; `extract all` does every source. Readings of schema v1 (craigslist, furnishedfinder) count as unread and are read again from the scraped row under them. The Pipeline stage is 'Text reading (Haiku)' with read / to read per source. Replaced: craigslist and furnishedfinder only, seven fields, title and description only, one call at a time, run by hand.
- **2026-11-25: Photos and floor-plan drawings are downloaded into our own deduplicated, capped store (`aptf-media`, table `media`).** User: "we should download images not url them, urling is sus cuz what if it changes"; the same picture appears on several sites at different sizes; "make sure the store is deduplicated and low res", "the store should inherently be deduplicated from the start", "limit to 5gb". User's decision: downloading the image files at URLs our sources already gave us, directly from this machine, is allowed (AGENTS.md rule 6 now states the exception; still no page scraping). Design: content-addressed small JPEGs (800 px q75, drawings 1,280 px), dedupe by exact hash or pHash within 6 bits per place before any write (drawings only against drawings, exact), a 5 GB cap checked before each write with priority-ordered fetching, every attempt in the new append-only `media` log (new data, not a copy of a fact). The dashboard serves the local file when there is one and the source URL otherwise. Replaced: "Photos are linked, not mirrored" and "Listing photos are not mirrored to data/media/ for now" (both 2026-11-24).
- **2026-11-25: Floor plan drawings from every source that has one, one field `extra.floorplan_image`.** User: "not just avalon, a lot of apts should" show plan drawings. Survey of the stored raw files: Avalon's units carry `floorPlan.highResolution`, a path on `resource.avalonbay.com` (the host the page's own `<img alt="Floorplan">` uses); Zumper plans carry media of type 2, drawings (in Chrome 13 sampled type-2 images were 25 to 87% white with no colour, their photos 0 to 2% white and 10 to 73% coloured), 213 of 342 offered plans in 38 buildings; Zillow gives `heroImageUrl` for 5 single units, none for buildings; Prometheus pages map plan names to SVG drawings (`floorplanSvg`; the parser had kept only the names); some own sites name drawings in alt text or file names (Sofia `1x1-770`, Selo `selo UNIT G floor plan`, vrent `/floorplans/`); Landsby's plan data and the portfolio pages have none; render's stored pages carry text only. The Avalon, Zumper and Zillow parsers now write `floorplan_image` (the name the Apartments.com parser already used; absent, not null, when there is none, so rows without one keep their content hash). Prometheus and `photos` site rows keep `plan_drawings` {label: URL}; a plan gets one only when exactly one label contains its name as whole words (so `1x1` matches neither of `1x1-770` and `1x1-763`), images captioned "open floor plan", "coming soon", placeholders, banners and CSS backgrounds are never drawings, and a file's WordPress size copies count once (the largest). Render's page function now returns each page's images (src, alt, class), and a plan read by Haiku takes a drawing the same way, only from those images, from the next render on (re-rendering the 123 sites now would cost about $2.60 Apify + $0.75 Haiku: not done, free only). `plans.plan_image` picks one per drawer plan row: own site, then own-site label, then any source. **Blocker:** Apartments.com's drawings (about 2,600 rows) are on `images1.apartments.com`, which Chrome refuses (0x0 with or without `referrerpolicy=no-referrer`); showing them needs mirroring through Apify (not built). Backfill: free reparses of the raw files behind current `direct`, `zillow` and `zumper` listings, in run order (an older file would revive retired listings); on a copy of live, plans with a displayable drawing 0 -> 384 of 4,973 (Zumper 199 in 50 places, own-site labels 141 in 12, Avalon 39 in 4, Zillow 5), active counts unchanged apart from one form-site photo row the site list already drops (Woodside Place, a touraptnow contact form). Each backfilled listing gains one history entry (`extra` is in the content hash). Replaced: drawings from Apartments.com only, all hidden as hotlink-blocked.
- **2026-11-25: Near-duplicate photo threshold set to 10 pHash bits, from a pilot on live data.** A 2,000-URL pilot on the box (`--limit 2000 --max-distance 0`: exact copies only) gave the within-place distance histogram of photo pairs: 0: 106, 2: 8, 4: 4, 6: 5, 8: 1, 10: 3, 12: 9, 14: 5, then 18: 13, 20: 34 and rising to a peak at 30 to 32. Side by side, every pair at 0 to 10 was one picture (re-cropped, re-graded, a Blueground banner moved); at 12 one pair of seven was a different drone shot of the same view, and different rooms appear only from 18. So 10, not the 6 first planned; the drawer also collapses separate files within 10 bits, which covers the pilot's files. Replaced: 6 bits.
- **2026-11-25: Furnished Finder photos through Apify's residential proxy.** User's request, after `staticproperties.furnishedfinder.com` answered HTTP 403 to all 88 direct downloads from the box (Apartments.com images, by contrast, download fine there). Hosts listed in `[media] residential_hosts` use the proxy as a plain HTTP proxy; traffic is metered and capped per run (`residential_max_mb = 300`, about $2.40 at $8 per GB), and the `-650-570` variant is fetched instead of the `-full` original because download size is what is billed. Replaced: those photos failing with 403.
- **2026-11-25: Merge a duplicate building by hand from the drawer.** User: merge leftover duplicates manually rather than by a rule. The drawer lists places within 300 m ("Same building as a nearby place? Merge…"); `POST /api/places/{key}/merge {into}` appends a user link for every listing of the place, each keeping its own unit (`<place>/<unit>` becomes `<into>/<unit>`), so a building's units do not collapse into one. Undo per listing with split. User links already win over rule links in identity. Replaced: merges one listing at a time through `/api/links`, which set the unit to the whole place.
- **2026-11-25: Compute an attribute: a search-candidates scope, typed confirmation above 20 Google calls, a run log.** User: allow routing the search candidates, log the calls, and require typing the number of queries and the calls left this month when a run is over 20 queries with less than 5,000 left. Scope `candidates` = `filter.routing_candidates` (can still pass some goal apart from commute) inside a walk zone. A Google run with `confirm_calls` > 20 is refused by the server unless `confirm_left` equals the calls left this month (cap minus `api_calls` this month); the dashboard unlocks Run only when both typed numbers match the dry run. `GET /api/compute/log` lists the latest commute runs (time, pair, provider, places, new trips, Google calls, status) under the control; every call is also a row of `api_calls`. Replaced: one click after the dry run whatever its size.
- **2026-11-25: Geocoding, identity and commute estimates run after every intake.** User: "why doesn't it auto run?" After a scrape someone had to remember `aptf-identity geocode`, `aptf-identity run` and `aptf-commute --provider estimate compute`, all free. The existing `after_intake` hook (which runs the Haiku reading) now also runs them, in that order, after any `run` or `reparse` that wrote rows; `reparse` skips geocoding so it makes no calls; `--no-followup` skips all three. Estimates go only to places without any (`compute_all` walks every place and every point, cheap per place but not per run). On a copy of live: `after intake: geocoding skipped, no GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY (424 new addresses), identity rebuilt (16 links), estimates for 339 new places (10509 trips)` in 5 s; a second pass found nothing to do. Replaced: running the three by hand (identity already ran inside `run_source`, but before geocoding).
- **2026-11-25: PadMapper dropped from routine runs (`skip = true`).** User: "is PadMapper an exact duplicate of Zumper?" The Mountain View trial hit its 60-item cap, so one uncapped run settled it: Sunnyvale (run 229, `--max-items 200`), 83 items, $0.1921; the comparison used its raw file `data/raw/padmapper/run229_Sunnyvale.json`, since storing the rows failed (`database is locked` past the 5-minute wait while other jobs wrote), so the run is `error` with no observations. All 83 PadMapper listing ids were in our Zumper raw data (82 as an item's `listing_id`, 1 as an active listing of a Zumper building), the same as all 60 in Mountain View. In reverse, Zumper's 85 Sunnyvale items (run 145) held 2 that PadMapper did not return, both single AppFolio units (670 Morse Ave, 3 bd, $4,495; 1623 Ontario Dr, 2 bd, $2,795). PadMapper's data is also poorer: whole-number baths only, no floor plans, no website, search results only, and at $0.0024 per item it costs 2.4 times what Zumper does. A source's `skip = true` in `sources.toml` makes `aptf-intake run` refuse it and leaves it out of `aptf-intake coverage` and the pipeline's intake grid; rows already stored are kept and still read by `extract`. Replaced: PadMapper as a routine per-city source (9 cities left to do).
- **2026-11-25: Possible duplicate buildings are a Verify list, answered with one click.** User: "make a duplicate builder as part of conflict resolution". `verify.duplicate_queue` pairs places by three kinds of evidence: the existing address scan, the same own website within 600 m, the same property name within 200 m. On the live copy: 92 pairs (address 15, website 41, name 69; 29 with two or more kinds), e.g. the Central Park at Whisman Station places with no address that only the website and name find. The website cap comes from the data: one complex's places sat up to 570 m apart, while managers' sites paired buildings 0.7 to 22 km apart (cbw-properties.com, vmg.appfolio.com) and rentbt.com / vrent.com named 17 places each. The bare '1600 Villa Street' place is Avalon Mountain View's Blueground stay place (`~blueground`), separate on purpose, so it is not a pair. Answers are a merge (`POST /api/places/{key}/merge`, unchanged) or an append-only annotation `place_pair` / `a|b` / `same_building` = no; nothing new is stored. Counted in `/api/status` `queues.duplicates`, the Verify button and the pipeline's Verify stage. Replaced: duplicates found only by opening a drawer and scanning 'Same building as a nearby place?', and the address scan as the only detector.
- **2026-11-25: Aggregator and listing-platform hosts are never a building's own site (`discover.AGGREGATOR_HOSTS`).** User: many failing "own sites" were vrent.com, an aggregator. Survey of the live config and database: 25 `[[found]]` sites sat on listing platforms (vrent.com 18 single-listing pages from Craigslist and Google Places links, rental.turbotenant.com 4, hemlane.com 2, a ricoh360 virtual tour 1), plus 41 vrent and 27 turbotenant links in listings. The old aggregator regex (applied only to search answers through `clean_url`) becomes one host set, matched on the host or a subdomain, extended with vrent.com, turbotenant.com, hemlane.com, matterport.com, ricoh360.com, vrbo.com. `is_aggregator(url)` is used by `clean_url`, `promote` (listing links, URLs added in Verify, and a found aggregator row no longer blocks the place's real link), `retryable`, `load_sites` (found and hand entries, by the URL and where it landed), the Verify own-site list (such a URL is not a tried site), `candidates`/`promote`/`own_site_queue`/`website_status` (own-site rows read from an aggregator page do not count as the place having a site). So such a building goes back to "no website" and is eligible for website search. Not listed: operator portfolio domains (greystar.com, avaloncommunities.com, udr.com, ...), rentcafewebsite.com, and leasing short-link hosts (apt-rental.com, *.leasehomenow.com, livewres.com, ...), which redirect to the building's page and are judged where they land. The stored `direct` rows from those pages stay (append-only) and still count in `plans.own_site_places`. On a copy of live: sites 206 -> 184, own-site list 253 -> 255 (no_website 107 -> 121, no_plans 85 -> 81, address_failed 31 -> 29, not_checked 19 -> 17, all_failed 9 -> 5), search candidates gain 19 of the 25 places. Replaced: listing-platform pages promoted, fetched, rendered and counted as own sites.
- **2026-11-25: Cards mark the attribute a place fails on, red for fail and amber for needs-check.** User: "in the search highlight red what attribute it fails on". Before, only the goal chip's tooltip listed the reasons as text. `evaluate_all` now adds `fails` per result (`rules.fact` of each non-pass rule, beside `reasons`, which keep their 3-tuple shape for `routing_candidates`), and `/api/places` passes it through; mapping is by rule type and field, never by reason text. On the live copy (2,722 places) `/api/places` grew by about 0.8 MB to 12.4 MB; the most common marks are lease beds fail (2,133), temp commute needs-check (2,532) and temp term fail (1,609). Replaced: reasons only in a chip tooltip; requirement rows without a time shown muted.
- **2026-11-25: Compute an attribute: the numbers to retype sit next to their inputs; a running job reports on its own line; a disabled button looks disabled.** User: after a 955-call dry run, a still-running job's status ("Running drive to …: 0/1 places") overwrote the line naming the numbers to type, and the locked Run button still looked clickable. Each confirm input is labelled with its value ("the Google calls (952)", "the calls left this month (4435)"), since the point is retyping to validate; the job status goes to `#c-job`, looked up on every poll, so the dry run's result in `#c-out` stays; every `button:disabled` is greyed (opacity .45, not-allowed cursor) and a "type both numbers to unlock" hint sits beside Run while it is locked. Replaced: one shared output line, and the numbers shown only in that line.
- **2026-11-25: `/api/places` carries per-place amenity flags.** User asked for a "require gym" control; the plan changed to editable goals (another change), which can use these. `amenities: {gym, in_unit_laundry, parking, pets}` per place, from every listing's amenity list at the place (Haiku's text reading included) via `filter.amenity_flags`: true when an entry contains one of the flag's words and none of its exclusions ("Dishwasher" and "Washer/Dryer Hookup" are not in-unit laundry, "no parking" is not parking), false when the place lists amenities but none match, null when nothing is listed. On the live copy (2,722 places): gym 488 true / 1,959 false / 275 null; in-unit laundry 1,087 / 1,360; parking 1,523 / 924; pets 645 / 1,802. The lease goal's gym nice-to-have is unchanged. A list checkbox row was built and verified but not shipped, since goals will replace the list filters. Replaced: no amenity data in the places payload.
- **2026-11-25: The duplicate list also reads the site list.** The request named the site list (`config.load_sites()`) and the found records as where own websites live, so their place keys join the website buckets (found sites whose last address check rejected them excluded). On the live copy with the box's config this adds no pair: every URL the site list gives two places (Greystar's Central Park at Whisman pages, citylineflats.com, paloaltoplaza.com, ...) is already named by those places' listings.
