- **2026-11-25: Apartments.com floor-plan drawings show from the photo store; own-site drawing maps are downloaded.** Browsers are refused images1.apartments.com, so `plan_image` skipped those drawings; the box downloads them fine, so a drawing on a blocked host is now shown when the store holds it (thumbnail and full-size link both `media/<sha>.jpg`). `aptf-media fetch` also takes the URLs of `extra.plan_drawings` as kind `plan`. Replaced: Apartments.com drawings never shown.
- **2026-11-25: Goals are editable in the dashboard; goals.toml is the seed.** User: "just have arbitrary frontend configurable goals." A goal edit, a new goal or a delete is one append-only `annotations` row (goal / id / definition, JSON in `comment`, a tombstone for a delete), the way saved list filters were stored; no new table. The effective set is the file overlaid by the latest row per id, used by `evaluate_all` by default, so the places list, goal chips, red/amber facts, `routing_candidates` (search candidates for compute), the pipeline's goal count and `/api/meta`'s goal list and default target all follow an edit; the dashboard cache already recomputes on any new annotation. New rule type `min` (min beds) and an amenity rule's optional `exclude` words (so an amenity rule can agree with the list's amenity flags). Rules are validated on the server against the shapes `rules.evaluate` reads; `/api/annotations` refuses goal rows so nothing unvalidated reaches the evaluator. `evaluate_all` split into `goal_inputs` + `evaluate_goal` (one goal on the live copy ~0.4 s once inputs are cached) for a draft preview. Checked on a copy of the live database: `evaluate_all` and `routing_candidates` output byte-identical before and after (sha256 of the sorted JSON dump) with no goal rows. Replaced: goals readable only from `config/goals.toml`, changed by editing the file.
- **2026-11-25: Short write transactions; identity rebuilt once per intake run.** User: "can the database not incrementally write". A one-place Google route from the dashboard (run 234) sat at "0/1 places" from 03:33:23 to 03:36:34: its first INSERT waited for the write lock until render run 212 finished at 03:36:34. Render appended each site's plans without committing and then asked Claude about the next site, so from the first site with plans the lock was held through every later Claude call, up to the end of the run (simulated with 5 sites and a 2 s Claude call: a second writer waited 8.0 s before, 0.01 s after). `aptf-media fetch` (run 207, still running) committed every 50 rows while waiting for downloads: its 50-row blocks took 4 s median, 11 s max, back to back, so the lock was nearly always taken. The other steps were measured on a copy of the live database with a second connection inserting every 100 ms (longest wait, before / after): identity rebuild with every link changed (389 links) 0.01 / 0.02 s; estimate compute for 400 places with every trip new (12,400 trips) 0.01 / 0.06 s; reparse of the 13.9 MB Apartments.com raw file (2,126 rows) 0.10 / 0.01 s; extract apply of 3,034 cached Apartments.com readings 0.53 / 0.18 s. The bulk loops were not the cause; they commit every 200 rows now so they stay short as the data grows. Also: the commute leg cache's connection waited only SQLite's default 5 s (a paid leg could lose its cache write to "database is locked"), now 5 minutes; the dashboard job could stay "running" forever when closing its run failed, now it ends with the error as is, and it says when it is waiting for the lock. A CLI `run` rebuilt identity three times for a text source (end of `run_source`, end of the extract apply, the follow-up step); `run_source`, `reparse` and `extract` take `rebuild_identity`, and the CLI leaves it to the follow-up step. Replaces: one transaction per bulk loop, render's single commit at the end, media's commit every 50 rows.
- **2026-11-25: Hovering links the list and the map.** User: "highlight matching dots in the frontend/sidebar when hovering over one or the other". A card under the mouse enlarges its marker (radius 13, 4 px outline in `--accent`, brought to front); on leave the marker gets back the style `drawMarkers` gave it (kept on the marker as `baseStyle`, so status colour and fail dimming return exactly). A marker under the mouse outlines its card (`.card.hl`, accent ring, full opacity even when dimmed) and, after 150 ms, scrolls it smoothly into view only when it is off-screen. Both listen only on devices with `(hover: hover)`, so taps are unchanged; the photo tooltip, click to open and `.sel` are untouched. Replaced: no link between a card and its marker until a click.
- **2026-11-25: The list filters are the selected goal; one mechanism.** User: "and then just replace the filtering with it". The filter panel edits the selected goal: its must-have and nice rules, a draft previewed live on the server (`evaluate_goal` on cached `goal_inputs`, about 0.4 s live), saved for everyone, saved as a new goal, reverted or deleted. The list shows places whose status for that goal is ticked; unknown is needs_check, so 'keep places without a time' is ticking needs check. Removed: `GET|POST /api/view/filters` and 'Save for everyone', the min beds and max $/bed inputs, client-side requirement-row filtering, the 'keep places without a time' box, and the per-browser filter copy; sort, target, mode, status ticks, inactive and outside walk zones are now per-browser view settings only. The old saved set (max $/bed 2,500 and drive to Googleplex 25, Rengstorff 25, Meta HQ 30 min live) is offered once as a new goal `saved_filters`, not merged into lease, so the existing goals do not change until someone edits them. A requirement row becomes a `commute_any` (one option) or `near` rule, so a straight-line estimate near the limit is now needs_check rather than in or out. Card prices and price sorting follow a goal's `term` rule, not the ids temp and lease. Browser check on a copy of the live database: a new goal '3bd near Meta' (beds ≥ 3, price ≤ $5,000/month, drive to Meta HQ ≤ 25 min, gym) gives 1 pass, 33 needs check, 2,581 fail; saving took 6.7 s. Replaced: list filters that sat on top of a goal and were stored separately.
- **2026-11-25: Net commute over a goal's commute rules: total, mean, median, spread; sortable and a rule.** User: "a net commute time, all the constraints added up, and then allow sorting that, so all the roommates minimise net commute time", then "mean/median/sqdiff" too. One term per hard commute rule of the selected goal (a roommate each), read from the same trips the rule checks (fastest option of a `commute_any`, nearest point of a `near` set), computed on the server per place and goal (`filter.place_commute_terms`, in `/api/places` and the draft preview) so the pair is exactly the rule's; combined on the card by the Net selector. Spread is the standard deviation in minutes (the square root of the mean squared deviation), so fairness reads in minutes. Unknown terms never count as 0 (the card says how many; the sort puts them after complete places; the rule returns needs_check). Nice-to-have commute rules (Caltrain, Google bus) are not terms. A `net_commute` failure is set aside by `routing_candidates` like the commute rules (routing can change it), so it does not drop a place from search candidates. Browser check on the live copy, the imported old filters as goal 'Lease + roommates' (three drive rules): sorted by total, first 24.2 min (Los Altos Hills cottage, fails term and $/bed) and 24.3 min (821 Altaire Walk, passes); with `net_commute` total ≤ 60 added: 11 pass, 198 needs check, 2,406 fail. The filter controls now scroll on their own (a long goal squeezed the card list). Replaced: no combined commute view; each commute fact judged alone.
- **2026-11-25: Near-duplicate threshold back to 6 pHash bits; merges above it are re-fetched.** The full run (33,995 URLs) merged 274 photos at 8 to 10 bits; 51 of those merged two photos of the same listing. Of 15 such pairs checked side by side, 2 were different rooms (a kitchen with a different kitchen, d=8 and d=10) and about 4 were different shots of one room; the cross-listing pairs at 0 to 6 and the pilot's pairs were one picture. A listing rarely posts one picture twice, so 10 was too loose. Now `url_files` ignores a merge recorded at more than the current threshold (the URL shows from its source), and the next `aptf-media fetch` downloads it again as a new row. Replaced: 10 bits (same day).
- **2026-11-25: Furnished Finder photos through curl; SVG drawings rasterised.** User: "ok use curl", "handle it". Furnished Finder's image host answered 403 to every httpx request, direct and through Apify's residential proxy, while plain `curl` from the box got 200 for the same files, so the block is on the client, not the IP: hosts in `[media] curl_hosts` download through the curl binary (the residential proxy stays available in `residential_hosts`, now empty). Prometheus floor plans are SVG, which Pillow cannot read (247 'not an image' rows): they are rasterised with cairosvg to the 1,280 px drawing size and stored as JPEG (one format to serve and cache; no SVG sanitising needed). Drawings now dedupe only on identical stored bytes (was: identical pHash). Replaced: Furnished Finder via the residential proxy; SVG drawings not stored.
- **2026-11-25: Listings priced too low per bedroom go to Verify.** User: "flag listings that are too cheap, less than 1k per bedroom, in the Verify conflicts, so they get checked by hand"; the trigger was 425 Oak Ave #A, Redwood City (Zumper, 5bd/3.5ba at $1,600, a room's price). `verify.cheap_queue` flags active monthly lease listings (not short stays, not rooms) below `[verify] min_price_per_bed` (new key, default 1000; studio = 1 bed) as one-listing conflicts, resolved with the existing price check (`current_checks`); no new table or annotation. On a copy of the live database: 47 (craigslist 25, zumper 9, direct 7, padmapper 4, zillow 2), mostly rooms in shared houses that the data calls a house (425 Oak Ave on Craigslist and Zumper, 910 Montage Circle 'Private bedroom for rent (shared house)'), below-market housing (Villa Montgomery, firsthousing.org, 3bd at $1,219), an RV (1 San Antonio Rd #E), and beds misread from '1 BD 1 BA' titles (1072 Terra Bella, 2bd at $1,100). Replaced: no check on implausible prices.
- **2026-11-25: Verify conflicts filtered by goal and walk zone.** User: "allow filtering conflicts by goals, like if I have a price target and all conflicting prices are higher than 2500/bed then there's relatively little point in me solving it ... You can filter by distance too." `verify.goal_relevance` judges a conflict per goal on the existing per-listing goal results (no threshold code per rule type): shown when a hard rule reads a disputed field and no rule fails on every reading; `/api/verify/batch` takes `goal` and `zone=walk` and reports what each filter hid. One distance control (the station walk zones) because the goal filter already applies the goal's commute rules. On a copy of the live database: 341 open conflicts; lease goal 10 shown (275 no lease rule reads the field, mostly Haiku address readings; 56 fail another rule or both values fail), 2 with the walk-zone box; temp goal 6 (258 / 77), 1 in zone; walk zone alone 130. Doubtful by design: an address text conflict never matters to a goal, though a wrong address could move a commute. Replaced: one unfiltered conflict list.
- **2026-11-25: RentCafe is a city source (`rentcafe`), through benthepythondev/rentcafe-apartments-scraper.** User: add RentCafe; its Mountain View search showed 463 rentals and 9 buildings missing from our data (Mari-Court, Tahoe Manor, 2045 and 2065 Winfield, 1939 Latham, 658 Sierra Vista, 1846 California, 2010 San Ramon, Ednamary). Store search found 10 RentCafe actors, all about $1 per 1,000 items. Probes on Mountain View ($0.17 in all): `azzouzana/rentcafe-scraper` returns the raw search cards (`CostLabel` "Base rent", per-bedroom floors `FloorplanBedPrice`, `UnitsAvailable` "6 Units available", the rentcafe.com link) but returned only cards 25 to 49 of the first page, so Mari-Court was missing; `benthepythondev` paged through (Mari-Court at position 14, Tahoe Manor at 35; the search holds 72 properties, the 463 'rentals' are units), and with details gives every floor plan with its id, beds, baths, sqft, rent and an available flag, so offers are per plan, not per bedroom floor. Its gaps: no base-vs-total label, no units-available count (only a per-plan flag and total units), and every link is a rentcafe.com page, so no own-site URL. memo23's `propertyWebsite` is the management company's site (its example: cimgroup.com), not the building's. Price $0.00135 per item on our Bronze tier until 2026-11-28, $0.00081 after. Rows: a plan with a rent is an offer; a property with none is `site_info`. New adapter, no change to other sources. Runs 353 (Mountain View) and 356 (the other 8 cities), real cost $0.49 ($0.10 + $0.39; probes $0.17): 360 items, 751 rows (611 plan offers, 140 `site_info`), 331 places, 196 already known from other sources and 135 new (Mountain View 15, Palo Alto 24, Menlo Park 19, Redwood City 15, Sunnyvale 27, Santa Clara 29, Los Altos 3). All 9 buildings named by the user now exist: 8 as new places, 1939 Latham joined an existing Craigslist place; 7 of the 9 say 'Ask for pricing' on RentCafe too, so they are `site_info`; Tahoe Manor (studio $1,600) and Mari-Court (2bd/1ba $2,895) are offers. Stanford and Atherton: 0 properties. Every Mountain View card read 'Base rent'. `runs.est_cost` undercounts this actor (it logged $0.054 for run 353, Apify charged $0.097): `base.event_cost` prices with the FREE tier of the actor's last pricing entry, here a future one (2026-11-28), while the account is on Bronze; not fixed here.
- **2026-11-25: Airbnb's "monthly" price qualifier is a price per month.** tri_angle labels a stay of 28+ nights `qualifier: "monthly"` in its newer runs (300 raw items) and `"month"` in older ones (797); only `"month"` was mapped, so "monthly" items fell back to `stay` and were divided by 35 nights (Jan 11 to Feb 15), reading about 13% low per month (listing 1550392174052425276: "$6,709 monthly" stored as $5,834 a month). Found while probing other Airbnb actors, which both say "monthly" for the same $6,709. Now "monthly" maps to `month`. Stored rows are not reparsed: 247 live Airbnb rows still carry `stay`; each is corrected when it is next observed, and its `price_month` then rises by about 15%, which can flip a temp result at the $4,000 limit. Replaced: "monthly" read as a total for the dates.
- **2026-11-25: Goal editor number inputs sized per kind; the count line gives totals by status.** User: "all the number textboxes are too small" (beds '3' showed as nothing, minutes '40' as '4', '2250' barely fitted: `box-sizing: border-box` put padding and the spinner inside 3 to 5.5 em), and asked for the count line to show the goal's totals by status. Inputs now take a kind class (`num-bed`, `num-min`, `num-money`, `num-count`) whose width is digits in `ch` plus 30px; the count line replaces 'N of M places' with totals by status, coloured, plus 'showing N' when some are hidden. Also removed, at the user's word ("remove"; goals stay in `goals.toml`): the 'Import old saved filters as a goal' button, its client code and the `legacy_filters` / `legacy_imported` fields of `GET /api/goals` (and their test); the old annotations stay, old localStorage is ignored. Also removed, at the user's word ("don't put these, we have the commutes from requirements and nice to haves"): the list's Target and Mode selects, the single target/mode commute fact on each card and the 'minutes to target' sort (net commute covers it); cards show the goal's commute rules and net commute as before, nothing under All; a new commute rule starts at the default target by bike; `target` / `mode` in a browser's saved view are ignored. The compute control keeps its own point and mode. Replaced: inline em widths per input, 'N of M places', the one-time saved-filters import, and the list's target/mode pair.
- **2026-11-25: Airbnb through `kaix/airbnb-listing-scraper`, a coordinate search per station box.** User: "try a cheaper Airbnb searcher that isn't broken". tri_angle cost $0.004 per item but about $0.045 per new listing inside a box, because its map-box search spills (the Mountain View run returned 137 items, 33 of them inside). Store search: 11 Airbnb search actors compared on price, 30-day runs, reviews and inputs. Probes on the Mountain View box, Jan 11 to Feb 15 ($0.08): `kaix/airbnb-listing-scraper` (coordinates plus `maxLocationDeviation`, $0.0001 per listing on our tier, full listing pages) returned 131 listings, 105 inside the box (80%; it searches a circle reaching the corners), 43 of the 52 known in the box and 62 new, for $0.012; `cirkit/airbnb-search-scraper` (the same map-box URL, $0.0005 per listing, search cards only) returned 132, all inside, 50 of 52 known and 82 new, for $0.066. The two agree on every price they share (105). cirkit was not chosen: its cards carry no description and no amenities, and a room's "1 bath" does not say whether it is shared (kaix: 25 of 30 such rooms share it). A cirkit row would supersede stored rows that have all three, since the newest row wins. kaix's input is the box's centre and half its diagonal; results outside the box are attributed by coordinates, as before (`area_of` reads both actors' coordinate shapes). Parsed: kind from `roomType` (`entire_home` or a room), beds and baths from `sharingTitle`, a room's private bath only when it says "private (attached) bathroom" or "dedicated" (shared: False, else unknown), price `amount` with qualifier "monthly" per month, available for the queried dates when priced (the search returns only stays free on them), description with HTML stripped, amenities without the "Not included" group, up to 8 photos. On the 46 probe listings already stored, beds, baths, kind and private bath match the stored rows exactly. Items of tri_angle still parse (reparse). The box `slug` is no longer used. Airbnb stays a ranked source: kaix found 105 of the 132 listings cirkit showed inside the box. `max_items` 270 (Airbnb's 15 pages of 18). Caveats: kaix has 101 users and 2 reviews (5 stars), with 2,590 runs and 99.9% success in 30 days; its price dates from 2026-10-28. Replaced: tri_angle/airbnb-scraper with a map-box URL and place slug.
- **2026-11-25: Zillow detail pages, read by the text reading; for-sale pages go to Verify.** User approved fetching the detail page of every active Zillow listing (904 pages, about $0.89 at $0.00098 a page) after a 5-page probe ($0.0049, Apify run q1FIQJc3BZcd1FTeO): every page has `description`, `whats_special` and `reso_facts`; a building zpid returns one representative unit's page; a listing held as an active rental can now say FOR_SALE (124743044, 104 Evandale Ave). Built as a raw-only fetch (`aptf-intake zillow-detail`) rather than an adapter through `run_source`: `run_source` turns every item into an observation (904 new listings, geocoding, places) and needs areas; the fetch shares its budget guard (`base.spend_room`, factored out) and logs one targeted run, so it can neither cover an area nor retire a listing. `extract zillow` adds the page as plain lines (no unit beds, price, dates or deposit; furnished only when true) and treats a listing with a page as having text; facts keep their verbatim quotes. No structured amenity mapping: Haiku quotes 'Community features: ... Fitness Center' like any text. Stale listings are Verify items, never deleted or retired. The first 20 pages (run 364) showed why a hand check, not a rule: FOR_SALE can still be for rent (104 Evandale Ave: listed for sale 11-11, for rent 11-23, price change to $6,800 11-24), while OTHER pages were 'Listing removed' the same day (2105 Cornell St, 300 Chiquita Ave); so each item shows the newest price-history event. Replaced: Zillow text = the search card's feature words and fees.
- **2026-11-25: A free precheck before own-site renders and address checks: dead domains and Cloudflare challenges are recorded, not sent to Apify.** User: "check dns + ip http request basic" and "put up a list for cloudflare websites". A test agent found some sites the render recorded as "403 blocked" or "session error" were dead domains (`www.velocityls.com` NXDOMAIN; `www.brewsterplaceapts.com` a CNAME to a CloudFront host that does not resolve; apfapartments.com and shayla alike), so we paid residential retries on them, and others sit behind a Cloudflare challenge ('Just a moment...', barkley-square) that nothing tested gets through. `intake/precheck.py`: per site a DNS lookup and one plain GET from this machine, which the user approved as a basic reachability check (an exception to all listing fetches going through Apify: the page is never stored or parsed). `dead` = EAI_NONAME/EAI_NODATA only; `challenge` = `cf-mitigated: challenge`, or a Cloudflare 403/429/503 with the challenge markers (a Cloudflare bot script on a normal 200 page is not one); everything else proceeds unchanged. Recorded as the render outcome and as the address check; neither `--retry-failed` retries them; rechecked by the next plain run once over 7 days old, or on demand (`render --site`, `aptf-intake precheck`). Verify's own-site list gets the reasons 'site dead (DNS)' and 'Cloudflare challenge' (each URL with its outcome, date and error; the existing 'Add URL' takes a working one), the pipeline's own-site stage both counts. Replaced: every listed site sent to Apify, and a dead domain retried as 'blocked'.
- **2026-11-25: Prometheus own sites read from Prometheus's own availability JSON, directly from the box (`aptf-intake prometheus`); render skips them.** User's decision (a dated, narrow exception to AGENTS.md rule 6): one small request per site to `shopping.prometheusapartments-prod-west2.com/<id>/available-units`, no page scraping, no Apify. A test agent found the endpoint in the pages' own requests and checked it against the renders: The Dean (22 units in 13 plans) and Madera (4 plans) matched the rendered prices and sqft exactly. Renders of these sites gave one row per plan (`<site>:r:plan-2c`) from Haiku's reading at about $0.02 a site; the JSON gives every offered unit with its number, ready date and lease term, free and exact. Rows keep render's source_id scheme and area (`<site>:r:<unit>`, `<site>#render`), so the first read retires the render's plan rows, and Verify's pending-render check, the render outcome list and the pipeline need no second notion of 'rendered'. A separate command rather than folded into `render`: render's code is an Apify-and-Claude pipeline with its own selection and outcome rules, and this reader is a dozen plain requests; keeping them apart leaves render's change to one entry in `SKIP_PLATFORMS`. The community id comes from stored page data first because the box's IP once got a Cloudflare 403 on a Prometheus page; a refused request is recorded as `blocked` for that site and retires nothing. Replaced: Prometheus sites rendered through `apify/playwright-scraper` and read by Haiku.
- **2026-11-25: Prometheus reader: units it cannot read are an error; one request per community.** A non-empty unit list that gives no rows (no unit numbers) was logged as `no_plans`, which retires the site's offers; it is now `error` (state failed), since a changed format is not 'nothing offered' (rule 8). The box's site list holds two entries for Park Place (`park-place`, `park-place-2`, same community id 4500556) and two for Spruce (`applewood-spruce-apartments`, `pepperwood-spruce-apartments`, one URL), so a run asks each community once and gives every entry the same answer. Replaced: `no_plans` for unreadable units; a request per list entry.
- **2026-11-25: The precheck skips only dead domains; a Cloudflare challenge is reported, not skipped; a malformed host is not dead.** The first precheck dry run on the box (207 sites) found 14 'dead' and 8 'challenge'. A cross-check against the last render outcomes: all 8 challenged sites had rendered through Apify's browser at HTTP 200 on 11-25 (barkley-square-apartments, midtown-apartments, the-laurel-at-mountain-view-55-2, jefferson-apartments-prodesse, palo-alto-place: ok; park-plaza, mission-hills-apartments, the-californian-apartments: no_plans; barkley-square has 5 priced rows), so a challenge to the box's plain GET does not predict a render failure and skipping them would have stopped working sites. 10 of the 14 'dead' were URLs with spaces for dots, all named by Zumper listings ('https://www heritagerealtysanmateo com/'): bad data, not dead domains; they now proceed with the error 'malformed host'. So `PRECHECK_SKIP` = {dead}; the own-site list has no 'Cloudflare challenge' reason until the user says what it should hold. Replaced: the same day's skip of challenged sites.
- **2026-11-25: Who may rent: Haiku tags income, senior, veterans, student, employer and other limits; an `equals restriction none` rule filters them.** User: tag every listing and place with housing restrictions so goals can drop them (RentCafe 'Alta Torre' 1 bd $880, 'PBV - Belovida (VASH)', 'The Laurel (55+)'; text such as 'BMR', 'income limits apply', 'senior community 62+', 'students only'). Built on the existing reading rather than a second pipeline: schema v3 adds `restriction {value, evidence}` (a limit needs its verbatim quote; 'none' needs none; accepting Section 8 is none), and every source is now read when it has a name or title, so name-only rows (RentCafe buildings, own-site plans, Google Places and portfolio buildings) are swept too. The brief proposed RentCafe's `vertical = 'housing'` as a deterministic income signal; the live data says otherwise: of the 19 active /housing/ rows, 6tenEast ($3,841 to $7,267, a luxury description) and single houses (1007 Bryant Way, $4,795) are market rate, so a /housing/ row with nothing stated is unknown (needs_check), not income and not none. Alta Torre states nothing anywhere we store (no description, name, amenities), so it is needs_check, never a guess from its price. A limit is pooled per place like amenities, because the building row often carries it ('PBV - Belovida (VASH)' is `site_info`; the priced plan at the same place is 'Belovida Santa Clara, L.P.'). The rule is the existing `equals` type (field `restriction`, 'none' first in the editor), so no new rule type; unread = needs_check (rule 5). The example goals carry it commented out, since a database without readings would turn every listing needs_check. Bumping the schema re-reads every listing once (cached by text afterwards). Known side effect, as for every reading: an extract observation counts as the listing's `last_seen`, and a render or Prometheus run (which do not call the after-intake hook) supersedes an own-site row's reading until the next `aptf-intake extract direct` (cached, free). Replaced: no notion of eligibility; text sources only.
- **2026-11-25: An Airbnb box run is complete when its search ended on its own; a capped search is split into quadrants.** User: "airbnb should handle it" (the 8 Airbnb boxes always showed partial, because Airbnb was a ranked source whose box runs never counted as complete). Rule: kaix's log shows each search page; a search whose last page had fewer than 18 results and that stayed under `max_items` (270) ran out of results, and the box is complete. Otherwise the box is split into 4 quadrants, recursively to depth 2, and the merged results are attributed by coordinates as before; a quadrant still capped at depth 2 leaves the box partial. The item count alone was not used: kaix drops results beyond its radius after paging (run 361: 200 found over 12 pages, 128 accepted), so a capped search can return far fewer than 270. The ranked override now applies to tri_angle runs only (`RANKED_ACTORS`; its run 61 found 33 of 51 known), and legacy Airbnb box runs stay partial; stored kaix runs 361 and 366 stay partial (recorded so), so the boxes are run again under the rule. Caveat: kaix's probe found 105 of the 132 listings cirkit showed inside the Mountain View box, so 'complete' means the search ended, not that every listing was seen; liveness keeps excluding Airbnb. Replaced: Airbnb box runs never complete (2026-11-24).
- **2026-11-25: A website written with spaces for dots is joined, any other host with a space is refused; the latest `[[found]]` row of a site id wins.** Ten own sites promoted from Zumper listings had URLs like `https://www heritagerealtysanmateo com/` and `https://WWW CMPMI COM/`. Cause: not Zumper's website field (`detail_entity_data_homepage` is empty on these items) and not the Zumper parser, but the landlord's description text, which writes the link that way ("For APPLICATIONS and OTHER AVAILABLE PROPERTIES please visit WWW CMPMI COM"); `extract` (Haiku) returned it verbatim, the verbatim check passed, and `clean_url` let a host with spaces through. `clean_url` now joins a host that is only plain labels (`[a-z0-9-]`) separated by spaces and ending in a known TLD (`SPACED_TLDS`: com, net, org, us, io, co, apartments, ...), lowercased; any other host with whitespace, or no host (`http gofivestarpm com rentals`, `Stayluxlife dot com`, `sfcityrents . com`, `www srecompany com availability`), is refused as `malformed host`. Every reader of a website goes through `clean_url` (extract, Places, Zumper, RentCafe, promote), so the fix is in one place. Stored readings were corrected with `extract zumper --reread` (cached answers reapplied, free; the scraped rows were never wrong, so no reparse). `discovered_sites.toml` stays append-only: the corrected URL is appended as a new `[[found]]` row under the same id, and `config.latest_found` (used by `load_sites` and `discover._records`) keeps the latest row per id, as `last_checks` does for checks. Replaced: a spaced host stored as a website and promoted as an own site; the first `[[found]]` row of an id winning.
- **2026-11-25: One site, one entry: duplicate site-list entries are collapsed and their rows retired (`config.duplicate_sites`).** User: "fix it forever", not by hand-editing config. After the Prometheus reader ran, `park-place` (hand-listed) and `park-place-2` (discovered, the same URL with a trailing slash; community 4500556) wrote 21 units twice, and `applewood-spruce-apartments`, `pepperwood-spruce-apartments` and `655-south-fair-oaks-avenue` (one URL, community 3323021) wrote 25 units three times; all on one place, so the duplicate-buildings list (which pairs places) never showed it. The box's list had more of the same among rendered sites (three entries for The Montclaire's Essex page, four for Central Park's Greystar page, two each for Novo, Aviana, Ladera, Middle Park and others) and a few operator homepages shared by several buildings (`ourmountainviewhome.com`, `heritagerealtysanmateo.com`, `caprop.com`): the same URL gives the same page, so one entry reads it and only the canonical entry's place keeps those rows. Rule: same `site_key` (host + path), or same Prometheus community id; canonical = first in load order (hand before discovered: the earliest entry, and a hand entry's name, rather than alphabetical, which would have named Spruce '655-south-fair-oaks-avenue'). Duplicates are marked in a run of their own before any fetch, so a fetch run that ends in error cannot leave a duplicate marked but unretired. `load_sites` dropped a discovered entry with a hand entry's URL (`rstrip('/')`) silently, which never retired its rows; that case is now a marked duplicate. Replaced: the reader's per-run 'one request per community id' (each entry still got its own rows); the silent same-URL drop in `load_sites`; discover's id-only `taken` check.
- **2026-11-25: The same-site key keeps a URL's query.** The first `site_key` was host + path only. 12 `[[found]]` URLs on the box are redirect links whose query names the building (`lease-apts.com/?...` twice, `leaseaptsnow.com`, `apts-rental.com`, `rentalsapts.com`): two unlanded links on one redirect host would have been one site, and the second building would never get its own. Stored site URLs have no query (`clean_url` drops it; a landed link uses where it landed), so this changes no other group: the box still has the same 26 duplicates. A duplicate's render outcome is never shown as stale. Replaced: host + path only.
- **2026-11-25: "Route with Google" shows its result at once and never falls back to estimates silently.** User: the button stayed on 'Routing…' and the table kept only '~' estimates (Meta HQ drive ~27, walk ~185.8). Cause: the routing worked (20 Google calls, 26 trips: Meta HQ drive 17.0, walk 249.9), but trips count as scraped rows in the dashboard cache key (2026-11-25 decision above), so the drawer's reload reused `best_trips` for up to 120 s and redrew the old estimates. Now the endpoint clears the cache after routing (the user's own action, like an edit), defaults to Google and answers 400 when the key is missing (it used to route by estimate and report success), rejects unknown providers, and the drawer keeps the outcome ('Routed with Google: 26 new trips, 20 Google calls', 'no changes', or the error) under the button and always leaves 'Routing…'. Replaced: a silent estimate fallback, a toast only, and a cached reload.
- **2026-11-25: Cheaper Apify actors: researched, tabled.** User asked where the Apify credit goes and whether cheaper actors would do. Research (real `usageTotalUsd` per actor this cycle; probes cost $0.155) is summarised under Still open, 'Known cheaper Apify actors'. Findings: (1) `apify/rag-web-browser` read the plans we hold on 6/6 own-site pages that need residential proxy (Essex Arlo studio from $4,107; Irvine Cherry Orchard unit-level 1bd $4,210 available 12/11/2026; Equity Briarwood 1bd $3,116+, 2bd $3,610+; Central Park Carnegie $3,571.40; Laurel Grove $4,595/$5,595; Ladera S2 $3,949, A1 $4,429) at ~$0.0025 a page against ~$0.029 with playwright plus residential proxy, but takes one URL per run, has no page function or clicks, leaves image alt text empty and failed on the Essex city page; (2) the portfolio run costs $0.70 to 0.83 a time for 5 new listings last run, so it can run less often; (3) `lergassy/craigslist-scraper` is 30% cheaper per item and matched 60/60 held items on 94306 at 1.5 mi (e.g. post 8001688458 "Spacious 1br in Barron Park" $2,995, 725 sqft), but needs parser renames; 47% of Craigslist items bought are listings already held and overlapping 1.5-mile ZIP circles add ~10% in-run duplicates; (4) `igolaizola/zillow-scraper-ppe` (~49% cheaper) could not be probed (needs a $0.50 minimum charge cap); (5) the other sources are already at floor price; `get_anything/web-unblocker` rejected (429, stripped plan lists, billed past its cap). Rule-8 flag: solidcode runs 153 and 154 (same second, both 94306, both 'complete') returned 45 and 119 items. Known bug, not fixed: `runs.est_cost` prices with the FREE tier of the actor's last pricing entry (possibly a future price) while the account is BRONZE, so it is off both ways (real vs estimate: Craigslist $6.94 vs $7.60, RentCafe $0.62 vs $0.25, Zillow $1.61 vs $1.43 missing the $0.05 start fee, Playwright $7.52 vs ~$6.8), and the reserve guard relies on it. Decision: the user tabled all switches; revisit when the credit is topped up. Replaced: nothing (current actors kept).
- **2026-11-25: Self-hosted OSRM routes drive, bike and walk for every place; transit is not shown.** User: "just do this" after a comparison of routing options ("it's fine, we don't need traffic"; "don't do transit", "keep transit as bike+train+bike, the algo already works"; "hide the est. transit time"). OSRM over Valhalla: both are in nixpkgs (osrm-backend 26.4.0, valhalla 3.9.1), OSRM's build is three stock commands per profile and its HTTP API is one GET per leg; Valhalla serves all modes from one process but needs a generated config and tile build. Box: 31 GB RAM, 54 GB disk free, 16 threads; the clipped Bay Area graphs build in 5.5 minutes including the download. `OsrmLegSource` (provider `osrm`) is a real route: it decides commute rules like Google (no estimate band); drive is free-flow (no traffic; Google's drive was traffic-unaware too). bike+train is built from OSRM bike legs (place to station, station to target), with the train table as before. OSRM has no transit, so `transit` and `bike_bus_bike` are left to the other providers. Free providers route every place (no prefilter, `max_km` or `auto`), OSRM after every intake run while the service is up, and from the dashboard (drawer 'Route (free)', Pipeline compute provider 'OSRM', the default when it is up; new Pipeline stage 'Commute routes (OSRM)'). Google stays selectable and is preferred where it exists. Transit is no longer offered or shown: the dashboard's mode list (`MODES`: drawer matrix, Pipeline compute, goal editor) drops it and cards skip a goal's transit option; stored Google transit trips stay in the log, and a goal that names transit still validates and still reads them (the example temp goal's `transit <= 25` option is left for the user to remove). Replaced: straight-line estimates as the only free provider; Google or estimates for station -> target anchors; transit as a dashboard mode.
- **2026-11-25: Commute times are coloured by source.** User: "put (google maps) source on time estimates that used it, just like you put est on ones that don't", then: colour each time by source with a tooltip naming it instead of text labels. Google Maps blue, OSRM violet, straight-line estimate muted grey with the existing '~' (theme tokens `--src-google`, `--src-osrm`, light and dark; chosen away from pass green, check amber and fail red). The drawer matrix, the nearest-points list and the cards' commute facts use it; a card fact that fails or needs a check keeps its red or amber (the failing-fact style wins), and the source is still on hover. Rule reasons stay text ('45 min (estimate)'). Replaced: '~' / '(est.)' for estimates only, the provider id in the cell's hover text.
- **2026-11-25: No 'outside walk zones' box on the list.** User: "this is duplicating caltrain walk zone". The checkbox hid places outside the 1.54 km straight-line radius around the 8 station boxes (`in_zone`), while the goal already has a `near` rule (nearest Caltrain, walk ≤ 25 min) that decides the same thing from walk minutes, with unknown as needs check. The list, the count line's '· showing N' and the map dots now follow only the goal's status ticks, search and the inactive box. `in_zone` and the walk zones stay for intake coverage, routing candidates (`compute` scope zone / candidates), the own-site list, Verify's zone filter and the map's station circles. A saved `outzone` key in `aptf.view` is ignored. Replaced: the per-browser 'outside walk zones' view checkbox.
- **2026-11-25: Who may rent: a minimum income and house rules are not limits; a long quote may touch a glued word.** The first full sweep (runs 405 to 425, every active listing, 575 Haiku calls) tagged 130 Craigslist rows income, 19 Airbnb rows other, 12 employer, 15+ senior. Reading every distinct quote: 'Income Requirements: 3 X Rent!' (Oakbrook, Villa Del Sol, Carlyle) and Zillow's 'gross income at least three (3) times the amount of rent' are ordinary screening, not an income cap; Airbnb's 'Guests must be 21 years or older', 'only fully vaccinated guests', 'no children under 6', 'no prior Airbnb reviews' are booking rules. The field description now names both as none (a maximum income, AMI limits, tax credit and BMR stay income; one gender only stays other). Only the 215 listings tagged by the first sweep were re-asked (a 'none' cannot become a limit under a stricter description): Oakbrook, Carlyle, the booking rules and 11 Craigslist rows became none; one Villa Del Sol row still reads '3X's Rent' as income and Alexander Station's 'Here are your income limits' stays income (Haiku's reading, left as is). The 126 listings whose answer had no usable restriction were re-asked too: 93 became none; The Acacia's 32 Craigslist rows (teachers-only: 'At least one member of your household is a current employee of Palo Alto Unified') stayed unknown because the scraped text reads 'Educator HousingAt least ...', so the whole-word check failed; a restriction quote of 20+ normalized characters now may touch a glued word. Replaced: the first description; whole words only.
- **2026-11-25: A broken image library stops `aptf-media fetch`; the dev shell loads the wheels' native libraries on NixOS.** Media run 400 was started on the box with `nix develop -c uv run aptf-media fetch`: numpy (needed by imagehash) failed to import ('libstdc++.so.6: cannot open shared object file', NixOS has no FHS libraries for uv's PyPI wheels; cairocffi likewise found no libcairo), and `_download` caught every exception as 'not an image', so 679 URLs got HTTP 200 'not an image: ImportError' rows (4 more 'imagehash has no attribute phash', a second thread seeing the half-imported module) and the run was left `running` when killed. `nix run .#media` was fine (Nix-built libraries). Fix: the dev shell sets `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` to libstdc++, zlib and cairo when `/etc/NIXOS` exists (not on other hosts: there a venv on the host Python has them, and Nix's libstdc++ needs a newer glibc than Ubuntu's); `fetch` first runs `check_decoders` (exit with the error, nothing written), and an `ImportError` inside a download re-raises and aborts the run. The 679 URLs were already stored by `--retry-failed` runs 402 and 426; run 400 was closed as aborted. Replaced: every decode exception recorded per URL as 'not an image'.
- **2026-11-25: Process: agents commit from a private git index; never reset, stash or rewrite.** Several agents worked in one working tree and committed through the shared index, so a commit staged from an index (or HEAD files) read before another agent's commit landed silently reverted that commit. Seen five times on 11-24/25: `b4ce1ae` swept another agent's uncommitted `media` table hunk into a store fix (rebuilt as `b454023`; the original kept on branch `backup-b4ce1ae`); `cad247c` reverted `ce5ce8a`'s goal dropdown (restored in `84cac92`); `f666f25` reverted `5925752`'s Prometheus reader (restored in `1ac1db5`); `4a40021` deleted `zillow_detail.py` and reverted `d6210bf`/`5ecea27` (restored in `8a07495`); `5ecea27` reverted the Airbnb switch to kaix (restored in `aaf4cd7`). Each was fixed forward with a restore commit, never by rewriting history. Rule: each agent commits from its own `GIT_INDEX_FILE` built with `git read-tree HEAD`, stages only its own hunks (for a file others are editing: HEAD's version plus its own additions, via `git hash-object -w` and `update-index --cacheinfo`), checks `git diff --cached --stat`, and never runs `reset`, `stash` or a history rewrite. Replaced: committing through the shared index.
- **2026-11-25: Process: kill processes by pid only, never by pattern.** `pkill -f "aptf-media fetch"` run over ssh matched its own ssh shell (whose command line contains the pattern) and killed it; an earlier `pkill -f` killed the calling shell the same way. Rule: note the pid of what you start and kill that pid; never kill processes you did not start. Replaced: pattern kills.
- **2026-11-25: Process: a long ssh call that times out orphans its Apify run.** A render batch run over ssh outlived the tool's timeout: the ssh session ended, our side lost the run, and the Apify actor run kept going (and billing) with nobody reading its result; runs 400 and 409 were left `running` in `runs` (400 was the media run, since closed as aborted). Lesson: a long box job should not hang on a foreground ssh call. Not built: an orphan sweep that closes stale `running` rows or aborts their Apify runs. Replaced: long jobs held on a foreground ssh call.
- **2026-11-25: Bug (not fixed): `discover-sites` ends a budget-capped run as BLOCKED.** `--max-usd` defaults to 0.5 (notional Claude spend). Each call gets `--max-budget-usd min(1.0, max_usd - spent + 0.05)`, so once the spend is near the cap the last call has about $0.05, Claude stops mid-search without JSON, and `ask` raises `Blocker` ('claude gave no JSON'): the command exits as an error instead of stopping cleanly at the cap. The rows already appended are kept. On the box most of the queue was done when it hit this; 39 buildings were left, to be run again with `--max-usd 2`. Fix when touched: stop before a call that cannot get a useful budget, and report 'cap reached' as a normal end.
- **2026-11-25: Correction: RentCafe addresses not geocoded are skipped by design, not by the monthly cap.** An agent reported 50 RentCafe addresses 'un-geocoded because the monthly cap was reached'. Wrong: the box had used 2,339 of the 4,900 monthly geocoding calls with 0 queued. `geocode()` only sends addresses with a single leading house number (`_lead_number`); those 50 have none, e.g. '603, 627, … San Juan Drive' (a property listing several buildings), so they keep the parser's reading. No change.
- **2026-11-25: Follow-up to 'Listings priced too low per bedroom': studios get no special case.** Asked whether studios are over-flagged: a studio already counts as 1 bedroom, so a $1,600 studio (Tahoe Manor) is above $1,000 per bed and not flagged. Decided: no studio rule.

The `api_calls` log showed the first click had routed (20 Google calls, 26 trips); the dashboard cache (`SCRAPE_REFRESH_S`, 120 s) served the old estimates. Lesson: check the call log and trips table before blaming the key.
- **2026-11-25: Apify reserve lowered to $2.00 on the box; render and media do not check it.** The box's `spend.reserve_usd` was $3.70 (the example config's is $0.30); the account's credit was $3.699, below the reserve, so no guarded run could start, so the user lowered it to $2.00 on 11-25. The guard (`base.spend_room`) runs only in `run_source` and `zillow-detail`: `render`, `promote-sites --browser` and media's residential downloads cap only their own `--max-usd`, which is how an earlier $4 round overspent. Not changed yet.

Three buildings were split across two places and were merged with the drawer's merge: Courtyard (1305 and 1309 Marshall St), Middle Park (1026 and 1028 Middle Ave) and Central Park at Whisman (the same 15 units at the same rents under two site entries). - **2026-11-25: A failed operator page makes only its city partial.** Every portfolio run over the 8 cities ended with all 8 partial: `portfolio.fetch` set the run-wide `truncated` flag when any page failed, and `run_source` then marked every area partial. Run 320 had one failed page (Irvine Company / Stanford, not returned) and run 175 four (Essex for Los Altos, Stanford and Santa Clara, Irvine Company for Sunnyvale); the other pages were fine or 404 (no community there, not a failure). Now an adapter may name the areas its items say are incomplete (`Adapter.area_failures`, portfolio: `failed_cities`, a city with a blocked, not returned or unread page); `run_source` marks only those partial, like Places' `truncated_areas`. `reparse --covers auto` applies the same rule to a saved raw file ($0, no actor, Haiku's answers are in the raw items). On a copy of the live database from before 06:21 it took the Pipeline's intake line from '95 done, 10 partial' to '102 done, 3 partial' (craigslist 94036 and 95089, portfolio Stanford). It was not run on the live database: per-city portfolio runs (437 onwards) had already re-fetched the cities, and a reparse re-appends the file's rows and claims every area in the file, so it must never be used on a file older than an area's latest run. Replaced: one failed page making the whole run partial.
- **2026-11-25: Commute times: KNN ranges from Google-routed neighbours, Google sampled through Route Matrix; the self-hosted OSRM router is dropped.** User: "honestly let's do knn" after a day of routing research ("log this research in design.md"). Research (leave-one-out on the box's stored Google trips unless noted): the straight-line estimate is far off (drive to Meta HQ median 4.9 / p90 17.8 min; bike to Downtown Palo Alto 9.8 / 14.2). KNN-3 without a distance limit: drive to Meta HQ 0.6 / 1.6 min with 166 routed places, 1.4 / 3.4 with about 19 (nearest neighbour 0.8 km); bike to Palo Alto 0.5 / 2.7, with about 20 routed 1.4 / 5.1; transit 2.2 / 8.4. Limited to 400 m: the true time lies outside the neighbours' min-max range 35-43% of the time, but by at most 0.7 min at p90, and the range is about 1 min wide (median), so the radius decides coverage, not accuracy; within 100 m misses are at most 0.3 min. Coverage with today's 166 Meta-drive trips: 73% of routed places have a routed neighbour within 400 m, 28% have three; bike 99% / 86%. Clustering counts over 1,165 candidates: 939 clusters at 50 m, 694 at 100 m, 317 at 250 m; occupied grid cells 898 at 100 m, 579 at 200 m, 289 at 400 m. Greedy cover picks (main thread): candidates 1,179 need 132 routed places for 400 m (72 given the existing Meta drive trips), 236 at 250 m (163), 639 at 100 m (558); walk zones 1,302 need 134 / 241 / 668. Barrier-aware clustering (never cluster across US-101, CA-85, CA-237, I-280, Central Expressway or the Caltrain tracks) was considered and dropped: "reinventing a routing engine". A self-hosted OSRM (nixpkgs osrm-backend 26.4.0, Bay Area clip of Geofabrik NorCal, 5.5 min build, 4 GB RAM peak, 5 GB disk) was built, compared and dropped: against 515 Google legs, drive median -1.5 / p90 3.0 min (free-flow), bike +7.8 min slower (OSRM 1.31x Google, routes 7% shorter: a speed gap), walk -4.5 (0.86x); per-mode factors would have been needed. The user chose KNN from Google instead and needs no traffic. Google pricing (read 2026-11-25, the user's billing table): Compute Route Matrix Essentials is its own SKU, billed per element (origin x destination), 10,000 free a month, then $5 per 1,000, separate from Compute Routes Essentials' 10,000; Pro (5,000 free, $10) is traffic-aware routing; Enterprise (1,000 free, $15) is `TWO_WHEELER` (motorized), not `BICYCLE`, so the 900 bike-call cap is probably unnecessary (kept until the billing line confirms). Google's terms are unclear about storing route results (the Routes policy allows place IDs indefinitely and coordinates for 30 days, says nothing explicit about durations, and lists 'directions, distance matrix results' as not to bulk download); the user accepted the KNN-from-Google approach knowing this. Decisions: `filter.knn`: a place without its own Google trip for a checked cell reads up to K = 3 Google-routed places within RADIUS_M = 400 m (1 or 2 when that is all there is; none: the straight-line estimate stays); derived at read time in `best_trips`, nothing stored. A rule pads the range by KNN_PAD_MIN = 0.5 min each side: pass when max + 0.5 <= limit, fail only when KNN_FAIL_MARGIN (0.9) x (min - 0.5) > limit, otherwise needs_check with 'N nearby Google routes span a-b min' (on a boundary, keep more places). The Pipeline panel's 'Compute an attribute' becomes a grid of checkboxes (rows: commute targets and point sets; columns: drive, bike, walk, bike+train for targets; sets with `paid = false` disabled), saved as an annotations row (`dashboard`/`knn`/`cells`, latest wins, shared), plus sampling over a scope (current search results by default, walk zones, candidates): cover within 100 / 250 / 400 m, random %, or refine; the dry run shows per cell places, uncached matrix elements and places with a routed place within 400 m before -> after, and a run over 20 elements needs the elements and the elements left this month typed. Sampling uses Compute Route Matrix (`matrix_monthly_element_cap`, 9,000 suggested; no cap set: no matrix calls); the drawer's Route with Google stays computeRoutes. Transit is not shown (2026-11-25 above); bike+train stays the transit option, built from bike legs. Removed: the `osrm` provider, flake app, script, systemd unit, Pipeline stage and the drawer's 'Route (free)' button (the box's service is disabled and its data deleted); OSRM's violet is now the KNN colour. Replaced: OSRM routes for every place (same day); a single (point, mode) compute with a provider choice in the Pipeline panel.
- **2026-11-25: A Zillow building's bedroom rows no longer get the representative unit's furnished, laundry, appliances and cooling.** User reported: 167 Zillow listings read furnished = true, 42 of them building rows (`<zpid>:Nbd`), 84 quotes just the page flag 'Furnished: yes'. Cause: a building zpid's detail page is one representative unit's, and `detail_lines` sent its unit-level `reso_facts` to every bedroom row. Tried first to find a building marker on the page itself: none (all 904 pages have `page_type` 'property'; `home_type` APARTMENT covers 455 single rows and 212 building pages; no units group in the item), so a row is a building row when its source_id has a ':' (`lease.py` writes `<zpid>:Nbd` and `<zpid>:studio`; a building can have one bedroom row, so 'several rows' is not the test). Tally of the 212 building pages on the box: furnished true 25 / false 188; laundry In Unit 109, Shared 45; appliances Washer 128, Dryer 117, Refrigerator 111; cooling Air Conditioner 103, Central Air 38; parking Covered 84, Garage 53, Parking Lot 27 (building policy, kept); utilities mostly empty, 'Cable Connected' 27, Garbage 18, Water 17 (building-level, kept). Dropped on building rows: furnished, laundry (a 'Shared' laundry line is lost too: unknown, never a rejection), appliances, cooling; kept: description, what's special, fees, pets, parking, community features, amenities, pool, utilities, lease term. A description that itself says the units are furnished is still read, with its quote. Single-unit pages (houses, condos, one row per zpid) keep every line. No `VERSION` bump: only the building rows' text changes, so only they miss the cache. `extract --id-like` added so the reread touches only those rows (`--reread --id-like '%:%'`) instead of appending ~690 unchanged single-unit observations. Replaced: the same lines for every row of a zpid. Box run (snapshot `data/backups/apartments-2026-11-25-pre-zillow-building-reread.db`; `extract zillow --reread --id-like '%:%'`, dry run 432 rows, 213 texts, 185 asked, 19 Haiku calls): active building rows furnished true 45 → 28, unknown 387 → 404 (none false either side); single-unit rows unchanged (true 125, false 35, unknown 531). All 17 that dropped were the page flag or a hedged line: 350 Sharon Park Dr, Menlo Park (3 rows, 'Furnished: yes', description empty) and 300 W Washington Ave, Sunnyvale ('Furnished: yes') now unknown; 575 S Rengstorff Ave ('Furnished apartments available.') and 2240 Calle De Luna ('Our furnished apartments ... for stays of 30 days or longer') now unknown too. The 28 left all quote the description: 837 Cowper St ('Furnished: yes' → 'cozy University South furnished two bedroom apartment'), 605 E Evelyn Ave ('Furnished: yes' → 'furnished two bedroom apartment'), 753 Alma St ('comes fully equipped with a bed, dresser, table and chairs'). Haiku is not consistent on 'Furnished apartments available.': 1984 W El Camino Real kept true on the same sentence. No goal status of any Zillow listing changed (temp, lease, uh): the building rows that lost furnished already failed the temp goal on other rules (all 1,123 Zillow listings fail it, before and after).
- **2026-11-25: Bike routing bills as Essentials; per-mode bike cap removed.** Google's docs (developers.google.com/maps/documentation/routes/usage-and-billing, the Routes SKU details and `RouteTravelMode`, read 2026-11-25): Essentials is "requests that use only basic features with a maximum of 10 intermediate waypoints"; Pro is triggered by `TRAFFIC_AWARE` or `TRAFFIC_AWARE_OPTIMAL`, 11 to 25 waypoints, `optimizeWaypointOrder`, side-of-road, heading or vehicle stopover; Enterprise by two-wheeled vehicle routing, tolls, traffic on polylines or large-vehicle routing. `RouteTravelMode` defines `TWO_WHEELER` as motorized ("e.g. motorcycle") and says it differs from `BICYCLE`, which covers the human-powered mode, so `BICYCLE` uses no Enterprise feature. Our computeRoutes and computeRouteMatrix bodies use `TRAFFIC_UNAWARE` for drive and send `departureTime` only for transit (usage-and-billing also says real-time traffic through a departure time of now bills higher; transit is hidden now), no extra waypoints and no Pro option, so every mode we send, bike included, bills as Essentials. Free tiers a month: Compute Routes Essentials 10,000, Compute Route Matrix Essentials 10,000 elements; Pro 5,000 each; Enterprise 1,000 each. So bike shares the Essentials pool. The 900 bike-call cap (`monthly_cap_by_mode = { bike = 900 }`, set 2026-11-24 while the SKU was unclear) was removed on the box when bike reached 895 of 900; `config.example/google.toml` now has `monthly_cap_by_mode = {}`. The total caps stay: 5,000 computeRoutes calls and 9,000 matrix elements a month. Also from the SKU page: `WALK` and `BICYCLE` routing are beta and may miss paths. Replaced: the 900 bike cap and the open 'BICYCLE SKU unconfirmed' item (2026-11-24 'Google Routes metering'; 2026-11-25 KNN entry's 'kept until the billing line confirms').
- **2026-11-25: The KNN grid explains what each cell bills.** User: "explain here what caltrain / gbus bills as, does it estimate the nearest two or what". Each row label carries a muted line and tooltip built from `point_sets.toml`: a commute target '1 route per place per mode'; a set 'routes to the k nearest by straight line; shows the fastest — k routes per place' (k = 1: the nearest only; `paid = false`: estimate only, no Google routes). Bike+train (checked against `_bike_train_bike`): bike to the `K_STATIONS` = 2 stations nearest the place + bike the whole way = up to 3 routes per place, train time from the timetable plus wait, station→destination bike legs shared by every place; `K_STATIONS` is now a named constant in `trips.py` passed to `Commute` (was the engine default). A line under the grid: Route Matrix Essentials, 1 element = 1 place × 1 destination, free tier and cap from `google.toml`. The dry run's places column reads 'places × routes per place = nominal' and the elements column says how many are cached or shared. Replaced: an unexplained grid.
- **2026-11-25: A 'How this works' dialog for the commute grid.** User asked for the explanation in a popup by the panel title. Checked against the code before writing it: random % is one draw over all places in the scope, shared by every cell, and a pick already routed for a cell is dropped for that cell (`sampling.random_plan`); the dry run's 'N not yet routed, over all cells' is a sum over cells, so a place counts once per cell; the typed confirm applies over 20 elements only while fewer than 5,000 are left; and bike+train's whole-way leg is not billed twice when plain bike is also ticked for the same destination: `route_plan` prefetches pair by pair through the leg cache and the dry-run total counts a shared leg once (only the per-cell rows both show it), so the tip says that instead of 'routes it twice'. Replaced: no help beyond the grid's notes.
- **2026-11-25: The Apify guard reads the real credit before every actor run, on every paid path; real charges are recorded.** Problem: the guard (`base.spend_room`) ran only in `run_source` and `zillow-detail`; `render`, `promote-sites --browser` (incl. residential), portfolio's second (residential) run and media's proxy downloads checked no reserve (the earlier $4 round overspent), and `runs.est_cost` was our own estimate (charged events x the FREE tier of the actor's last pricing entry; the account is STARTER, tier BRONZE), which misses actor start fees, platform compute and residential proxy and is wrong both ways. Real (`usageTotalUsd`) vs estimate this cycle: Craigslist $6.94 vs $7.60, RentCafe $0.62 vs $0.25, Zillow search $1.61 vs $1.43, playwright-scraper $7.52 vs ~$6.8. This round: the credit fell $0.90 while our logs showed ~$0.11. Real charges since 05:45 UTC per Apify run (`usageTotalUsd`): apify/playwright-scraper $1.065 (18 runs), delicious_zebu/zillow-property-details-scraper $0.276 (2), kaix/airbnb-listing-scraper $0.087 (8), get_anything/web-unblocker $0.076 (1, aborted), lergassy/craigslist-scraper $0.063 (1), apify/rag-web-browser $0.017 (7) = $1.585. Checked while building: (1) `/users/me/limits` read limit $22, usage $19.2005, so $2.80 left, which matches the ~$2.80 the coordinator reported (the console itself was not read), so the guard measures against `maxMonthlyUsageUsd` (the plan's own credit is $19, already used up; the $2.80 is headroom to the hard limit). (2) `usageTotalUsd` now includes pay-per-event charges (Zillow detail run: $0.130 for 134 items + 1 start, empty `usageUsd`; Craigslist: $0.063 for 60 `listing` events), so the 2026-11-24 finding ($0.0001 for 18 events) no longer holds and `event_cost` is only the estimate. (3) Residential proxy is billed after the run ends: box portfolio runs 464 and 467 stored $0.0341 and $0.0142 (`usageTotalUsd` at finish), but their Apify runs total $0.142 and $0.141 now (EKI7AVXhz0kV4P5Y8 $0.009 + fwqVMqrVsm7vsP9XN $0.133, of which $0.120 residential proxy; zQygipdbPXWCOkq6a $0.006 + 2kQkyrXvMlYNWsx2g $0.135). So a recorded `apify_usd` can be short for proxied runs, and the credit the guard reads may lag the same way; the reserve has to cover that lag. It also means the poll-time abort cannot stop a residential run (the proxy share is not visible while it runs), so residential browser runs get an up-front estimate check (pages x $0.029 against the room) and each Apify run's charge is re-read just before our run closes. No probe was run to time the lag (no paid runs for this change). (4) `apify/playwright-scraper` has no pricing model (platform-billed), so `maxTotalChargeUsd` never capped it: $1.065 of the round's $1.585. Fix: the guard moved into `apify.run_actor` (credit read before each run; cap cut to credit - reserve; refused under $0.02 or when the credit is unknown, where `spend_room` used to let an unknown credit through); `run_actor` aborts a run whose `usageTotalUsd` reaches its cap; media checks the guard before using the proxy; each run's real charge goes in `counts.apify_usd` / `counts.apify_runs` next to the estimate in `est_cost`; the dashboard and pipeline panel show the live credit and the reserve, and the Apify-reported charges; the dashboard's credit read tries once (no backoff, so an Apify outage cannot hang `/api/status`); `aptf-intake credit` is the dry check. Scripts (`scripts/actor_trial.py`, `render_trial.py`) also start actors only through `run_actor`. Orphan `running` rows on the box (217, 236, 239) are not touched here. Runs 400 (media) and 409 (render) were already closed on the box (`aborted`, `ok`). Replaced: the guard in two callers only; our estimate as the only recorded spend; 'usageTotalUsd omits pay-per-event charges'.
- **2026-11-25: A restart closes the dashboard's own cut-off commute runs and says so; deploys wait for a running compute job (`scripts/deploy.sh`).** User ran "cover within 400 m of every place in a walk zone" (549 picks, 727 elements) and the panel showed "Stopped: unknown error". Cause, from the box's runs table and the service journal: the sampling job runs in a thread of the dashboard process, and other agents' deploys restarted `aptfinder.service` mid-run. Runs 470/471 (random 10%, Downtown Palo Alto bike+train 360 elements, Googleplex drive 132) finished; run 472 (random 10%, Rengstorff bike) started 06:43:35 UTC, its matrix request (120 bike elements) returned and was cached, and the restart at 06:43:38 killed it before any trip was written; the cover run then did 473 (Downtown PA bike+train, 35 places, 105 elements), 474 (Googleplex drive, 61), 475 (Rengstorff bike, 130 places but 105 elements: 25 legs reused from 472's cache) and 476 (Rengstorff bike+train) started 06:45:47 and was killed by the restart at 06:45:51 with no element logged (a request in flight then may have been billed by Google and never logged or cached). After a restart `job` was `idle`, and the page printed anything not running or done as "unknown error". Leg cache check: `Commute.prefetch` already commits each request's elements as it returns; the box had 923 `google_matrix` elements logged since 06:40 and exactly 923 Google legs cached in that window (bike 480 + 250, drive 132 + 61), so nothing paid was lost; a test now pins it (a failing second request keeps the first request's legs, and the rerun asks only for the rest). Decisions: (a) dashboard runs carry `params.from = "dashboard"` (`route_plan(origin=)`; the single-attribute compute already did); on start the dashboard closes its own still-`running` commute runs as `error` "interrupted: the dashboard restarted mid-run" (the one allowed runs update) and `GET /api/compute/status` reports `state: error, interrupted: [run ids]` with that message; runs from the CLI or after-intake estimates (other processes) are left alone; an `idle` status shows nothing. (b) No cache change needed. (c) `scripts/deploy.sh` on the box: `git pull --ff-only` (through `nix shell nixpkgs#git` when git is not on PATH), polls `GET /api/compute/status` (base path read from `.env`, never printed) until no job is running (default timeout 30 min: exit 2 without restarting; a non-200 answer: exit 3; not answering: restart), then restarts the service and waits for it to answer. AGENTS.md rule 14 now requires it for every deploy. 472 and 476 predate the tag and were closed by hand on the box (the allowed close-out, 06:57 UTC). Deployed with the new script at about 06:57 UTC: it found a job running (the user had pressed Run again), waited for it to end ('state: done'), then restarted. By then the user's reruns (runs 477 to 496, cover 400 m, refine, random 10%, cover 100 m) had logged 3,462 matrix elements since 06:40, and 3,462 Google legs were cached in the same window. Run 477 redid 476 (Rengstorff bike+train, 73 places, 68 elements). Dry run after it (cover within 400 m, every place in a walk zone, 1,324 places, same functions as the dashboard): 79 uncached elements left, 73 of them Meta HQ drive (a newly ticked cell, 987 of 1,324 places covered before), Rengstorff bike+train 2 places / 0 elements (legs cached), Caltrain walk 4, Google bus walk 2; the cells the cut-off run had were done. Two older after-intake estimate runs (236, 239, 03:36 UTC, another process) are still 'running' and were left alone. Replaced: bare `systemctl --user restart` deploys; a cut-off run left `running` forever.
- **2026-11-25: Accuracy per KNN cell in the Pipeline panel and the dry run.** User: per ticked cell, % of scope places with their own Google route, % with a KNN time, % estimate only, and KNN accuracy from leave-one-out with n, median error, "the 5%" (read as the worst-5% bound: p95 of |error|) and % inside the range. Coverage counts the provider of the value the list shows for the cell (`sampling._value`: for a set cell the fastest point, so the three shares match the colours on screen). Leave-one-out (`filter.knn.loo_errors`): every place with its own Google trip for the cell (a set cell pools all its points) is predicted from its other Google-routed places within RADIUS_M, up to K, by the same `neighbours`/`knn_trip` as `add_knn`; error = 0 inside [min - KNN_PAD_MIN, max + KNN_PAD_MIN], else the minutes outside it (so "inside" is against the padded range and is not comparable with the 35-43% unpadded misses in the KNN entry above); places with no routed neighbour within 400 m are not scored. Percentiles are nearest-rank. LOO is scope-free and cached per cell under the dashboard's data version; coverage is computed per request. One line in the "How this works" dialog. First real numbers (box, 2026-11-25 ~07:00 UTC, scope every place in a walk zone, 1,324 places; own Google / KNN / estimate; LOO n, median, p95, inside the padded range): Downtown Palo Alto bike+train 34.9 / 65.1 / 0%, n 465, 0.0, 1.0 min, 80.4%; Googleplex drive 32.6 / 67.4 / 0%, n 436, 0.0, 0.8 min, 83.7%; Rengstorff bike 30.3 / 69.7 / 0%, n 406, 0.0, 0.9 min, 78.8%; Rengstorff bike+train 30.1 / 69.7 / 0.2%, n 403, 0.0, 1.0 min, 80.9%; nearest Caltrain walk 30.9 / 69.0 / 0.2%, n 826 (pooled over stations), 0.0, 4.2 min, 63.1%; nearest Google bus walk 25.3 / 72.2 / 2.5%, n 380, 0.0, 4.4 min, 58.4%; nearest Caltrain bike (ticked, never sampled) 0 / 0 / 100%, n 0. So for targets the worst 5% are off by about 1 minute past the padded range; walking to the nearest stop is worse (p95 over 4 min). No LOO place had a neighbour at the same coordinates (0 at 0 m), so shared legs do not flatter the numbers. Replaced: nothing (coverage before/after the sample was the only number).
- **2026-11-25: Commute times shown in whole minutes.** User: "round everything to the minute." Every commute time the dashboard shows is `Math.round`ed (`mins()` in app.js): the drawer matrix (was '~48.2'), KNN ranges ('17–19', one number when both ends round alike) and their neighbour list, card facts, hover legs, the nearest-points list, net commute and its spread, the Verify trip sample, and the accuracy table's errors; rule reasons already used whole minutes (`:.0f`). The database, the KNN ranges and rule evaluation keep full precision. Replaced: one decimal place (`num`).
- **2026-11-25: Refine converges: candidates only, an absolute width bar, estimate-only places left to cover; a 'do I still need to sample' table.** User: "I ran some samples so far, why are we not getting anywhere": a refine dry run over every place in a walk zone (1,324 places) planned 1,016 places / 1,422 elements after several runs (4,078 of 9,000 matrix elements used this month). Causes, measured on the box with the old code (1,370 walk-zone places, 7 ticked cells): (1) 'the widest 10% of KNN ranges' is relative, so every refine finds a new 10%: 502 of the picks (72 Downtown Palo Alto bike+train, 74 Googleplex drive, 82 Meta HQ drive, 76 Rengstorff bike, 77 Rengstorff bike+train, 52 Caltrain walk, 69 Google bus walk), the narrowest of them only 1.6-2.1 min wide for the target cells; (2) outside the candidates scope a straddle counted for any goal, including places that already fail that goal on price, size or term: all 39 straddles were such places (Caltrain walk 29, Google bus walk 10), and 0 straddles were at candidates; (3) estimate-only places counted as straddles (3). Rengstorff is in no goal, so its two cells were pure widest-10% picks. Decisions: refine always restricts a place to the goals it can still pass apart from commute rules (`sampling.candidate_goals`, the `routing_candidates` logic merged to buildings, whatever the scope) and to goals with a commute rule on the pair (must-have or nice-to-have: Google bus walk is a nice rule of `lease`), and picks a KNN range that straddles such a goal's limit or is wider than `REFINE_MAX_SPREAD_MIN` = 2 min; a routed place is never picked again, so it runs out. Estimate-only places (no routed neighbour within 400 m) are counted per cell (`no_neighbour`) and the dry run says 'use cover'. `refine_plan` returns (plan, notes); `--frac` is gone. New refine dry run (box, same cells): 22 picks / 29 elements for both scopes (walk zone 1,369 places, candidates 1,201): Caltrain walk 7 places / 14 elements and Google bus walk 15 / 15, all 'wide', no straddles; every target cell 0; Meta HQ drive 2 candidates with no routed neighbour. Progress metric (`sampling.hinges`, `hinge_counts`): a place is undecided because of a commute when no deciding listing passes the goal and one is needs_check on must-have commute rules only; per cell when that cell is a needs_check option of such a rule and none of the rule's options passes. Live: lease 0, temp 103, all 103 waiting on Downtown Palo Alto x transit (3 also on bike and walk to it): transit is not a grid column (not shown since 2026-11-25), so no sample can settle them; the panel says so instead of suggesting a run. Coordinator follow-up for the user ("tell me whether I still need to run some data point, for current search results and for the overall dataset; the worst 10% and 1% is what really matters"): a table at the top of the commute panel, columns current search results / all places (walk zones), a row per ticked cell and a total: p90 / p99 expected error (own Google 0; a KNN place: the cell's leave-one-out errors of ranges on the same side of 2 min wide, `knn.loo_samples` now also returns the range width; estimate only: the cell's measured |estimate - Google| from places with both, latest of each), undecided count, verdict done (green: 0 undecided, p99 <= `DONE_P99_MIN` = 2 min) / run the cheaper of cover (400 m) and refine that picks something, with its uncached elements / settled (nothing to pick) / unknown (no measurement). Whole minutes in the panel. Live values, walk zone (p90 / p99 min, verdict): Downtown PA bike+train 0 / 1.3 done; Googleplex drive 0 / 1.0 done; Rengstorff bike 0 / 1.6 done; Rengstorff bike+train 0 / 1.5 done; Meta HQ drive 2.8 / 18.5, run cover 36 places / 36 elements (its estimate-only places: estimates are off by up to 18 min); Caltrain walk 0 / 3.6, run refine 7 / 14; Google bus walk 1.5 / 5.5, run cover 1 / 1; all cells 0.3 / 7.8, 103 undecided (temp, transit). Candidates scope alike (Meta HQ cover 35 / 35, Google bus refine 15 / 15). Cost: about 18 s to evaluate goals and 9-12 s per scope uncached on the box (mostly `planned_legs`), cached per data version afterwards. Known limit: walking cells' KNN error (p95 about 4 min) may keep p99 over 2 min after refine runs out; the verdict then says settled, not run. Replaced: refine's needs_check-for-any-goal plus widest 10%; the panel's coverage-only progress.
- **2026-11-25: A conflict is relevant to a goal only if another reading would change the goal's result.** The Gardens of Sunnyvale unit d (Zillow 3bd/2ba $7,000, Zumper 3bd/2.5ba $7,000) was shown under the lease goal (sizes 3bd/2+ba or 4bd/3+ba, < $2,500/bed) because its size rule reads baths, though both readings pass it. `verify.goal_relevance` now re-evaluates the touched rules on every combination of the candidate values per listing (cap 64) and keeps the conflict only when the listing's status (pass / needs_check / fail) differs; 'a rule fails on every reading' stays as the special case 'settled', the rest is hidden as 'every reading gives the same result' (new count in the Verify header and `hidden.same`). Too-cheap and stale items keep the earlier test (their alternative is an unknown price or no offer, not a stated value). On the live database (532 open conflicts): lease 5 shown -> 4 (Gardens d now 'same'; 422 irrelevant, 105 settled unchanged), temp 7 -> 7 (395 / 130), 'uh' (no rules) 0 -> 0. Replaced: 'a rule reads a disputed field and no rule fails on every reading'.
- **2026-11-25: 'No longer for rent': a user mark and removed/sold Zillow pages take a listing off the market; newest evidence wins.** User, on the Verify item 2105 Cornell St (Zillow $6,500, 'Zillow's page now says other, not for rent: still offered?'): there was no way to say "it's gone", only ✓/✗/? on the price. Then, for Maravilla (733 Independence Ave, $7,495, latest event 2026-11-24 'Listing removed') and The Chateau (302 Easy St, 2026-11-23 'Listing removed'): retire those automatically. New view `withdrawals` (no table): gone evidence is a user `checks` row on field `active` (true_value `"gone"`; undo appends `"offered"`) or a detail page that says SOLD / RECENTLY_SOLD or whose newest event is 'Listing removed'; offered evidence is an undo, a FOR_RENT page, or a fresh intake observation (not extract, not reparse: those re-append old data, and the 2026-11-25 extract runs 438/461 postdate the detail run). `listings_base.active` is 0 while the newest evidence says gone, so the listing leaves the list, counts, conflicts and Verify. Of the 31 stale items on the box, 25 retire on their own (OTHER or RECENTLY_SOLD with 'Listing removed') and 6 stay for the user (104 Evandale, 3357 Brittan, 609 Arcadia, 1373 W Middlefield: FOR_SALE; 2105 Cornell, 300 Chiquita: OTHER with no event). A checks row rather than an annotation because it is the user's check of a listing against its page, with the date; it is kept out of accuracy and the sample queue. Replaces: 'Nothing is retired automatically' and ✗/? as the only answer to a gone listing.
- **2026-11-25: Who may rent: 'none' needs real text; own-site pages are read for it; Verify's too-cheap items can answer 'income-restricted'.** User: "villa montgomery conflict is because income restriction". Villa Montgomery (Redwood City, own site on firsthousing.org) offers studio $838, 1 bd $1,012, 2 bd $1,212, 3 bd $1,219, and its rendered page says 'This Community is Income Restricted' with an AMI table, yet its four plan rows read `restriction = none`: the extract sweep saw only 'Name: Villa Montgomery Apartments' and the plan name, and Haiku answered none with nothing to go on. Three changes. (1) A 'none' counts only when the entry is described (40+ normalized characters beyond its Title, Name, Amenities and source-extra lines); otherwise it is unknown (needs_check under `equals restriction none`). Judged on the text actually sent, raw lines included, so Apartments.com, Zumper and Zillow rows whose about text sits only in the raw item keep their none. No schema bump (that would re-ask every listing); applied in `check_restriction` on cached answers, and `extract --recheck-none` re-applies only the affected listings for free. (2) render's Haiku call also returns the community's restriction with a verbatim quote, stored as `extra.page_restriction` on the plan rows (a sibling key, not `extra.extracted`, because extract replaces `extracted` wholesale and would erase it; render rows are the scraped base extract builds on, so the key survives later readings); `render-restriction` reads it from the stored raw page text of sites already rendered (no Apify; all rendered sites, not only cheap ones, so market-rate sites get a page-backed none in place of the name-only none that (1) removes). (3) A too-cheap Verify item gets 'income-restricted' / 'other restriction' beside ✓/✗: one place annotation `restriction`, which pooling reads first and which drops the place's too-cheap items. Considered and not done: inferring income from a low price (rule 5: unknown never rejects; the user's click is the evidence) and a list of affordable-housing operators (the page text says it directly). Known side effect: Avalon and Prometheus plan rows are not read by render, so their name-only none becomes unknown until their text says otherwise. Replaced: 'none' from any text, including a name; render read plans only; too-cheap items answered only on the price. Live (box, snapshot `data/backup-2026-11-25-restriction.db` first): `render-restriction` read 142 of 152 rendered sites (the 10 others are Prometheus rows, no page text) for $0.11 on the subscription, 771 plan rows: none 125, income 11, employer 3, senior 2, other 1. Villa Montgomery: income, 'This Community is Income Restricted. Average income of the households may not exceed the maximum income limits for the property's program.' (firsthousing.org); others: The Millton 'income-restricted households qualifying at 80% of Area Median Income (AMI)', Sierra Vista 'Project-Based Section 8 housing program, and income restrictions apply', Tree House 'administered under the Tax Credit Program', Colonnade (Los Altos) employer 'a 167-unit community for Stanford employees', The Cardinal employer 'dedicated to Stanford's workforce'.
