Suspect: MV Apartments ($4,037 to $5,998) read income from 'Below-Market Rates', likely a BMR program for a few units (left as read; to check by hand). `extract all --recheck-none` ($0, cached) turned 1,325 thin 'none' readings unknown; active offer rows none 9,086 -> 8,519, unknown 96 -> 612 (RentCafe 299, own-site 230, PadMapper 39). Places: none -> income 2 (Villa Montgomery, MV Apartments), none -> employer 2, none -> unknown 55. Goal lease (`restriction = none`): pass 57 -> 51 (Villa Montgomery fails; Avalon Mountain View and Hiro, whose Avalon / Prometheus rows are not page-read, and three RentCafe name-only buildings become needs_check); its Verify too-cheap items shown 1 -> 0 (Villa Montgomery, now settled). All conflicts: 50 too-cheap items before and after (only the new Verify answer drops one).
- **2026-11-25: Obvious duplicate buildings merge by themselves: the same building page under 100 m apart; homepages are no evidence.** User: "can you do obvious merges when the website is the same and it's less than 100 m apart", and, on the Verify pair Le Parc Dauphine (1921 California St) / Montclair Dauphine (426 Escuela Ave), 62 m apart with 'same own website: zell.com': a property manager's homepage is not one building. New rule `auto-merge:same-site-100m` in `identity.run` (see identity); the duplicate list's website reason now uses the same building-page test. Dry run on the live data (3,283 places): 5 pairs, all with the same name or address and all with a building page: Windsor Ridge 825 / 829 E Evelyn Ave (7 m, essexapartmenthomes.com/apartments/sunnyvale/windsor-ridge), Spruce 655 Fair Oaks Ave / 655 South Fair Oaks Avenue (12 m, prometheusapartments.com/ca/sunnyvale-apartments/spruce), Selo of Pastoria 833 W El Camino Real / 501 S Pastoria Ave (29 m, a corner lot, deanzaproperties.com/south-bay-area/selo-of-pastoria), Verve 2020 / 1984 W El Camino Real (30 m, udr.com/.../mountain-view/verve), Central Park at Whisman Station 100 N Whisman Rd / 100 Whisman Station Dr (51 m, greystar.com/central-park-at-whisman-station-mountain-view-ca/p_17043). Four of the five you had already merged by hand (their listings' user links already point at the kept place), so the rule agrees with you there and changes one place now (Spruce, 1 listing). Skipped as homepages (31 pairs under 100 m, by design; merge by hand if they are one building): stanfordvilla.com (Stanford Villa 3351-3375 / 3375 Alma St), sharongroveapts.com, middleparkapts.com, paloaltoplaza.com, courtyardapartmentsrc.com, 600whisman.com, mountainviewplaceapts.com, foothillheights.com; and rightly: zell.com (Le Parc / Montclair Dauphine), caprop.com (Camille North / Camille; Noel Oaks / Laurel Court), marymountapts.com (Marymount Tower / Manor), heritagerealtysanmateo.com, rentbt.com, vrent.com. Duplicate list: 182 pairs before (196 earlier today), 169 once homepages, portfolio pages and aggregators stopped counting (13 pairs gone, 6 more keep their other reasons): Le Parc / Montclair Dauphine, Camille North / Camille, Marymount Tower / Manor, Noel Oaks / Laurel Court, the G5 Broadway pair, but also four that look like one building and now need their address or name to come back (600 Whisman, Mountain View Place 2283 Latham / 551 Ortega, Windemere 395 Ano Nuevo, Flats at Cityline). Why a rule inside identity rather than written links: recomputed from the same evidence every run it is idempotent, reaches new data, and undoes itself when you answer 'not the same building'; a user link still wins. Replaces: every merge by hand in the drawer; the queue's website reason used any shared URL, homepages included. Live (snapshot data/backups/apartments-20261125-before-auto-merge-same-site.db, then `aptf-identity run`): 31 rule links appended (links 19,090 -> 19,121), places 3,283 -> 3,282 (Spruce's Google Places row joined 655 South Fair Oaks Avenue, now 'Spruce', 99 listings), duplicate list 182 -> 169 (homepage rule) -> 168 (the merge); a second run appended 0.
- **2026-11-25: Verify answers in place; the screen never reloads under you.** User: "can you make it so it doesn't disappear the page when I'm clicking through a bunch of conflicts back to back" and "the page reloads in the middle". Causes found in app.js: each duplicate-pair answer and each own-site answer refetched and redrew its whole list (blanked to 'Loading…', back to the top) and reloaded the map; the Verify button always restarted at the first conflict page, so opening a place from a conflict, Escape (also when cancelling a typed true value) or x lost the page and its scroll; conflict buttons waited for the POST before showing as pressed. No timer, ETag check, page reload or service-worker reload exists (the only background redraw is the list after a compute run, which now keeps the list's scroll). Now: optimistic answers with an inline error on failure, checked conflicts dim, answered pairs and buildings fold to a stub, focus moves to the next open item, a Refresh button, paging subtracts the answered items that left the queue (the duplicate and own-site lists added a full page before; an added URL keeps its building), and close/reopen keeps the screen. Replaced: a full refetch and redraw after each duplicate or own-site answer, and a fresh Verify on every open.
- **2026-11-25: app.js function names are unique (test).** `loadStatus` was declared twice (the KNN "still needs a sample?" table and the header queue counts); the later one won, so the KNN status table never loaded. Renamed to `loadKnnStatus` / `loadQueueStatus`; `tests/test_static_js.py` fails on any repeated `function NAME(` in app.js. No other duplicates found. Same commit: the goal editor's "+ add" menu gains "no restrictions (who may rent)" (`equals restriction none`). Replaced: one `loadStatus` name for two functions; no preset for who may rent.
- **2026-11-25: Transit is sampled through Route Matrix and shown again (KNN grid column, drawer, cards).** User: 103 temp places are undecided only on the goal's "transit <= 25 min to Downtown Palo Alto" option, and the user chose to keep transit. Google's docs (read 2026-11-25): Compute Route Matrix Essentials is "Requests to Compute Route Matrix that don't use Pro or Enterprise features"; Pro is triggered by "routingPreference property set to TRAFFIC_AWARE or TRAFFIC_AWARE_OPTIMAL" or a side-of-road, heading or vehicle-stopover modifier; Enterprise by "Two-wheeled vehicle routing elements", "Toll calculation elements", "Large vehicle routing elements" (sku-details/routes). TRANSIT is none of these, so transit elements bill as Compute Route Matrix Essentials. Usage-and-billing: "Maximum allowed total number of elements per ComputeRouteMatrix request with travelMode set to TRANSIT is 100", "Enable real-time traffic models by setting the departure time to now" (billed higher), and a rate limit of "3,000 EPM elements per minute" (`MeteredMatrix` paces at 2,500). Decisions: `GoogleRoutesLegSource.matrix` accepts transit and sends `departureTime` = `next_departure(depart)` (the next Tuesday 08:30 for `weekday_0830`, always in the future, never now), at most 100 transit elements a request; `Commute.prefetch` splits transit requests at 100 origins (each request still commits its elements to the leg cache as it returns). Transit matrix elements store no steps (the matrix gives duration and distance only), and they sit in the shared leg cache under provider google, so for a sampled place the drawer's Route with Google reuses the step-less transit leg (no legs shown) and bike+bus there falls back to plain bike (`_bike_bus_bike` needs transit steps; slower, never a wrong pass). KNN for transit is unchanged (400 m, 3 neighbours); transit time depends on stop proximity and schedules, so its leave-one-out error is expected to be worse than drive or bike (the status table shows it). Dashboard: `transit` is back in `MODES` (drawer matrix, Pipeline compute, goal editor) and a `transit` column in the KNN grid for commute targets only (like bike+train; a set row has no transit cell and `POST /api/knn/cells` refuses one); cards show a goal's transit option again. Transit has no straight-line estimate, so a place with no own route and no routed neighbour within 400 m stays '–'. Replaced: transit not offered or shown (2026-11-25 OSRM entry) and Route Matrix limited to walk, bike and drive. Live run (box, 2026-11-25, after deploy): the `Downtown Palo Alto x transit` cell was ticked in the grid (appended to the user's seven cells). Before: 102 temp places in the walk-zone candidates (293 over every place) waited only on that transit time, 99 of them with no transit value at all; 2 places had their own Google transit trip. Cover within 400 m: dry run 127 places / 127 elements, run 127 elements in 2 requests (100 + 27), 127 new trips, 0 no-route, times 4.8 to 45.4 min (median 29.6), e.g. Singularity House 6.3 min (452 m: Google's transit route is a walk), Startup House 17.2 min, a place 7.8 km out 24.5 min; undecided 102 -> 13. Refine: 35 elements (19 straddles, 16 wide), undecided 13 -> 3; refine again (ranges moved with the new routes) 10 elements, 3 -> 1 (g_e19bce981532e5de, KNN 23.8-25.0 min against the 25 min limit). Total 172 elements; matrix use this month 5,564 -> 5,736 of the 9,000 cap. Transit KNN leave-one-out (minutes outside the padded range): after cover n 43, median 2.3, p95 11.6, 23% inside; after both refines n 111, median 0.0, p95 6.9, 51% inside: much worse than drive or bike, as expected from stop proximity and schedules (a place 90 m from one at 23.2 min reads 19.7 from another 280 m away). The dashboard serves the transit grid column and transit in the drawer matrix (e.g. The Acacia, 231 Grant Ave: Google 15.0 min; 363 Everett Ave: KNN 8.0-12.3).
- **2026-11-25: Backend/frontend audit: lease vs stay prices by term; who may rent in the drawer; editor reaches every rule type.** User: "see what else may be mismatching between back and frontend" (trigger: no way to add `restriction = none` in "+ add…", fixed the same day in fac6f12). Method: FastAPI's route table (in process, on a copy of the box database taken read-only through SQLite's backup API, local `config/` which differs from the box only in `google_bus_stops.paid`) against every `api(` / `post(` call in app.js, the response keys each screen reads against what the routes return, and a headless Chrome (CDP over Node's WebSocket; the chrome-devtools MCP failed with 'Target closed') that added every "+ add…" entry to the lease goal and previewed it, opened a drawer, the four Verify screens and the Pipeline panel: no JS exceptions (only 404s for photos stored on the box). Every fetch matches a route, method and body model; no response key the JS reads is missing. Found and fixed: (1) **Lease vs stay prices** (user's example: 328 Bryant St, Blueground furnished '1-12+ months', 3 bd / 2.5 ba, $6,480: the Zillow row is term long, furnished; the card under the lease goal said 'no lease price' while the goal judged that row as a lease at $2,160 / bed and passed it). The places view's `min_lease_*` dropped every furnished row (`term IS NOT 'short' AND furnished IS NOT 1`), so a furnished long-term lease never showed; 181 places have such active rows, 146 of them had no lease price at all. Now by term, as the goals' term rule reads it: lease = term long, stay = term short; only an unknown term falls back to furnished (furnished = stay, else lease); each listing counts in exactly one. A listing offering both (1-12+ months) counts once, by the term its source gave (here Zillow long, the Zumper copy short at the `~blueground` stay place); counting it in both was considered and not done, since a row has one term and the goals read that. Live copy after: 328 Bryant lease $6,480 / $2,160 per bed, lease goal pass; 1,564 places have a lease price. Other furnished-as-short proxies checked: Verify's too-cheap queue already uses `term IS NOT 'short'`; `plans.plan_key` keeps 'furnished' (a furnished lease) as its own plan row for display and price inheritance, unchanged. (2) **Who may rent had no answer outside the too-cheap conflicts**: the backend takes any restriction value as a place annotation, but the only buttons were income / other on too-cheap items, and 5 lease places were needs_check only on 'restriction unknown' (none annotated on the box). The drawer now has the select above. (3) **'nearest point of a set' (`near`) had no "+ add…" entry** (reachable only by switching a commute row's point): added, plus a test that runs `ruleTemplates()` in Node and asserts every `RULE_TYPES` type has an entry and every entry passes `validate_rule` (fields and values inside a rule already come from `/api/goals`). (4) The amenity rule's `exclude` words had no input (kept only from a preset): an 'except words' input. (5) A strict limit's fail chip said '> 2,500' for a price per bed of exactly $2,500: `rules.fact` now carries `strict` and the chip says '≥' / '≤'. (6) The card's net commute was coloured by a net_commute rule's verdict even when the Net selector showed another aggregate: it is coloured only when they match, else the fail stays a chip. (7) A KNN time's hover said 'from 3 nearby Google routes' with 1 or 2 neighbours: it counts them. Left for the user (not changed): POST `/api/compute/dry_run` and `/api/compute/run` have no caller since the KNN grid replaced the single compute (tests still use them); `/api/verify/next` with conflicts (the UI always sends conflicts=false); unused response keys (`/api/meta` goals, `/api/places` amenities and in_zone); annotation ratings carry the selected goal but every goal's amenity rule reads the latest rating; a free-text 'restriction' comment typed in the notes block is not checked against the rule's values; whole-minute rounding at a limit (a KNN range shown 24-25 against 25 is needs_check after the 0.5 min pad); studios have no price per bed in the view (beds 0), so a $/bed rule is needs_check for them while Verify's too-cheap check counts a studio as one bedroom. Replaced: lease = not short and not furnished (2026-11-24 'three separate offers').
- **2026-11-25: Parking: Haiku reads offered / kinds / spots / cost per listing (schema v4); three goal rules; cards show it.** User: "look at parking: scrape through everything with a Haiku parking agent — how many spots, or free / unspecified, or paid, or whatever." Built on the existing reading, like who may rent, not a second pipeline: the v3 `parking` fact (one kind: garage / covered / lot / street / none) is replaced by a `parking` object beside `restriction` (offered yes / no, kinds, spots, cost included / paid with `usd_month`, a list of verbatim quotes), so one call per text still reads everything; bumping to v4 re-asks every listing once (cached by text afterwards). The conservative readings are in code (`check_parking`), not only in the prompt, because the cache key is the text and a prompt tweak would silently reuse old answers, while code applies again for free: street parking alone is offered no; a stated price with no cost is paid; a number of spots or a price counts only when a kept quote holds it. render's page reading asks parking in the same call (`extra.page_parking`), and `render-restriction --parking` reads it for sites already rendered from their raw page text (no Apify, parking-only schema so who may rent is not re-asked). Rules: `equals parking_included true`, `min parking_spots`, `max parking_usd_month` (no new rule type; the fields are in `EQUALS_FIELDS` / `NUMERIC_FIELDS` / `KNOWN_FIELDS`, so `/api/goals` offers them and the editor lists them; three presets in "+ add…"). Pooling is source-based rather than a Haiku 'scope' answer: an own site's pages and building-level sources hold for every unit at the place, a single post speaks for itself, and a row's own reading wins field by field. Unknown (unread, cost unspecified) is needs_check (rule 5). Considered and not done: inferring 'at least one spot' from 'parking included' (no number stated: unknown). Replaced: the one-word v3 parking fact. Trial (box, 4 listings per source, 44 listings, 11 Haiku calls): Haiku often left `parking` out of its answer (the key was optional): all 4 Craigslist posts ('Amenities: off-street parking', 'carport', 'Driveway parking') and 3 of 4 Zillow pages ('Parking: Attached; Garage; Off Street', 'Parking is offered based on availability and is subject to a fee') came back with no parking key, so they read as unknown. `parking` is now required in the schema (null still allowed) and the version is 5, so the 44 trial answers cached under v4 are not reused.
- **2026-11-25: Net commute hover lists its trips.** User: "hovering over spread (5 trips) should tell me which trips are included." `rules.commute_trips` now picks each term's trip and returns it whole (rule label, mode, point, minutes, provider, KNN min / max / neighbour count, `why` when skipped); `commute_terms` and the `net_commute` rule read it, and `/api/places` and the draft preview send it as the third element of each `commute` term, so the hover list is the same data the aggregate uses, not a second computation. A KNN term counts its neighbours' median (`minutes`), not either end of the range; the hover says so. Shown on the card's net commute fact (title, after the rule's verdict when it colours the fact), on the drawer's `net_commute` reason, and as an expandable line under each goal in the drawer. Replaced: `commute` terms as `[minutes, estimate]` only; no way to see which trips made the number.
- **2026-11-25: Duplicate buildings answered in bulk: a checkbox per pair and a sticky bar.** User: "can I checkmark and do them". Each pair has a checkbox; the bar at the top of the list (sticky while scrolling, wraps at phone width) gives 'select all on this page' and 'N selected: Merge each smaller into bigger | Not the same building | Clear'. The merge direction per pair is the highlighted single button's (smaller into bigger). New `POST /api/verify/duplicates/batch` takes `{a, b, into}` items and writes exactly what the single buttons write (the merge's user links, via the same helper, and the `place_pair` / `same_building` = no annotation) in order in one transaction, returning each item's error while the rest commit; `append_annotation` gained `commit=False` for that. The page sends one pair per request so progress ('merging 3/12…') and failures are per pair; each pair folds in place like the single buttons (the list is not fetched again; after a merge, other pairs with the merged-away place fold too), and a failure unfolds with its error and stays ticked for another try. The single buttons stay. Replaced: one click per pair only.
- **2026-11-25: Empty beds and baths: a Zillow single unit's own page fills them; else Haiku from the row's own text, quoted.** User: Zillow 453786796 (411 Forest Ave, Palo Alto) had beds, baths and price NULL while its page says '1 Bed / 1 Bath: Type D ... 1 beds 1 baths' ("haiku should catch this"); 734 Cowper St (453786840, '2 Bed / 2 Bath: Type A', 1,100 sqft) showed as needs_check in a 3-bedroom lease search. Cause, from the raw files: these are `apartmentComplex` search cards (`groupType`, `matchingHomeCount` 5) with no `unitsGroup`, `bedrooms`, `bathrooms` or price (`minBaseRent` 0), so `zillow_parse` makes one row with nothing; the zpid's detail page (run 367) is that unit's own (`bedrooms` 1, `bathrooms` 1, `living_area` 515, `listing_price` 0 = Price on Request). Active rows with NULL beds or baths before (live DB, by source): zillow 466 (42 single units, all with a page holding beds and baths; 424 building bedroom rows `<zpid>:Nbd` with beds and no baths), direct 169 (116 site_info, 53 plan rows), rentcafe 143 (140 site_info, 3 rows: 'Office', 'RV Storage'), google_places 308 and portfolio 99 and padmapper 16 (all site_info), airbnb 11 (rooms), craigslist 5. Built: `zillow_detail.unit_facts` (beds; baths = full + half / 2 as the search parser, 825 Kiely Blvd 1.5 not the page's 2; sqft 200 to 20,000; price only FOR_RENT and above 0, never the rent Zestimate) applied by the text reading to single-unit rows only; Haiku's `beds` / `baths` (`check_units`) for rows `units_wanted`, the number next to a bed / bath word in a verbatim quote, from anywhere in a one-home entry (`WHOLE_ENTRY`) but only from the row's own Title / Floor plan / Unit line on a building's plan rows; marked `from` 'zillow page' or 'text' in `extra.extracted.units`; `extract --units-missing`. No schema version bump: only these rows get their plan / unit lines and a marked cache key, so the 10k other rows keep their cached answers (and the parking re-read's version change is untouched). Not filled, on purpose: the 424 Zillow building rows' baths (the search card's `unitsGroup` holds only `bedrooms`, `minPrice`, `isRoomForRent`, the page is one representative unit's, the description is the building's: any value would mix units across rows), airbnb rooms (a room's baths is not the home's), site_info rows (a building, not a unit). Trial on a copy of the live DB (13 Haiku calls, $0.03): all 42 Zillow singles got beds and baths from their pages, 37 sqft, 1 price (1941 California St, Mountain View, $2,450); 411 Forest Ave 1 bd / 1 ba / 515 sqft, price unknown; 734 Cowper St 2 bd / 2 ba / 1,100 sqft. Text fills: craigslist 8003805243 baths 1 ('2 br/1 bath'), Jefferson Apartments (1331 Jefferson Ave, Redwood City) two plans 1 bd / 1 ba ('One Bed One Bath- B', 'One Bed One Bath - C'). Haiku proposed beds for 29 more direct plan rows ('4BR', '1 BR', 'Studio units'), all agreeing with the stored value except Indigo 'SMALT JR. 1-BED' (stored 0, read 1), none applied since only empty fields are filled; it proposed nothing for the craigslist room 'Private Bedroom in 2 Bed/1 Bath Apt', Hotel North Beach's 'shared baths', 'SRO/0BA' or 'Park Plaza 1S (Standard) Floor Plan'. Bad source data seen: 892 Gallatin Dr, Santa Clara (2 bd / 2 ba) has `living_area` 110 on its page, so sqft below 200 is unknown; 1010 Noel Dr, Menlo Park (Noel Oaks) reads 3 bd / 2 ba on its own page while the building's site says '2 bedroom 1 bath apartments and 2 bedroom 1½ bath townhomes': filled as Zillow states, a hand check (the page is unit #71's own and consistent: `bedrooms`, `reso_facts` and `ad_targets` all 3 bd / 2 ba, while its same-address siblings #68 and #3 are 2 bd / 2 ba). Live (box, snapshot `data/backups/apartments-2026-11-25-pre-units-missing.db`; `extract all --units-missing`, runs 555 and 557 to 559, 13 Haiku calls, $0.031): 45 rows filled, the same as the trial except 892 Gallatin Dr now without sqft (36 sqft, not 37). Active rows with NULL beds or baths: zillow 466 -> 424 (all 424 building bedroom rows, by design), craigslist 5 -> 4, direct 169 -> 167, rentcafe 143, airbnb 11, google_places 308, portfolio 99, padmapper 16 unchanged; total 1,217 -> 1,172 (non-site_info 538 -> 493; counted while the parking re-read's craigslist run 556 was still going, craigslist 8003805243 kept baths 1). Goal effect (lease, 3bd/2+ba or 4bd/3+ba): 734 Cowper St and 411 Forest Ave needs_check (beds unknown) -> fail ('is 2bd', 'is 1bd'); Noel Oaks (1010 Noel Dr) passes the size rule on #71's 3 bd / 2 ba and stays needs_check on price per bed (price unknown, as on 41 of the 42 rows). Considered and not built: a building-level 'unit types offered' reading so an unknown-size row at a 2-bedroom-only building fails a 3-bedroom rule (a new place-level fact and filter semantics; reported). Replaced: Zillow detail pages never read for beds, baths, sqft or price; no reading of beds or baths from text.
- **2026-11-25: Audit follow-up: the single-attribute compute endpoints are gone.** User approved the 2026-11-25 audit's leftovers. `POST /api/compute/dry_run` and `/api/compute/run` (one point or set x mode for chosen places, by estimate or Google Routes calls) had no caller since the KNN grid's sampling (`POST /api/sample/dry_run|run`, Route Matrix elements) replaced that block of the Pipeline panel; removed with `ComputeIn`, `_check_spec` and their five tests (estimate run, Google dry-run cap, typed confirm over 20 calls, redacted refusal, the 'waiting for the database' note). Kept: the job slot, the restart's close-out of cut dashboard runs, `GET /api/compute/status` (the page's poll and `scripts/deploy.sh`'s wait) and `GET /api/compute/log` (the panel's run log), now covered by the sampling test (the run appears in the log) and a redacted-view test on the sample endpoints; the scope parsing (`_compute_places`, list / zone / candidates) serves the sampling endpoints; `trips.dry_run` and `compute_all` stay for the CLI (`aptf-commute compute`). Replaced: two routes with no caller.
- **2026-11-25: Audit follow-up: `/api/verify/next` is the sample only.** Its `conflicts=true` default put every conflict before the sample, but the dashboard pages conflicts with `/api/verify/batch` and always sent `conflicts=false`; the parameter and that branch are removed (the page no longer sends it), and the test that walked a conflict through `next` reads it from the batch. Replaced: `GET /api/verify/next?conflicts=` (default true).
- **2026-11-25: Audit follow-up: `/api/places` drops `in_zone`.** No reader (app.js, tests, scripts, other endpoints; the Pipeline computes its own walk-zone set). Two other unused keys the audit named are kept because tests read them: `/api/meta` `goals` (`tests/test_goals.py` checks an edited goal's label there; the page reads goals from `/api/goals`) and `/api/places` `amenities` (`tests/test_web.py`, `test_places_carry_amenity_flags_for_the_require_checkboxes`; no page reads it since the require checkboxes became goal rules). Replaced: a per-place walk-zone flag nobody read.
- **2026-11-25: Audit follow-up: a rating belongs to the building, not to a goal; a later comment keeps it.** The drawer sent the header goal as `goal_id` with every rating and comment, though every goal's amenity rule (and the card's stars) read the latest row whatever its goal: now it sends none, and old rows keep theirs (readable, unread). Testing 'the filter reads the latest rating' found a hole: `filter.place_annotations` (and the dashboard's `latest_annotations`) kept the latest row per attribute, so a comment typed after a rating (the drawer's comment form) made the amenity rule fall back to the amenity lists, and the card's stars disappear, while the drawer still showed the rating. Now the latest row carries the latest rating of its attribute. Test: an old goal-labelled 2/5, then 5/5, then a comment: an unsaved goal's gym rule passes. Box copy (2026-11-25): 3 place annotations, none rated, none with a goal_id, so nothing changes there today. Replaced: goal_id on ratings; 'latest row' hiding a rating behind a comment.
- **2026-11-25: Audit follow-up: a 'restriction' answer is one of the rule's values.** `POST /api/annotations` with entity_type place and attribute restriction now requires the comment to be one of `EQUALS_FIELDS["restriction"]` (none, income, senior, veterans, student, employer, other; trimmed and lowercased, stored that way), else 400 'restriction must be one of: ...'. Before, the drawer's free notes block accepted any text under that attribute and `filter.annotated_restriction` took it as the answer, so 'students welcome' would beat every source and fail an `equals restriction none` rule as a value no rule names. The drawer's comment form now shows the server's refusal in a toast instead of failing silently. Box copy: no restriction annotations yet, so nothing to migrate. Replaced: any text accepted.
- **2026-11-25: Audit follow-up: a studio counts as one bedroom for price per bed everywhere.** The listings view gave a studio (beds 0) no price per bed, so a `$/bed` rule was needs_check for it and the card showed '$?/bed', while Verify's too-cheap check already divided by `MAX(beds, 1)`. Now the view, `plans.inherit_prices`, `verify._with_values` (goal relevance) and the drawer's plan rows (`group_plans`) all divide by `MAX(beds, 1)`; unknown beds still give none. Cards: the beds line already says 'studio'; the lease '/bed' figure has a hover 'per bedroom; a studio counts as one', and the plan table shows a studio's $/bed. Box copy (2026-11-25, 3,101 places, goals temp / lease / uh): goal statuses unchanged for every goal (temp 40 pass / 243 needs_check / 2,818 fail, lease 45 / 30 / 3,026, uh 3,101 pass; 0 listing results changed either): the lease goal's 3-bedroom size rule already fails studios and no other goal reads price per bed. 269 places have a studio listing; the places view's minimum $/bed changed at 153 places (124 had none before), the lease $/bed at 116 (88 had none), e.g. Hayden Apartments none -> $2,049 (a $2,049 Craigslist studio), Monte Sierra Apartments (1901 Montecito Ave) $2,850 -> $2,250 (an Apartments.com studio), Dinah's Court $3,495 -> $2,895. Replaced: price per bed only when beds > 0.
- **2026-11-25: Audit follow-up: `plans.plan_key` reads the term first, like the cards.** The offer part of a plan's key was stay for term short, else 'furnished' for any furnished row, else lease; so a furnished listing with no term was a 'furnished lease' plan while the cards (2026-11-25 audit) count it as a stay. Now: term short, or no term and furnished, is a stay; term long and furnished stays its own 'furnished' row (a furnished lease such as AvalonBay Furnished+, kept apart for display and price inheritance); everything else is a lease. Box copy: exactly 5 listings have no term and are furnished, all PadMapper building rows (`padmapper|building:24234026`, `:51425447`, `:58444898`, `:58679060`, `:60873148`) of kind `site_info` with no price, beds or baths; `goal_inputs` and the drawer's `group_plans` drop `site_info` before `plan_key`, so no plan row, inherited price (140 inherited before and after, identical) or goal result changed. Replaced: furnished-without-term = furnished lease plan.
- **2026-11-25: Who may rent: an income tag needs explicit eligibility words and its strongest quote; operators fill in.** User, on a drawer reading 'income-restricted ("The community consists of both affordable garden apartments and townhomes ...", direct page)': "it says both the words affordable and subsidized"; the tag was right but the quote the weak one. Known false positive: MV Apartments ($4,037-5,998) tagged income from a menu link 'Below-Market Rates'. (1) The prompt (extract's `RESTRICTION`, render's `PAGE_RESTRICTION`) asks for explicit eligibility language only, says marketing words are no evidence, asks for the strongest quote (limit / AMI / subsidized / Section 8 / LIHTC over 'affordable housing community' over others) and optionally a second (`evidence_2`). (2) `check_restriction` keeps an income quote only when `restriction_rank` finds such words and puts the higher-ranked quote first; a 'none' may carry a quote that says market-rate. No schema bump (it would re-ask every listing); `extract --recheck-restriction income` re-applies cached answers to rows read as income (free). (3) `render-restriction` used to skip a site whose new answer was unknown, so a rejected tag stayed: it now appends the plan row without `page_restriction` (`cleared`), and skips rows whose reading is unchanged. (4) User: "midpen" (the place is MidPen's Redwood Court), then "Avalon has no restriction, idk why it's unknown": `config/operators.toml` lists affordable-housing nonprofits and housing authorities (income) and market-rate REITs and managers (none) by domain and operator field; the filter computes the operator's reading at read time (nothing stored), below any quoted limit: income over a listing's none unless a page says market-rate; none only where nothing was read. Seen in our data and added beside the user's lists: EAH Housing (eahhousing.org), Mercy's leasing site (livewithmercyhousing.com), San Mateo County's housing site (housing.smcgov.org), and market-rate operators with 3+ buildings here: Blueground (operator field, 104 places), Legacy Partners, Klingbeil. Not added (lead-generation hosts or managers that also run affordable homes): asset.aptlooknow.com, amc.touraptnow.com, vrent.com, avenue5 and conam on aptfeatures.com, housingbayarea.mtc.ca.gov (the region's affordable-housing portal, not an operator). Replaced: any quote on the page for income ('affordable apartments' was the prompt's own example); a re-read that could add but never remove a tag; no operator knowledge.
- **2026-11-25: Baths for own-site plans that state none: borrowed from the same plan elsewhere, else read from the plan's drawing, else set by hand in the drawer.** User: "Indigo is kind of stupid, they don't specify baths in their website text — you have to pull that from Zillow or honestly image context… it may just be manual." Own-site plan rows with unknown baths keep the lease size rule (3bd/2+ba or 4bd/3+ba) at needs_check. Built: `plans.inherit_baths` (read time, so nothing is stored on the row and a hand check on the unknown value keeps winning: a stored fill would make that check lapse, since a check holds only while the value is unchanged; it would also be superseded by every re-render), `aptfinder.planread` (Haiku vision over the stored drawing, answers in `runs.counts`), a 'set ba' button in the drawer's listing table, and accuracy no longer counts a check on an empty value. Trial and error: `claude -p` with `--tools ""` takes only text on stdin; an image goes as a base64 block with `--input-format stream-json`, which requires `--output-format stream-json` (and `--verbose`); first try ('requires output-format=stream-json') failed, second worked: Realm Plan 2d's drawing read as 2 bedrooms, 2 full baths, 0 half, confident, 'Bedroom 1, Bedroom 2, Bath 1 (shower), Bath 2 (tub/shower)', $0.0013. Live data before the run (2026-11-25): 44 active direct plan rows without baths, 9 of them without beds; 8 priced (Indigo 5, Verve 2, Realm 1). Indigo's direct rows (LAPIS, SMALT JR. 1-BED, CELESTE, MIDNIGHT, ULTRAMARINE) have no sqft and no drawing, and Indigo's other sources give no matching plan (Zillow's rows are bed counts without baths; Zillow's building page is one representative unit's, so its 'bathrooms 2' is not a plan's), so neither fill applies: Indigo is manual ('set ba'). Not built (an inference rule the user did not ask for): 'every listing at the place with the same beds agrees' without a size or name match (it would fill Verve's '2 Bedrooms' from 20 Apartments.com 2bd plans, all 2 ba). Replaced: own-site plan rows without baths stayed unknown; the drawer had no way to set a value outside Verify's conflict queue. Live run (2026-11-25, run 560, $0.0011; snapshot `data/backups/2026-11-25-pre-planread.db`): 42 direct rows without baths (7 without beds); borrowed 1 (Realm Apartments Plan 2d, 2 bd / 1,052 sqft / $5,918 -> 2 ba from rentcafe plan:6739845 '2x2B'); 0 disagreements; 5 with a stored drawing (all Park Plaza), 4 of them without beds so not asked; 1 read: Park Plaza 'Studio Floor Plan' -> 0 bd, 1 full bath, confident (Haiku noted a 'BEDROOM' label in the open room and counted it as the studio); 36 have no drawing (Indigo's five among them). Goal results unchanged on every place (Realm's row is a 2 bd, Park Plaza's unpriced); no existing check has an empty stored value, so accuracy is unchanged. On a copy, setting Indigo ULTRAMARINE to 2 ba through `POST /api/checks` turns the lease goal from needs_check to pass ('3bd/2ba', $2,343/bed). The hover's 'seen' is kept to 1,200 characters (500 cut Haiku's caveat).
- **2026-11-25: Dashboard speed: KNN search, one best_trips per data version, cached list and drawer reads, gzip.** User: "also fix performance", the dashboard felt slow. Measured on the box against a copy of the live database (3,283 places, 11,193 listings, 139,184 trips; FastAPI TestClient in-process; cold = new app, warm = repeat, edit = after one rating, which invalidates every cached result). cProfile: the KNN neighbour search ran the haversine on every place of 3 x 3 cells 2.2 km wide for a 400 m radius (3.5 M calls per `best_trips`); `best_trips` (KNN included) was built again inside `evaluate_all`, inside the separately cached `goal_inputs` and inside `pipeline.overview`; `/api/places` and the drawer read the `listings` (1.7 s) and `places` (2.5 s) views on every request, uncached; the drawer's pipeline section read `listings WHERE place_key = ?` twice (each a full view read) and its 'related' list read the `places` view; `compute_for_place` re-read `points.toml` once per place (4,845 reads in one KNN status table, 13 s); `planned_legs` read the `places` view once per cell (13 x 2.5 s). Fixes: `knn.neighbours` rejects by a lat/lon box before the haversine (same neighbours: `best_trips` and every cell's leave-one-out identical on the copy); the dashboard builds `best_trips` once per data version and passes it (`goal_inputs(trips=)`, `evaluate_all(trips=)`, `pipeline.overview(res=, places=, conflicts=, own_queue=, dup_queue=, web=)`; `best_trips(located=)`, `planned_legs(located=)`, `sampling.summary(loc=)`, `candidate_goals(loc=)` take the cached places), each missing argument computed as before (CLI paths unchanged); `compute_all` reads points and point sets once; `/api/places` is built once per data version from the cached views; the drawer reads `place_status` from the cached listing rows (`by_place=`), 'related' from the cached places and checks from one cached `current_checks`; Verify's sample queue is cached per seed; responses are gzipped (`GZipMiddleware`, level 5, photos excluded; added after the redact middleware so redact still reads plain text). `/api/places` no longer carries times to single points of non-target sets (stations, bus stops, groceries: 22 % of `minutes` keys, nothing in the list read them; sets come through `nearest`) unless a goal's commute rule names that point. SQLite indexes were tried (runs, checks, links, a descending observations index): the `listings` view stayed at 1.9 s, so none were added. Every response body was compared before and after on the same copy (identical, apart from the intended `minutes` trim), and a fresh app answers the same as the long-lived one after an edit. Timings, ms, before -> after (cold / warm / after an edit): `/api/places` 22,473 -> 14,317 / 6,119 -> 288 / 22,946 -> 14,176, 17.7 MB -> 1.9 MB on the wire; drawer 8,002 / 4,559 / 8,341 -> 1,957 / 427 / 1,911 (the other two drawers 4,4xx -> 15x every time); goal preview 9,366 / 761 -> 3,832 / 563; `/api/pipeline` 44,595 -> 15,163 cold (warm 4 ms both); KNN status table 57,117 -> 5,625 cold; KNN accuracy 764 -> 479; Verify sample item 1,213 / 1,2xx -> 1,295 / 5; sample dry run 14,559 / 14,6xx -> 985 / 1,039. Unchanged: `/api/status` cold 22.9 s (seven Verify queues each read the `listings` view), the residual after any edit. Replaced: per-request view reads and best_trips rebuilds; ungzipped JSON.
- **2026-11-25: Who may rent: nothing stated is assumed none in the goals (option A).** User: "assume no restriction unless stated otherwise" (Avalon Mountain View, Hiro, Middle Park, Calderon, University Gardens were needs_check only on 'restriction unknown'). In the filter's pooling (read time, nothing stored), a listing whose place has no reading at all gets `ASSUMED_NONE` (none, 'no limit stated — assumed none', source assumed), so an `equals restriction none` rule passes; a stated limit (page or listing quote), an affordable-housing operator (`config/operators.toml`) or your Verify answer still restricts. The market-rate operator list stays: it gives a none its basis, shown in the drawer ('no limit stated — assumed none ("operator: AvalonBay (market-rate)", operator)'); `place_restriction` now returns such a none (a page's market-rate quote or a market-rate operator) and the card badge skips none. Replaces, for goal evaluation only, the earlier strictness that an unread or name-only restriction is unknown -> needs_check (2026-11-25 'none needs real text'); stored readings still follow that rule, so the AGENTS.md 'unknown never rejects' rule still holds (an assumed none never fails anything).
- **2026-11-25: 'Count in net commute' box per commute rule (`in_net`).** User: "add a checkbox to see whether it should count towards total commute". `commute_any` and `near` take an optional boolean `in_net` (a `RULE_TYPES` optional key, so `/api/goals` advertises it and the editor shows the box only where it is listed; `validate_rule` rejects a non-boolean). Unset keeps today's behaviour: every must-have commute rule of either type counts, no nice-to-have does; the editor stores the key only when the box differs from that default, so existing goals are unchanged. `filter.net_rules` is the one selector (counted = must-haves not unticked, then nice-to-haves ticked); the `net_commute` rule (`Ctx.commute_rules`), the card's net fact, the net sort and the hover all read it. An unticked must-have's trip is sent apart as `commute_off`, never as an unknown term (an unknown is '+ ?', sorts later and makes the rule needs_check), and the hover lists it as 'not counted: unticked'. A nice-to-have left at its default is not listed (it never counted). Replaced: the net commute was fixed to the goal's must-have commute rules.
- **2026-11-25: Net total needs every counted time; mean is the default; two unused response keys dropped.** User (via the coordinator): a total with a counted trip missing is not a smaller sum. The card's total is now 'total needs check (n of m trips without a time)' and sorts after known totals (and after partial means / medians); mean, median and spread still use the trips that have a time; the `net_commute` rule was already needs_check on any unknown term (unchanged). The Net dropdown starts at mean (a saved view's choice still wins). From the 2026-11-25 audit, left then because tests read them: `/api/meta` `goals` (the page reads goals from `/api/goals`) and `/api/places` `amenities` with `filter.amenity_flags` (no page reads them since the require checkboxes became goal rules) are gone; the test that read the flags now checks the editor's actual path, `/api/goals` `amenities` presets judged as amenity rules. Replaced: 'total X + ? (n unknown)' as a partial sum sorted with the partial places; total as the default; the two keys.
- **2026-11-25: p99 error in the KNN accuracy table.** User asked for the worst-1% bound next to p95: `knn.accuracy` returns `p99` (nearest rank), None when fewer than `P99_MIN_N` = 100 places are scored (below that it is just the worst place); the table and the 'How commute times work' line show it, '–' when n is small. Replaced: p95 as the only tail figure.

- **2026-11-25: Zillow building pages fill a building's bedroom rows (baths, sqft, availability) where every plan agrees.** User approved: 424 active Zillow building rows (`<zpid>:Nbd`, 209 buildings) had no baths, since the search card's `unitsGroup` has only bedrooms and min price and the zpid's detail page is one representative unit's. Actors compared (Apify store, BRONZE prices): `delicious_zebu/zillow-property-details-scraper` $0.00097 a page, takes `/apartments/` building URLs (and, tried, `/b/<slug>/<lat>,<lon>_ll/`) and returns `page_type` 'building' with `floor_plans`; `maxcopell/zillow-detail-scraper` $0.003 a page, returns `listingFloorPlans` for the same URLs; sian.agency, api-ninja and cirkit detail scrapers $0.0025 to $0.005. Probe (3 buildings, both actors on `/b/..._ll/` URLs; zebu run hc6g8NK0Ihdy0Px60, maxcopell run 7lKfhYLZ1RY5I8EHZ): all three resolved to our building (`zillow_id` = our zpid) with every plan: The Tillery (1720 Villa St, Mountain View) 1 bd plans 1M/1J/1O/1B all 1 ba (677 to 813 sqft, from $4,924 = our row), 2 bd plans 2A/2E/2H/2D/2B all 2 ba (from $6,126 = our row); Blu Harbor (1 Blu Harbor Blvd, Redwood City) studio 1 ba 588 sqft $3,899, 1 bd 1 ba, 2 bd 2 ba, 3 bd PH1 2 ba 1,775 sqft $8,865 (all = our rows); Casa Alberta (1575 Tenaka Pl, Sunnyvale) 1 bd 1 ba 600 sqft $3,050, 2 bd 2 ba 1,000 sqft $4,025 and Townhouse 2 bd 1.5 ba 1,100 sqft $4,150. Chose zebu (a third of the price, same plans). Built as a raw-only fetch applied by `extract zillow` (see intake), not observations written by the fetch: `zillow_parse` writes `baths = None` on every search run and the text reading starts from the newest scraped observation, so a value written once would be lost at the next search or reading; applied by the reading it is re-applied each time. Plans that disagree on baths leave the row's baths unknown with the options in `extra.building_plans` rather than splitting into plan rows (the search would keep returning the bedroom row beside them). Replaced: a Zillow building's bedroom rows never get baths or sqft. Live (box): `zillow-buildings --max-usd 0.4` (run 570, 2 actor runs, 209 pages, $0.20): 187 pages ours, 0 failed, 22 `mismatched` and not read, all checked by hand: 19 are small buildings whose page lists no floor plans (550 Ortega Ave, 837 Cowper St, 715 Everett Ave, ...; 2 of them resolved to another address, 9 Forest Glen St -> 14 Forest Glen St and 2615 State Highway 82 -> '3390 Eichers Place'), 3 are the right building with plans but without our zpid among the current units (Solstice 299 W Washington Ave, Franklin 299, Marina Cove): left unknown, a hand check. Applied by the parking sweep's `extract zillow` (run 577, started after the deploy, so with this code): of the 424 building rows, 364 got `building_plans`; baths 339 (1 ba 217, 2 ba 118, 1.5 ba 3, 3 ba 1), sqft 200 (one sqft across the bed count's plans), available_from 364; 25 rows have plans that disagree on baths and stay unknown (The Shadows 750 N Shoreline Blvd 2 bd: 'Two Bedroom-One Bathroom D' 1 ba $4,295 and '...Two Bathroom E' 2 ba $4,895; Encinal Place 2 bd: 2X2, 2X1, 2X25 at 2, 1, 2.5 ba; The Montclaire 2 bd: Plan 2A 1 ba and 2B 2 ba, both 910 sqft); 60 rows have no plan (33 at the 22 mismatched buildings, 27 at buildings whose page lists no plan of that bed count, e.g. ARLO Mountain View studio and 1 bd). 56 of 364 rows' search price differs from the page's lowest plan price (mostly a few dollars, 2071340937:2bd $3,500 vs $4,000): the row keeps its own price. Goal effect (lease, 3bd/2+ba or 4bd/3+ba), listing level: 4 rows needs_check -> pass (The Shadows 3 bd 2 ba $6,295; Indigo 675 Bradford St 'Ultramarine' 3 bd 2 ba 1,481 sqft $7,031; 620 Veterans Blvd 3 bd; 1600 Villa St 3 bd); no lease place changed status. Cherry Orchard (250 W El Camino Real) 3 bd reads 3 ba from Plan 7.
- **2026-11-25: Nothing blocks the dashboard: per-thread SQLite connections, one background worker, stale-while-revalidate.** User: "make sure nothing blocks the backend ever." Before, one connection and one global lock served every request, and a whole-database result was computed inside the request under that lock, so after any edit one slow rebuild (the list 14 s, the Pipeline 15 s) held up every other request, writes included; a dashboard Google route held the lock for its network calls too. Now `aptf-web` runs `create_app(background=True)`: a connection per thread (`_ThreadConn`; WAL lets reads run beside a write), the lock only around short check-then-write sequences and the job state, and one worker thread (`schedule`) that rebuilds out-of-date results while requests answer from the last snapshot with `X-Updating` / `X-Snapshot-Age`; a watcher (every 3 s) rebuilds what a page read in the last 10 minutes as soon as the data version changes; at start the list, header counts, queues and Pipeline are queued first, the list first (the first list answered after 15 s, was 48 s with one warm-up task). The list is kept as JSON bytes and gzip per snapshot. The page shows 'updating…' and refetches the list and an open drawer 5 s later until fresh. Without `background` (tests, scripts) requests compute in place as before. Trade-off: after an edit the page shows the previous results, flagged, until the rebuild lands (about a minute under continuous load on the box copy) instead of waiting for them; a user's own route shows when the rebuild lands, not at once (supersedes the 2026-11-25 'Route with Google shows its result at once' for the served app). Load check on the box copy (25 s of requests right after an edit, so the whole rebuild ran throughout; ms p50 / p95 / max): one client: list 35 / 53 / 69, header status 14 / 18 / 18, drawer 160 / 194 / 394, Pipeline 8 / 10 / 13, Verify conflicts 28 / 35 / 1,311 (once), duplicates 6 / 8 / 229, KNN status 34 / 38 / 46, a write (annotation) 6 / 8 / 8; four clients at once: list 109 / 237 / 319, status 209 / 395 / 437, drawer 415-454 / 553-604 / 1,012, Pipeline 22 / 156 / 183, Verify 287 / 524 / 534, KNN status 71 / 261 / 658, write 13 / 102 / 119 (the worker and the clients share one Python interpreter, so CPU-bound answers slow by the share, but none waits for the rebuild). `tests/test_nonblocking.py` checks the list and a write each answer within 300 ms during a 3 s rebuild. Replaced: one shared connection and an RLock held around every computation.
- **2026-11-25: Own-site list (283 buildings): diagnosis, three free fixes, and a search-first + click-agent pilot (`aptf-intake find-plans`, report only).** User: "go to the Heltsley website and click floor plans ... Same with Colton ... Can we not agentically do this?", then "web-search '<building> <city> floor plans' first", then Noel Oaks (its site was recorded as the manager's homepage caprop.com).

  - **Fix 1, prices with cents.** `render._num_on_page` rounded Haiku's 3857.8 to 3,858, which the page never shows ('$3,857.80 /mo*'), so Savoy's 7 units were stored without a price. A re-read of the stored text (Haiku, $0.002, no Apify) now keeps all 7 (A7 1 bd 796 sqft $3,858 shown as rounded, available now).

  - **Fix 3, the address check** (`discover.vouched`). A page without the street address may still be the building's: you added the URL; its host is this place's own Google Places website (a host no other place uses, for a site not itself found from Places); or the distinctive words of the name are in the URL or title and the city is on the page. Never an error page or an aggregator.

Adding the error page, shared host and self-vouching rules left 25. The city rule then dropped rentvillaoaks.com, a 'Villa Oaks' in Santa Rosa.
    - Villa Oaks still passed: the page's text carries its operator's portfolio JSON, which names Sunnyvale buildings. The city check now ignores `{...}` blobs.

Locale ($6,050 Apt 1272D, through its SightMap embed after 3 hops). Briarwood (1 bd 556 sqft $3,116). The Marston (B14 2 bd 1,406 sqft $6,256). The Heltsley (HOME 202 Redwood 2 bd 920 sqft $3,525, now; income-restricted read in 1 of 2 runs: 'Annual Income Limits by Household Size' in the smoke run, none in pilot 1). - **2026-11-25: `building_units`, units in the building (apartment vs house).** User: "add a field for # units (regardless of size) to mainly differentiate apartments from houses. As a nice-to-have I would like apartment." A place-level numeric field from the best evidence at read time (`filter/building.py`): stated total (RentCafe `units_total`, a count in the text), house type -> 1 (duplex 2), else a floor from the units / plans one source lists, which is a lower bound and so never fails a `min` rule (rule 5). Type-only 'apartment' evidence gives no number (no invented counts); the card names the type. In `NUMERIC_FIELDS`, an editor preset 'apartment building (≥ N units)' (min 5), and the nice-to-have `building_units >= 5` added to `lease` and `temp` through the goals API. Trial and error: a first text pattern that also took 'N apartments' / 'N homes' matched names and prices ('1101 Apartments', '1/1 units: $1,999', 'two homes', 'will add an additional 79 apartments'), so the pattern now needs 'unit' next to a building noun (32 places, all checked by hand to be real counts); the places view's `n_units` was not used as the floor because it counts one plan under two sources' labels twice and craigslist reposts at a new price as new units. Not used: Zillow `matchingHomeCount` (raw files only; a reparse was not worth it for this). Replaced: nothing (new field).
- **2026-11-25: Loading spinner for rule changes and `X-Updating` answers.** User: "add a loading spinner when new changes to rules are being loaded." CSS-only spinner by the goal editor and on the count line while a preview or save is in flight (150 ms delay, list dimmed), on the count line while the list is `X-Updating`, and on the KNN status table and Pipeline panel while theirs are; replaced: plain 'updating…' text only.
- **2026-11-25: Group leases judged by price per person (goal-level `people`), not per bedroom.** User: "it's price per required bed, not price per bed, since it's a scenario with only 3 guaranteed roommates; the price/month should multiply accordingly; the requirement is still that 3 people have a budget of $2,500 each": a 4bd at $7,500 is fine for 3 people. A goal now takes an optional `people` (stored in its definition row beside label / rules / nice, absent when unset so existing goals are unchanged), and `price_per_person` (`price_month / people`, read with the same untrusted-price dependency as `price_month`) joins `NUMERIC_FIELDS`; it is computed at evaluate time from the goal (`Ctx.people`, `rules.fact`'s `people`, Verify's `goal_relevance(people=)`), never stored, so no second copy of a price. A rule on it under a goal without people is needs_check naming 'goal has no 'people'' (rule 5), not rejected by `validate_rule`. For a fixed group size `price_per_person <= 2500` with people 3 is the same test as `price_month <= 7500`; the gain is that the card and chip state each person's share, and the budget stays per person when the group size changes. Replaced: the group goals' total-rent caps (`lease` max price_month 7500, `goal3` max price_month 10000) and '/bed' as the price shown for every goal.
- **2026-11-25: `building_units`: a house type against apartment evidence is unknown.** After the first deploy, 64 places had a house or townhome type and apartment-building evidence at once (almost all a Zillow row with propertyType TOWNHOUSE on an `apartmentComplex` card; one Zillow house where Zumper says apartment building, 328 Bryant St). Giving them 1 would hard-fail a must-have `building_units >= N`, so they are now unknown (type 'townhome or apartment building', both evidences on hover). Replaced: the house type winning outright.
- **2026-11-25: The live dashboard's address stays out of the repo; a secrets check enforces it.** The 2026-11-24 entry on moving the dashboard to the NixOS machine named the live tunnel hostname in this public repo; it now reads `<your-tunnel-hostname>` (a forward commit: the earlier commit still has it, history is the user's call). `tests/test_no_secrets.py` and `scripts/check_secrets.sh` (`scripts/check_secrets.py`, standard library only; a pre-commit hook on the staged files and a commit-msg hook on the message, install in AGENTS.md rule 10) fail on a hostname under the personal domain, a Cloudflare tunnel token, or any word or hostname whose SHA-256 is in `scripts/secret_hashes.txt` (the base path and the live hostname, stored as hashes only). Findings name file and line, never the match. `.env.example` now lists every variable the code reads (`APTF_PORT`, `APTF_ROOT`, `APTF_CONFIG`, `APTF_DATA`, `APTF_DB` added, commented out). Replaced: rule 10 covered only the API keys.
- **2026-11-25: The drawer never waits for a rebuild; snapshots survive a restart.** User: "clicking on apartments is still hella slow"; measured live while an `aptf-intake extract` ran: a first drawer click 21.2 s, then 0.40 s and 0.16 s. Cause: three drawer inputs (current checks, website status, plan readings) were not built at start, so the first click waited for them behind every rebuild queued on the one worker (first in, first out). Now the worker's queue has priorities (a result a request waits for goes first), the start queues those three right after the list, and the list, header counts and queues, Pipeline, KNN status and every whole-database input of the drawer are pickled to `data/cache/<db name>/` (at most every 5 minutes each; 139 MB in 16 files on the box copy) and loaded at start: same code and data are current, otherwise served flagged while rebuilt. Also: inputs of a cached result are read inside it, never captured from the request (Verify's goal filter and the KNN accuracy closed over the request's snapshot, so a rebuild by the watcher could reuse old conflicts or trips and its hidden counts kept growing); a user's own route waits (no lock) for the rebuilt trips and goal results, so the reloaded drawer shows them at once again. Box copy, ms: first clicks on 5 random places right after the list 3,252 (inputs still being built for the first time) then 191 / 180 / 170 / 161; while the data changes every 2 s 184 / 158 / 827 / 167 / 168; right after a restart 212 / 181 / 187 / 168 / 179; the first list after a restart 1,443 (was 15-20 s). After the writes settle, every endpoint equals a fresh app (the Pipeline differs only in its `now`). Edit to fresh list on an idle app: 20 s (every endpoint 59 s more); with a write every 5 s the list stays flagged until about a minute after the last write. Replaced: first-in-first-out rebuilds; snapshots in memory only.
- **2026-11-25: Address text conflicts compare street lines, not raw text.** Found while building the Sonnet reconciler: 402 of the 517 open conflicts on the live database were address text conflicts, and most were formatting ('240 Ortega Ave' vs '240 Ortega Avenue', '707 Leahy St' vs '707 Leahy', a unit in the street line), because `extract._disagrees` compared the first comma part with only case and punctuation normalized. New `store.keys.same_street`: same street number, and the shorter `parse_address` street (suffixes and directions abbreviated, unit dropped) is the start of the longer. `extract` uses it for new readings, and `text_conflict_queue` applies it at read time to conflicts already stored (the observations are append-only). On the live data it drops 328 of the 402; the 74 left are real differences (ranges such as '1220 1244 University Drive' vs '1228 University Dr', a neighbouring number '942' vs '940 Rose Ave', Misora '388 Santana Row' vs '3060 Olsen Dr', a missing direction 'N Britton'). Replaced: first comma part compared as text.
- **2026-11-25: Goal gaps audit: the temp goal's undecided commutes routed; a stay place's old estimates no longer hide its building's trips.** User: "for all plans check what's failing and see if you can help with it."
  - **What was blocking temp.** Over a copy of the box database, `temp` had 242 places at needs_check, 223 of them waiting on transit to Downtown Palo Alto (173 on that alone). The places were Airbnb (97), Furnished Finder (108) and Zumper (21) stays, median 13 km away, none inside a station walk zone. Their bike and walk times were straight-line estimates, the transit estimate is always unknown (`commute.sources`), and no Google transit trip lay within `knn.RADIUS_M`. The transit cell was already ticked, but `aptf-commute sample` (CLI) plans only over walk-zone candidates, so it never reached them.
  - **Routing.** Routed with the existing `trips.route_plan` and an explicit plan (runs 574-576, 578-579: 237 Route Matrix elements, free tier). Then 4 places whose new KNN neighbours widened their range to straddle 25 min were routed. `lease`'s two Caltrain-walk straddles were settled by `aptf-commute sample --pair 'set:caltrain_stations|walk' --refine` (run 573, 8 elements).
  - **Result.** `temp` went from 42 pass / 242 needs_check / 2822 fail to 47 / 17 / 3042: 7 new passes, and 2 former passes (Airbnb "Palo Alto Retreat | Room A", transit 29 min; "Lovely Private Suite in Garden Setting", 28 min) now fail on their own Google time. `lease` went from 36 / 7 to 37 / 5 (715 Everett Ave settled at 19 min walk). Google gave no transit route for 2 places (Airbnb 1550926, Furnished Finder 1056219_1), which stay needs_check.
  - **Bug fixed.** A third, 1945 Latham St ~blueground, showed "transit not computed" while its building had a 47.5 min Google trip: `filter._Trips.get` returned a stay's own trips whenever it had any. 6 of 75 stay places still hold estimates from before the stay rule. Now the building's trip wins per (point, mode), and the stay's own trips fill only what the building lacks (1945 Latham St, needs_check to fail; no other goal status changes). Replaced: the stay's own trips, when present, shadowing all of its building's.
  - **Reported to the user, not changed:**
    - Blueground units are syndicated to Zillow, PadMapper and Apartments.com as `term` long at the same price as their short-term Zumper row. 5 of `lease`'s 37 passes are such twins: 108 Bryant St, 49 Showers Dr #1622, 1421 San Antonio St, 328 Bryant St and 715 Everett Ave 'Floor 2-ID2141'.
    - Zillow 447941826 (2675 Fayette Dr) is a whole building listed as 11bd/10ba at $99,000.
    - Identity splits: 365 Forest Ave 2A appears as a Craigslist place 800 m from its Zillow place, and Middle Park Apartments appears as separate rentcafe and direct places (21.1 vs 24.2 min to Caltrain).
    - Whole-unit Apartments.com and Zumper rows leave `private_bath` unknown, unlike the Airbnb and Furnished Finder parsers.
    - Two `temp` passes look wrong: Furnished Finder 829696_1 says "6-Months Min Beginning March" yet passes Jan 11-Feb 15, and 986607_1 is an "RV Tiny Home, Delivered & Setup, Any Bay Area" with no fixed location.
- **2026-11-25: Parking: two fixes after the full sweep.** (1) The street-only rule turned Airbnb's 'Free parking on premises | Free street parking' (44 listings, plus Zillow 'Additional Street Parking Available' style pairs) into offered no, because Haiku tagged only the kind street; now a reading is forced to 'no' only when every quote is about the street. Applied again from the cache (free). (2) Three own sites (Arlo Mountain View, Magnolia Square, Radius) read the boilerplate 'Price reflected is base rent and does not include mandatory or optional fees such as utilities, parking, storage' as paid parking; the parking description now says such a notice is null, and those three sites are read again (`render-restriction --parking --site`). Replaced: street-only from the kinds alone.
- **2026-11-25: Sonnet reconciles Verify conflicts (`aptf-verify reconcile`).** User: "use more Sonnet to see if it can reconcile any conflicts, feel free to use the rest of my Apify residential credits." Sonnet decides each disputed field from stored text first, then from current pages fetched through Apify; code accepts only a high-confidence answer whose verbatim quote writes the value (see Verify above). Accepted answers become machine checks (author `reconcile:sonnet`) that close the conflict at once; no 'suggested answer' UI was built, because the acceptance test already requires a quote stating the value and the user can override any machine check from Verify or the drawer (a newer user check wins). Not handled by design: addresses, stale Zillow items, prices on nightly listings, fields the user already checked. Machine checks are excluded from accuracy and from the sample queue so hand-checked numbers stay hand-checked. Replaced: conflicts resolved by hand only.
- **2026-11-25: find-plans stores its readings; base rent is the price, fees kept beside it.** User decisions after the pilot: store results the way render does; a found building page replaces a manager homepage or a missing site as the building's site, by appending to the site records; run step 1 (buildings with a known site, `--no-search`) and step 2 (no site: Google, then the click agent on hits) within $1.70 of Apify; store base rent as the price and keep the fee-inclusive total in extra, shown on hover.
  - Changes:
    - `findplans.store` and `--store`.
    - Search modes: `--no-search` and `--google-only`. `--reason` picks own-site list reasons.
    - Screenshots and vision are off unless `--shots` (0 right, 2 wrong readings in the pilots).
    - Pages are fetched in actor runs of 40 (4 GB, 40 min poll), since one 117-page run would outlast the poll.
    - render's reader returns `total_monthly`, checked on the page, stored as `extra.total_monthly` / `extra.required_monthly_fees` by render and find-plans.
    - `load_sites` keeps a find-plans site beside a hand-listed one of the same place, as it does for a URL you added.

- **2026-11-25: Parking: live numbers of the full sweep (box).** Snapshots `data/backups/apartments-2026-11-25-pre-parking.db` (before) and `...-pre-parking-reread.db` (before the free re-apply). `extract all` at schema v5: about 595 Haiku calls over 5,885 distinct texts (10,554 active listings), $3.84 notional on the subscription (Craigslist $1.41, Apartments.com $0.64, Zillow $0.34, Furnished Finder $0.29); one Airbnb batch failed with Haiku's invalid-JSON error (`error_max_structured_output_retries`), so the run stopped after 878 listings; a second run read the rest. `render-restriction --parking` over 142 own sites: $0.12, 518 plan rows. Active offer rows read by extract (7,195 with a parking reading): offered yes 6,877, no 300, unknown 2,698; of the yes, cost included 986, paid 572, unspecified 5,319; spots stated: 0 299, 1 374, 2 299, 3+ 35; a monthly price on 295 paid readings, $15 to $400 (median $75). Own-site pages: 422 plan rows yes (paid 149, included 22, unspecified 251). Goal rule fields after pooling: `parking_included` true 1,039, false 1,485, unknown 7,231. Examples: Craigslist 'One assigned onsite parking space for $100 a month' (paid, 1 spot, $100); 'Comes with 2 assigned parking spots in a gated carport area ... at no extra charge' (included, 2); 'Street Parking ONLY - no spots available on-site.' (no); Apartments.com '$150 - 225 Unassigned Garage Parking' (paid $150), 'Water/Sewer/Gas/Trash and 1 Parking Space included' (included, 1); Zillow 'Parking is offered based on availability and is subject to a fee.' (paid, no price); Airbnb 'Free parking on premises' (included); Zumper 'We offer one assigned parking spot per residence ... Additional parking is available for $50 a month' (1 spot; the extra space is $50); RentCafe 'Water, Sewer, Garbage & Parking Included'; own site Savoy 'Parking - Garage | $100.00 Per Rentable Item Monthly Optional'. Known misses, left as read (needs_check, never a wrong rejection): about 28 listings say the home 'includes' a space ('The apartment also includes an assigned covered parking space') with cost left unspecified. Who may rent, re-asked by the bump: 39 active rows changed value; improvements ('Shared room for men' none -> other, 'Maximum annual income based on household size' none -> income, "Income Requirements 3X's Rent" income -> none) and regressions (5 Zumper 'brand-new affordable apartment community' income -> none; The Acacia's 2 employer rows and 2 'Tax Credit Program' rows -> unknown), none of which changes a goal status: each regressed place still fails `restriction = none` from another listing's pooled limit. - **2026-11-25: Zillow's complex size (`matchingHomeCount`) is a `building_units` floor; new field `is_apartment_building`; `reparse --changed-only`.** Follow-ups to the `building_units` entries above (the user left the calls to us). (1) The Zillow parser keeps the search card's `matchingHomeCount` as `extra.complex_home_count` (only when given, so other rows hash as before). In the three raw search files (905 cards) it is on 256 `apartmentComplex` cards (213 with `unitsGroup`, 43 single-unit cards, values 2 to 80) and never on a house card; it counts the homes matching the search, and no field states the complex's total, so it is only a floor (the max over a place's cards, never a sum), used when it beats the listed plans. (2) `is_apartment_building` (equals true / false): 5+ units (value or floor), or a building-level apartment type with no house type at the place, is true; a house type that decided the value is false; anything else unknown. Why a separate field: 'apartment building' was the user's nice-to-have, and `building_units >= 5` left every type-only apartment complex (no count) at needs_check. Judgment calls: Google `condominium_complex` and Apartments.com communities count as building-level evidence; Zillow `groupType apartmentComplex` is sound evidence (tabulated over the raw cards: on 415 apartment, 256 building and 44 townhome cards, never on a single-family one; the 44 townhomes stay unknown as before). (3) A plain reparse appends every row of the file, which would bring back listings retired since (their new row's run id outranks the retiring run) and, with skips, mark the file's skipped listings superseded; `--changed-only` (see Intake) avoids both. Trial on a copy of the live database (runs 598 to 603, $0, 0 Claude calls): run4 / run21 / run156 appended only changed rows (run21: 39 appended, 94 unchanged, 6 inactive; run156: 310, 438, 12); Zillow active set, withdrawals (26), active Zillow rows with NULL beds or baths (85), the page fills of 411 Forest Ave (1 bd / 1 ba / 515 sqft) and 734 Cowper St (2 / 2 / 1,100) and every active Zillow row's reading all unchanged. Places with a Zillow `apartmentComplex` card: 694 (676 active); with a unit number 226 -> 300; the Zillow figure decides 124 (46 of them 5+): The Colton (1515 Broadway St, Redwood City) >= 80, Hearth (2870 Kaiser Dr) >= 27, Mylo Santa Clara >= 23, Hartwood >= 22, 360 Cowper St >= 17, 481 On Mathilda >= 3, 743 Roble Ave >= 2. Replaced: `matchingHomeCount` not parsed; the editor preset 'apartment building (≥ N units)' (now 'apartment building' = `is_apartment_building = true`, and 'units in building at least' for the count).

- **2026-11-25: A goal save re-judges only the goals, in about 1.4 s.** User: after a goal save the list stayed stale for about a minute until the one worker finished a full rebuild. A goal edit was an annotations row like any other, so it put every snapshot out of date (trips with KNN, goal inputs, places and listings views, the list, Pipeline) and the list then queued behind them on the one FIFO worker. Now the goal annotations are their own part of the data version, read only by goal-dependent results (all but `GOAL_FREE`); the save runs `goal_refresh` on a second one-thread lane (not the worker, which a running 15 s Pipeline rebuild would hold) over the last goal-free snapshots, re-judging only changed goals, and answers when the new list is in place; a guard keeps an older-goal rebuild from overwriting it. `results()` now judges over the cached `goal_inputs` (no second read of the listings), and the list is split into a goal-free `place_rows` and the goal overlay `with_goals`. Measured on a copy of the box database (TestClient, `/api/pipeline` and `/api/status` read just before): PUT to a fresh `/api/places` 15.5 s and 48.7 s (a second save queued behind the first's rebuilds) before, 1.3-1.6 s after (inside `goal_refresh`: re-judging the edited goal 0.25-0.55 s, overlay 0.25-0.65 s, JSON and gzip of 26 MB 0.4 s; re-judging all 3 goals without reuse was 1.5-1.8 s); create 1.3 s, delete 0.8 s; a save landing while the worker runs a full rebuild (a place note just posted) still answers in 1.3 s with the new goals in the list (flagged `X-Updating` until the note's rebuild ends, 40 s); the swapped list equals a cold app's. After a restart (list from disk): a save in the first seconds, before `goal_inputs` and `place_rows` are built, falls back to the worker (20 s on the copy); 30 s after the start a save answers in 1.4 s. The goal version sits in a goal-dependent snapshot's edits as a tagged `("goal", id)` item; an untagged snapshot (older code) is never treated as a goal version and `goal_refresh` leaves it to the worker. Replaced: one background worker for every rebuild, and any annotation (goal edits included) putting every snapshot out of date.
- **2026-11-25: `not_operator` rule; Blueground's long-term reposts excluded from `lease`.** User: Blueground furnished units are reposted on Zillow, PadMapper and Apartments.com as long-term listings at the same price as their short-term Zumper rows; 5 of `lease`'s passes were such reposts (108 Bryant St, 49 Showers Dr #1622, 1421 San Antonio St, 328 Bryant St, 715 Everett Ave 'Floor 2-ID2141'). Checked against the stored data first: the reposts sit at the bare place (the Zumper rows at the `~blueground` stay place) and carry no operator; the stored signals are Apartments.com `management_company` 'Blueground' (12 rows untagged), PadMapper ids equal to a Blueground Zumper id (12, Zumper and PadMapper share listing ids), and for Zillow nothing in observations (3 of the 5 have no ID in the address and no 'blueground' in their data) but the detail page's lister: 68 active Zillow rows' pages say brokerName 'Blueground Furnished Apartments', agentName 'Blueground US'. Rejected: an address pattern ('Floor 2-ID2141', '#Id1646', 'ID1073176P'; it found 16 of the 68 with a strict regex); a twin rule (same beds and price as a Blueground row at the place's `~blueground` stay; 52 hits, all 5 targets, but it misses on price drift, 715 Everett 1bd $3,380 Zillow vs $4,430 Zumper, and had one unconfirmed hit, 550 Ortega Ave 2bd, a Zillow building row with no lister); pooling per place (would fail The Crossings' own listings). So: per-listing lister names matched against the existing Blueground entry of `operators.toml`, whose `names` gain 'Blueground US' and 'Blueground Furnished Apartments' (no shape change; the box has no private `config/operators.toml`, so it reads the example). Tagged on the box: 124 Zumper, 77 Apartments.com, 44 Zillow units, 12 PadMapper. New rule type `not_operator {name}` (no equals/exclude rule could say it); it passes when nothing names the operator. Goal `lease` gains `not_operator Blueground` through `PUT /api/goals/lease`, nothing else changed; `temp` (a furnished short stay, where Blueground is wanted) keeps no such rule and is unchanged. Dry run of this code on the live data before the deploy: no place changes without the new rule; with it `lease` 38 -> 33 pass (38 / 4 / 3,064 -> 33 / 4 / 3,069), exactly the five places above; `temp` 53 pass / 8 needs check unchanged. Replaced: operators used only for who may rent.
