The sentence is a pricing example (the cheapest stay window), so using it turns unknown into a wrong fail. The Zumper parser no longer reads it; the two Zumper files were reparsed again ($0), so those 94 rows are back to no date. Temp: 2721 Midtown #1895 (sentence: December 6, 2026), 918 Webster #2154 (August 27, 2027), 920 Webster #1650 (May 2, 2027) and 838 Roble #1866 (June 24, 2027) return to needs_check on availability (#1866 also fails price_month 5,230 > 4,000 on its 2 bd plan; its studio plan is undecided). Replaced: the move-in sentence as available_from.
- **2026-11-25: 'no listings', a place outcome of its own; needs check means unknown or disputed only.** The lease header read '3,288 places · 37 pass · 187 check · 3,064 fail', but `/api/places` gave only 5 places a real needs_check: the other 182 had no goal result at all (only site_info rows, or a building row with nothing for rent: rentcafe 96, google_places 95, direct 33; 160 with no active listing) and the page counted a missing result as check. The user's rule, per goal and place: no offers at all -> no listings; a beds/baths plan that fits but every fitting one has no price or is marked unavailable -> no listings; plans of which none fits -> fail; otherwise as before. Built in `filter.place_outcome` (definitions under filter, Outputs); listings keep pass / needs_check / fail, so routing candidates, sampling hinges and Verify's goal relevance are untouched. 'Unavailable' reads structured fields only (Apartments.com: 8 '0 Available units' rows, 2 'No availability' plan names; own-site labels: 64 'Unavailable on selected date', 6 'Call for details', 3 'Contact for Availability', 1 'WAITLIST CLOSED'); descriptions are never read for it (Craigslist's 72 'unavailable' hits are about parking). Found on the way: own-site price-less plans (725 rows at 91 places) were dropped before any rule ran, so they could neither fail a place nor make it no listings; they are now judged for fit only (`goal_inputs` `unpriced`). A row with neither beds nor a price is no plan, so an empty building row or a gone Zillow card no longer holds a place at needs_check beside plans that fail (the active-first test's all-gone place p2 is now a fail on its 2bd). Live, same data and config (origin/main ec91d87 vs 5ccaaf8, both run on the box with its goals and `config.example`; equal to the deployed `/api/places`), places per goal as pass / check / fail / no listings: lease 33 / 4 / 3,069 / (182 without a result, shown as check) -> 33 / 0 / 3,086 / 169 (161 no offers known, 7 no price, 1 unavailable); temp 52 / 14 / 3,040 / (182) -> 52 / 14 / 3,061 / 161; goal3 3 / 0 / 3,103 / (182) -> 3 / 0 / 3,123 / 162. No pass changed, and no listing's status changed (0 of 9,755 per goal), so Verify relevance, sampling hinges and routing candidates see the same results. Lease transitions: no result -> no listings 161, no result -> fail 21 (Garwood, St Croix, Park Taylor, Mountain View Town Center II: own-site plans without price, none fits), needs_check -> no listings 4 (Noel Oaks 1 fitting plan, Six50Live 5, Springline 4, Latham Square 4: each was 'price_per_person unknown' only because no price is known), fail -> no listings 4, the judgement cases (ARLO Mountain View 'fitting plan unavailable ("Unavailable on selected date")'; Fayette Townhouse, The Marc, Landsby: priced plans that fail beside a fitting own-site plan without a price; goal3: Six50Live). No offers known, e.g.: Olive West Apartment and Calderon Apartments (google_places), Avenue Two Apartments (rentcafe). Near-miss audit (the user's clarification: a value just over a limit is a fail, never a check): 0 of the 23 needs-check places before this change were margin cases; each was an unknown field (price_per_person, private_bath, availability, baths, beds) or a transit time not computed. Exact values have no band (Google 21.1 vs 21 and $2,612 vs $2,500 fail; tests pin both). Estimates keep their 0.67 / 1.5 band and KNN its 0.5 min pad and `KNN_FAIL_MARGIN` 0.9 (the user's earlier decision): a range is not this place's own time, so near the limit it is an unknown. Measured: 1,916 KNN rule evaluations lie wholly over their limit yet inside the 0.9 band; a margin of 1.0 would change 1 listing and 0 places, so it stays. Cost: `evaluate_goal` judges the 725 extra rows, about +0.1 s a goal on the box (2.7 s -> 3.0 s for three goals; `goal_inputs` 9.3 s unchanged). Replaced: a missing place result shown as needs check, and price-less own-site plans ignored by the goals.
- **2026-11-25: Reconciler run 609 on the live data, and three rules from it.** 112 open conflicts examined after the address fix (72 address-only and 5 stale items skipped): 44 closed (9 of 15 text, 11 of 46 cross, 24 of 50 too-cheap by their price or kind room; the one pending-render item stayed open), 87 fields written (net after the retractions below: 9 text, 2 cross, 22 too-cheap); rejected: 50 undecided, 43 medium confidence, 26 'kind' other than room (a whole unit: no row needed), 12 value not in the quote, 6 multi-price page without the unit named. Goal-relevant: 3 conflicts in all (temp, Airbnb private bath), 2 closed; lease and goal3 had none. Pages: 78 through the datacenter proxy (58 read, $0.084), 15 through residential (14 read; RentCafe answers 403 to the datacenter proxy but not to residential), plus a pilot ($0.007 + 8 residential, 6 of them blocked Zillow / Apartments.com pages). Real Apify charge of all four actor runs $0.473 (pilot $0.012 + $0.061 residential; run $0.087 datacenter + $0.313 residential, about $0.02 a residential page); the run's own `est_cost` said $0.789 because it added the residential estimate on top of a charge that already held most of it (fixed: the larger of the two). Sonnet notional $2.90 for the run, $0.11 for pilot 1, pilot 2 not captured (estimate about $0.3). Problems found in the written rows, and fixed: (1) a too-cheap price confirmed while Sonnet was only medium-sure the listing was a room (Craigslist 7999842789, '$1200/Month' for a room in a shared 5-bed home: the item closed with the listing still a $240/bed house; 8006082921 a $995 co-living suite): price now needs a high-confidence kind; (2) 'available now' rewrote past dates to today: two dates on or before today now count as the same; (3) a Craigslist 2bd / 900 sqft unit overrode an own-site 1bd / 614 sqft plan grouped into the same unit (p_e20682839596132a): own-site rows are never corrected by a machine. The bad rows were retracted with the new `--retract` (4 rows). A review then found a fourth problem: cross conflicts won by a source quoting itself (RentCafe's page restating its $2,595 over Zillow's $2,395, whose page we cannot read; Craigslist 1320 sqft over Zillow 1137; Apartments.com's text restating 3.5 baths over PadMapper's 3): such a quote proves nothing about the other source, so it is now refused (see Verify above), and 32 more rows were retracted (those decisions plus the six 'available now' rewrites, now no conflicts at all). What stands: Sonnet's reading of a listing's own text against that listing's field (Airbnb 'Shared bathroom' vs private_bath true, Zillow's '3 bedrooms, 2.5 bathrooms' vs its own 2 baths, Furnished Finder 'The actual property is unfurnished.'), and too-cheap items. Replaced: nothing (first run).
- **2026-11-25: Two availability dates already past are no conflict.** Found in reconciler run 609: 15 of the open cross-source conflicts were availability dates both on or before today (Apartments.com 2026-11-24 vs the own site's 2026-11-25 for one unit; Craigslist 11-04 vs PadMapper 09-02), which Sonnet rightly could not decide because both mean 'available now'. `verify._differs` now treats such a pair as agreeing (the reconciler already compared them that way). Live: cross-source conflicts 36 -> 29, those on availability 16 -> 0. Replaced: any two different dates conflicted.
- **2026-11-25: A Sonnet reading survives a re-scrape while it is the newest reading and the text is unchanged.** User: "Sonnet readings must survive a re-scrape" (Furnished Finder 190174_1: Sonnet read private_bath true, quote 'One with ensuite bathroom the other has sole use of the non ensuite bathroom.', run 628; the cache is keyed by model, so the next scrape's default Haiku reading would have put the row back to unknown). `extract` now looks up each listing's newest reading (`extra.extracted.model`, highest observation id); when its model outranks the run's (`discover.MODEL_RANK`) and that model's cache entry exists for the current text, the listing gets the cached Sonnet answer and `model: sonnet` again, with no call (`kept_stronger` in the run counts). Because the hash covers schema version and entry text, 'text unchanged' needs no separate test. Not done: timestamps in the cache (the observations already order the readings). On the live copy 190174_1's newest reading is still run 628 (Sonnet), so the rule holds it. Replaced: the run's model only, so any later Haiku run reset a Sonnet reading.
- **2026-11-25: Three address-parsing bugs behind split buildings and a mixed unit; `extract` geocodes the addresses it writes.** User asked about three cases. (1) *365 Forest Ave 2A, Palo Alto*: Craigslist's raw item has no address, only the post's pin (37.4443, -122.1497, about 780 m east of Zillow's 37.445225, -122.15842) and the text 'Property Address 365 Forest Ave 2A | Palo Alto, CA 94301'. The text reading (run 595, cached answers only) rewrote the address as '365 Forest Ave 2A, Palo Alto, CA 94301', a string never sent to Google (standalone `aptf-intake extract` rebuilt identity without geocoding; 33 address strings were uncached, 32 of them from that run), and the parser read the bare '2A' after 'Ave' as the city ('365 forest ave|2a'), so the ad left Zillow and Zumper's place (p_40c9dd…, unit 2a) for its own (p_29d182…), placed on its own pin. Before that the pipe form was geocoded and the ad sat with Zillow. Fix: a token with a digit right after the street type is the unit (not a ZIP; a route number after Highway/Route stays in the street; any later token with a digit is not a city either), and `extract` geocodes new strings before it rebuilds identity. (2) *Middle Park Apartments, Menlo Park*: RentCafe writes '1024, 1026, 1028 Middle Avenue, Menlo Park, CA 94025'; the parser took '1024' as the street, '1026' as a unit in its own comma slot and '1028 Middle Avenue' as the city; Google answered '1028 Middle Ave 1024 1026' but the house-number check wanted 1024, so the parser's reading stood. Fix: a leading list of house numbers (comma, '&', 'and', spaced hyphen, or space separated) is one street at its first number (`keys.house_numbers`, `NUM_LIST_RE`; '4180-4190' stays a range), the city is the next comma part that is not another street, and Google's answer counts when its number is any listed one; the 24 cached fallbacks for list addresses were deleted and geocoded again. Same-site could never catch it: the RentCafe row names no website. (3) *850 Roble*: the own site's rows carry the address of the Craigslist ad that named the site ('850 Roble Ave #C'), and `unit_key` preferred that address unit over the floor plan, so the 1bd/614 sqft plan joined the 2bd/900 sqft unit C. Fix: own unit, then floor plan, then the address's unit. Effect on the live data (deployed; snapshot `data/backups/backup-2026-11-25-identity.db`; 57 geocoding calls: the 33 uncached strings and the 24 list fallbacks, 54 placed precisely, month at 2,433 of 4,900; identity appended 69 links): 45 listings changed place, 18 changed unit only, 36 place keys vanished (no annotation on any; 13 had Google trips, not recomputed), 15 new places got free estimates. Merges into existing buildings: RentCafe Middle Park into 1028 Middle Ave (its 3bd plan now shares unit threebedroomtwobath with the own site's), '415 - 425 S Bernardo' (RentCafe, Zumper) into 425 S Bernardo (7 listings), '1305, 1309 Marshall' into 1309 Marshall St, '3875, 3833 Park Blvd' into 3833 Park Blvd, '120 - 122 East Creek' into 122 E Creek Dr, '848,850,852,854 Jordan' into 848 Jordan Ave, '670,680,690,2333 Eastridge Ave,680 Sharon Park Dr' into the 27-listing 680 Sharon Park Drive place (Google's pick; one complex on two streets), Craigslist '555 W Middlefield Rd, 1, 555 W Middlefield' (city was '555 w middlefield') into 555 West Middlefield (40 listings), '225 Vera Ave. #3, 225 Vera Ave. 3' with Zumper, '1500 Laurel Street +, 1275 White Oak Way' with Zillow; Craigslist 365 Forest Ave 2A back with Zillow and Zumper (unit 2a). Unit-only: 535 Everett's three plans (were all unit #1230), Balmoral's four (#100), 850 Roble's 1bd, 545 University's two (#4), RentCafe '#340' and '#14' plans, Zumper/PadMapper Whitney Drive plans (were '/2485'). The merged places' commute rows stay those computed at the old pin (trips are keyed by place): Middle Park walk to Menlo Park Caltrain still 24.2 min (Google, at the pin before RentCafe joined). Not changed by this: user-linked rows keep the unit their manual merge gave them (Middle Park's three inactive `middle-park-apartments-2` plans stay in unit c). Seen, not fixed: a Craigslist pin is often far from the building (30 of 155 places with Craigslist and another source have the Craigslist pin over 150 m from the others' mean, vs 23 of 409 for Zillow) and `places` averages pins, so a merged place still moves toward it (365 Forest's merged pin is about 260 m east of Zillow's, Middle Park's about 180 m from RentCafe's); not only Craigslist (Zillow 23 of 409, RentCafe 14 of 196), and moving pins strands place-keyed trips, so it is a separate decision.
- **2026-11-25: Transit with no time reads the nearest Google times at any distance (an estimate); a Google row with no route never hides a time; the candidates scope leaves the walk zones.** User: places where Google found no transit route should use the fallback estimate, not 'not computed', and fail through the estimate band, not a hard rule; new stays' transit should use KNN like other stays, not wait on auto-routing.
  - **Baths unknown checked, nothing to fix.** A priced offer whose beds fit and whose baths are unknown is already needs_check (`min baths` and `size_options` both say 'baths unknown'), and `place_outcome` keeps it as an offer. Live (box, all three goals): 0 places where such an offer, failing no other rule, ended fail or no listings. Of `lease`'s 10 priced 3bd+ rows with baths unknown, 8 fail another rule (Caltrain walk, price per person) and Laurel Grove's two Zillow rows are beside an own-site pass. Under a hypothetical 2bd/2ba goal, Encinal Place (Zillow only, 2bd baths unknown) is needs_check and The Montclaire passes on its own site's Plan 2B; The Shadows fails because its own site prices only 2bd/1ba units (Plan D) and own-site-wins makes Zillow's 2bd row (baths unknown) not decide. A regression assertion covers the case.
  - **Why transit had no time.** The straight-line source has no transit speed, so every place's transit estimate row is NULL (3,731 rows to Downtown Palo Alto); a place with no Google-routed place within 400 m had none, and `best_trips` let a Google row with NULL minutes (no route: Airbnb 1550926, Furnished Finder 1056219_1) outrank everything.
  - **Candidates compared** (leave-one-out over the 424 Google transit trips to Downtown Palo Alto, limit 25, wrong = the band's verdict disagrees with Google): a straight line `a + b x km` (detoured km) wrongly fails 6-9 places whatever the constants (e.g. 10 + 2.6 km: 234 fail, 7 wrongly, Google 13.6-24.9 min at 11-22 km: Caltrain); KNN-3 ranges at any radius: 256 fail, 1 wrongly; the median of the 3 nearest through the estimate band: 203 fail, 0 wrongly, 24 pass, 1 wrongly; the same restricted to places whose nearest routed place is over 400 m (the ones that need it): 97 fail, 0 wrongly; 8 pass, 1 wrongly. Chosen: the last (`knn.far_estimate`: provider `estimate`, `far`, its neighbours and their km; the reason says 'transit Google found no route; 50 min (estimate: nearest 3 Google routes 3.8-3.9 km away)').
  - **Only a row with no time gets it**, never a key the place has no row for: a first try filled every missing key and put 'walk to caltrain_stations 26 min (estimate: nearest 3 Google routes 13.7-16.0 km away)' on 32 `lease` places (the nearest places with a walk time to a far station are near that station). `best_trips` now ranks a trip with a time above one without, then by provider.
  - **Sampling.** The candidates scope (CLI `sample`, dashboard 'search candidates') dropped its walk-zone filter: 666 candidates instead of 224 (temp 391, 244 outside every walk zone). Dropping it alone made a cover over all ticked cells ~2,800 elements (206-209 picks per cell, out-of-zone `lease` candidates included), so cover in that scope is per pair over the candidates of goals with a commute rule on the pair (`sampling.goal_cover_plan`): Downtown Palo Alto transit 4 picks / 2 uncached elements (`temp`); each `lease` cell 105 picks (Meta HQ drive 105 elements, Downtown Palo Alto bike+train 313), shown in the dry run, not run. KNN already reached stays outside walk zones (temp candidates' transit: 313 own Google, 74 KNN, 4 with no time).
  - **Effect** (box, old and new code on the same data back to back): `temp` 52 pass / 14 needs_check / 3,056 fail -> 52 / 10 / 3,060: Airbnb 1550926 'Cute private cottage near Stanford' (10.6 km; Google no route; 50 min estimate from routes 3.8 km away), Furnished Finder 1056219_1 'Beautiful Los Altos Retreat' (15.7 km; no route; 57 min, 1.9-2.2 km), g_f9e681afe5a45f6a (21.8 km; 61 min) and a new stay g_0e13fdbcc903e8d6 (40 min from routes 0.5-0.7 km away) go needs_check -> fail. `lease` and `goal3`: 0 changes. `best_trips` 2.4 -> 2.6 s on the box.
  - Replaced: transit 'not computed' wherever no routed place lay within 400 m; a Google row with no route outranking a time; the candidates scope limited to walk zones.
- **2026-11-25: The reconciler reads every page we already hold (stored pages), and one listing can be read on request.** User: "Everything should feed into the reconciler" (live Apify fetches of zillow.com and apartments.com answer 403, and Apify credit is nearly gone). Each listing's stored raw pages become quotable evidence blocks with their fetch date (see Verify); the quote gate and the self-quote rule are unchanged. - **2026-11-25: Apartments.com and Zillow prices are base rent; their fee-inclusive totals were being stored as the price.** User: Landsby (100 Aspen Way) has the wrong price; Verify's sample showed its Apartments.com 1 bd 577 sqft at $4,221. Evidence (raw files scraped 2026-11-24; the rows' 11-25 `observed_at` is a reparse): memo23's item (run 22) labels the One Bedroom plan 'Starting at $4,221 Total Monthly Price' (577-729 sqft, 5 units) while its `rentals` give `Rent` 4,175 and `MinTotalMonthlyPriceExact` 4,220.06; the older epctex item (run 3) gives `basePrice` '$4,175 - 4,985' and `totalPrice` '$4,220 - 5,030'; Zillow's card (run 4) gives the 1 bd `minPrice` 4,221 with `listPriceIncludesRequiredMonthlyFees` true, `totalRequiredMonthlyMinFee` 46 and `minBaseRent` 3,945 (the studio). So the same plan sat at $4,175 (epctex) and $4,221 (memo23, Zillow), and the 3 bd at $7,790 and $7,836. Landsby's own site and RentCafe show no prices ('Ask for pricing'). The right figure under the base-rent rule is $4,175, and it is the One Bedroom family's starting price, not necessarily the 577 sqft unit's. Cause: the base-rent rule (find-plans entry above) covered own-site readings only; memo23's `rentLabel` and Zillow's card prices are totals whenever the building is in a fee-disclosure state. Survey of the raw files: 946 memo23 plan entries (all raw files, repeats included) carry a 'Total Monthly Price' label (37 buildings; in all of them every rental agrees on one fee: Vasara $130, Encasa $95, The Clara $61, 201 Marshall $54, Landsby and The Village Residences $46, Highwater and Lynhaven $43, Revela $34, Sevens and Rowe at Pear Village $31, ... Misora and Naya $6, Tamarind Square $5; 707 Leahy and Woodland Park $0); their units' prices are totals too (The Clara unit $3,934 against `Rent` $3,873). Plain labels ('Starting at $3,425') are base rent (Avalon Silicon Valley: `Rent` 3,425, total 3,516). Zillow: 24 building cards (and one single home) say their price includes fees, and on each of them `minPrice - minBaseRent` equals the fee. Fix: see intake, 'Base rent is the price'; the web plan table's '+fees' hover now takes `total_monthly` from any source, not only the own site. Zillow's `required_monthly_fees` text (the text reading's input) still reads `property.rental` only: reading the fees on `property` too changed the text of 117 active Zillow rows and dropped their cached readings on the box copy (Indigo's 3 bd went from pass to needs_check, baths unknown), so that stays for a later text-reading pass. Measured on a copy of the box database (identity rebuilt first, then `reparse --changed-only` of the 4 Apartments.com and 3 Zillow raw files that hold active rows): 602 active listings change price, 535 Apartments.com and 67 Zillow, in 37 buildings (Highwater 69, Sevens 62, Vasara 60, The Village Residences 51, The Clara 47, Passero 40, Lynhaven 31, Lume 26, Savoy 22, Encasa 21, ... Landsby 8: 1 bd $4,221 -> $4,175, 3 bd $7,836 -> $7,790; Zillow Indigo 3 bd $7,031 -> $7,015, Central Park 1 bd $3,572 -> $3,542); cached readings all kept (Zillow 1,097 with a reading before and after); no place changes outcome under any goal (temp, lease, goal3). Landsby under `lease` stays 'no listings' (its four own-site 3 bd plan rows have no price); its priced 3 bd fails at $2,597 a person (was $2,612) against the strict $2,500. Replaced: memo23 `rentLabel` and Zillow card prices stored as given.

New rule type `parking_cost` (see Filter), a `parking cost (low to high)` sort, the card's '🅿 +$150/mo for 2 spots'. The cross conflicts (22) barely moved: a stored page is the same source's data, so only a page that disagrees with its own row can win (dry run on a copy: Zumper's stored 'base_max_price: 5500' against its row's $5,628 beside Zillow's $5,500). **829696_1:** `--listing furnishedfinder:829696_1 --field available_from` gave 2027-03-01 from '6-Months Min Beginning March.' only at medium confidence (Sonnet: the stored item's available-now flag conflicts), so the gate wrote nothing and the listing still passes temp; first try before the month rule: undecided ('no year or day'). Replaced: nothing.
- **2026-11-25: A point added to a set reaches the places already estimated; sampling counts a set as routed only at the current nearest point.** User: "walk to nearest Google bus" did not change after 5 GBus stops were appended to the box's `google_bus_stops.csv` (46 -> 51: Menlo Park CalTrain, San Carlos Caltrain, San Mateo Event Center, Burlingame Golf Center, Millbrae BART) and a deploy. Suspected first: the dashboard's on-disk snapshots (`data/cache/apartments/`) keyed without the config. Ruled out: `raw_version` already puts every file mtime under `CONFIG_DIR` (recursive, `points/` included) in the edits part of each result's version, and the snapshots keep it. An uncached `best_trips` + `nearest_by_set` over the live database gave the same old answers (the place 57 m from Menlo Park CalTrain: Palo Alto Caltrain, 26.1 min Google; 95 m from San Carlos Caltrain: Redwood City P&R, 42.5 min estimate; 303 m from Millbrae BART: Redwood City P&R, 290.9 min estimate). The cause: nearest-point times come only from stored `trips` rows, and with `k = 1` each place had a row only to its then-nearest stop; `estimate_missing` skipped every place with an estimate to any current point, so the 3,722 places with bus-stop estimates never got one to a new stop (only 8 places intaken since had one to Menlo Park CalTrain, none Google). Now `missing_estimates` lists the places lacking an estimate for some (point, mode) the config asks (each set's k nearest by each mode); `estimate_missing`, the Pipeline estimates stage and a new dashboard check (at start and whenever `point_sets.toml` or a point file's mtime changes; marks every snapshot out of date when it wrote trips) use it. Second, the same bug in sampling: `routed` counted a set pair as done for a Google trip to any point of the set, so the 696 places with a Google walk to an old stop would never be picked for a new nearer one; it now means the place's fastest point (what the list shows) is its own Google trip. Old trips to a stop that is no longer nearest stay (append-only) and still compete by minutes in `nearest_by_set`, which is right: the fastest stop wins. Replaced: estimates only for places with none; 'routed for a set' = a Google trip to any of its points.
  - **Recompute on the box** (database snapshot `data/backup-2026-11-25-busstops.db` first). The deployed dashboard's start check found 542 incomplete places and appended 1,440 estimate trips (run 667; 543 to the five new stops, the rest other sets' nearest points that had changed the same way); `missing_estimates` then listed none. Places whose fastest Google bus stop is one of the five new ones: 9 -> 521. Sampling `set:google_bus_stops|walk` over the 665 candidates: cover 112 places, 112 Route Matrix elements (coverage within 400 m 301 -> 517), then refine 10 places, 4 uncached elements; month's matrix elements 6,030 -> 6,146 of the 9,000 cap, free tier. - **2026-11-25: Craigslist addresses: what the actor never gives, what Haiku missed, and a text address never moves a pinned row off its building.** User (Verify sample, Sharon Grove, p_a2628d082a0b473a): the Craigslist post 7995924216 / 8003715591 shows address '?' though 'the post has the address'. Findings: (1) neither post has a street address in anything we store: the title, the whole description (2,912 and 2,833 characters, under the cap; it names 'Sharon Grove Apartments | West Menlo Park, CA' only) or any field of the raw item. solidcode/craigslist-scraper returns the same 25 keys on all 4,476 stored items (url, postId, title, priceUsd, location 'menlo park', latitude / longitude, attributes, description, imageUrls, ...), with no map-address field, so the address line Craigslist shows above its map is never in our data. That is a blocker of the actor, not of the parser or Haiku; unblocking needs an actor that returns the map address (not probed, rule 9). The rows sit at the right place anyway, joined by their pin (geo_near_address). (2) Over the 1,886 active Craigslist rows, 425 had no address: 384 hold no numbered street address a regex finds, 10 had one that Haiku gave but `check_answer` refused (it took the part before the first comma and needed `^\d+\s`: 'Extended Stay America Suites - 3 Circle Star Way', '848, 850, 852, 854 Jordan Ave', '11720-11780 San Pablo Ave - El Cerrito', '153a Haas Ave'), about 12 where Haiku answered null although the text has it ('850 Calderon Ave' as the title, 'Tuscany / 3229 El Camino Real, Santa Clara', '353 Main Street Family Apartments', 'Office: Located at 7375 Rollingdell Drive'), and 7 whose address sat past the parser's 3,000-character description cut ('3900 Business Center Dr', '2525 Bayfront Blvd' in the signature). Fixes: `street_from` keeps the answer from its street line on (the first comma or ' - ' part starting with a house number, letter suffix and range included; '1325 40th St' too); the address wording asks for it wherever the text gives it (title, body, a 'Community Location' or office line, a signature); a test batch of the 12 texts gave 3 of 12 with the old wording and 9 of 9 real ones with the new (the Sharon Grove posts stay null, rightly); the Craigslist parser keeps 6,000 characters and `entry_text` sends up to `MAX_TEXT`; `extract --address-missing` reads again only rows still without an address, replacing their cached answers (`--cached-only` applies them for free). Placement guard: a row with no address whose pin joined an address-keyed building keeps it; a text address on another street line goes to `extra.extracted.conflicts` (Verify) instead of the row. Found on a copy: without the guard 3 rows left their correct building, Napa Cove (100 Toscana Dr) for its office '100 Roja Lane', '3488 Agate Dr' for '3508-3518 Agate Dr' (315 m from the pin) and '777 Tennessee' for '777 TENN, Potrero Hill'. With it, on a copy of the live database (cached answers only): no-address rows 425 -> 408 (30 gained; 13 rows whose reparsed text was not yet asked lose their reading until the next `extract`), 23 rows move, all from a coordinate-only place to their stated address: 18 at 0 m from their own pin, 3229 El Camino Real 80 m, '201 Marshall' joining 201 Marshall St 122 m away, 850 Calderon Ave joining the building other sources hold 333 m from the post's pin, Extended Stay 3 Circle Star Way 784 m (Google's street-level reading of the address; the pins are the poster's). Haiku: about 130 calls on the subscription ($0.90 notional) across the trials; Google: 20 new geocodes. Replaced: first-comma-part address check, 'street address as written' wording, 3,000-character Craigslist description.

- **2026-11-25: A building written as an address range absorbs the places inside it that share its name or operator.** User: University Gardens, Menlo Park was four places: RentCafe's '1222-1244 University Dr' (plans 2 Bed x 1.5 Bath $4,495, 3 Bed x 3 Bath $5,695) and three townhouses each listed by Zillow and Craigslist (1226 and 1228, 3bd/3ba $5,695; 1242, 2bd/1.5ba $4,495), all named 'University Gardens'. The address-list fix earlier today keeps '4180-4190' a range on purpose, so nothing joined them; same-site could not (no listing names a website). New rule `auto-merge:address-range` (see identity). Why name or operator is required: a range on a long street (El Camino) spans unrelated houses. Why the house number as the unit: copying the same-site re-pointing would have kept '~3b5695' and made 1226 and 1228 one unit. Why before same-site: a range place that same-site then merges carries what it absorbed. Dry run on a copy of the live DB (11,369 listings): 15 places, 56 listings, every one checked: University Gardens (1226, 1228, 1242 into 1222-1244 University Dr, 10 to 16 m), Palo Alto Plaza 541 into 521-541 Del Medio Ave (11 listings, 4 m; dropped, see below), Stanford Villa 3375 into 3351-3375 Alma St (9, 57 m; the 11-25 same-site entry asked for it by hand), Maddox Sunnyvale 874 into 870-874 E El Camino Real (8), Wildwood Manor 260 into 220-260 N Mathilda Ave (5, 129 m), 120 East Creek Dr #1 into 120-122 E Creek Dr (4; the name is the address text), Coleman Arms '858, 862 Coleman Avenue' into 858-862 Coleman Ave (3), Curtner Apartments 300 into Curtner Apts 300-310 Curtner Ave (2), Windemere 395 into 395-396 Ano Nuevo Ave (2; flagged as one building on 11-25), Laurel Court 1041 #1055 into 1041-1063 Laurel St (2), Miramar Plaza 1141 into 1121-1145 Miramar Way (2), Magnolia Place '160, 170, 180 Locksunart Way' into 160-180 (1), and RGOR1 Midtown 2643 into 2643-2681 Alma St (Zumper and Zillow both name 'rgor1'; 1,302 m apart only because Zillow's pin is off, so the merged pin moves). The first dry run caught one trap: you had merged Palo Alto Plaza's range place (521-541 Del Medio, 3 Apartments.com plans) into 541 Del Medio Ave by hand at 07:11, so the rule would have moved 541's 11 rule listings to the now-empty range place and left your 3 behind, splitting the building. Fix: a place with hand links in or out is left to you (near miss 'merged or split by hand'). Second dry run: 14 places, 45 listings. Near misses left alone (15 in the first run, all 'no shared name or operator' but one ZIP-unknown repeat): 37 Sherland / 37-41 Sherland Ave, 456 / 456-458 Morse Ave, 122 E Creek Dr 'Creekside' / 120-122 E Creek Dr, 848 Jordan 'Los Altos Court' / 848-854 Jordan 'Los Altos Court 1 Bed 1 Bath', 3060 / 3060-3068 Emerson St, 592 / 592-594 S Eden Ave, 180 Locksunart Way APT 9 'Magnolia Place Spacious 1 Bedroom' / 160-180 'Magnolia Place', 696 Towle Way #11 / 675-699 Towle Way, 363 Everett / 347-367 Everett Ave, 544 E McKinley / 544-548; and two that look like different buildings: 1141 W Olive 'Parallel' / 1120-1154 W Olive 'Castlemont Arms', 810 Coleman / 806-810 Coleman 'Garden Oaks'. Several near misses are one building whose 'name' is address text that does not match exactly; merge those by hand. Miramar Plaza: same-site used to keep 1141 Miramar Way and absorb the range place; now the range place (p_c7d2255265b7cf16) is kept and 1141 (p_bd76ddac661a0d9c) is absorbed, the same building either way. Seen, not changed: `places.address` is the newest observation's address, so the merged University Gardens reads '1226 University Dr' rather than the range. Trips of absorbed places are left to the stale-trip change. Replaced: nothing merged a range place with its own townhouses or buildings.
- **2026-11-25: Craigslist pins no longer position a place that another source places; trips record their origin and go stale when the place moves.** User-approved, after the identity agent's survey: of 155 multi-source places 30 had the Craigslist pin over 150 m from the other sources (Zillow: 23 of 409); one pin, 37.4056, -122.0775, carries 23 posts of 4 buildings; Revela passed `lease` on an 18.6 min Caltrain walk from a Craigslist account pin 2 km away; Middle Park showed a 24.2 min walk routed at its old pin. (1) *Position*: the `places` view averages only non-Craigslist pins when the place has any (`COALESCE(AVG(CASE WHEN source NOT IN APPROX_PIN_SOURCES ...)), AVG(lat))`); a Craigslist-only place keeps its pin, and a place whose other listings carry no pin (an own-site row without coordinates) keeps the Craigslist one rather than losing its position. On the live snapshot this moves 160 places, 69 by more than 30 m, 19 by more than 150 m (median 21 m, max 3.5 km: Bella Vista, 1500 Vista Club Circle; then 545 University Ave Menlo Park 2.6 km, Maplewood 1.4 km, Prado 1.0 km, Colony 0.8 km, Revela 765 m, Somerset Place 583 m). *Airbnb and Furnished Finder* pins are approximate too, but not the same problem: 0 places mix either with a precisely placed source (their stays are keyed apart: no address, `~operator` stay keys, `NO_NEAR_MERGE`), so they are unchanged. *Not done: geocoding Craigslist addresses to a point.* 310 Craigslist-only places have a street address (324 distinct strings, 316 placed precisely by Google), 24 of them on a pin shared with another place; but the geocode cache (`data/geocode_cache.json`) never kept Google's coordinates, so this needs about 324 new calls (2,433 of 4,900 used this month) and a home for the point that the SQL views can read (the JSON cache is outside the DB). Left for the user to decide. `identity._near_address` still averages every pin of an address place for its 80 m join (unchanged: changing it moves links). (2) *Stale trips*: trips are keyed by `place_key` only and never recorded where they were routed from, so nothing could tell a trip from an old pin (Middle Park, the 13 merged places with Google trips). Now every `trips` row records `o_lat, o_lon` (new nullable columns) and counts only while within 30 m of the place's position (`trips.is_current`, `STALE_M`: well above the leg cache's 4-decimal rounding and averaging jitter, about half a minute's walk). Readers that skip stale rows: `best_trips` (before KNN trains, so a stale time never spreads), `missing_estimates` (moved places are estimated again, free), `compute_for_place` (appends again when only the origin changed). Considered and rejected: re-keying trips by rounded position (breaks the stable `place_key` join every reader uses) and a read-time check against the leg cache on every page load (slow, and wrong for composed modes whose first leg ends at a station). *Legacy rows*: the log is append-only, so rows from before cannot be given an origin in place; `aptf-commute adopt-origins` (one-time) reads each latest Google row's origin back from the leg cache (the Google leg of its first leg's mode, minutes and distance, and for a single-mode trip its destination, whose origin is nearest the place now) and appends the row again with that origin and its own computed_at. On the snapshot: 6,563 legacy latest Google rows; 6,048 adopted (5,864 still within 30 m of their place, 184 not); 348 with no cache match (all but 5 to points no longer in the config; the 5 are `bike_bus_bike` rows whose first leg was re-timed); 167 for places that no longer exist (merged away). Estimates are not adopted: free, they were recomputed (3,194 places, 99,014 rows). Then `aptf-commute reroute-stale`: 35 places, 263 uncached Route Matrix elements on the snapshot. Before re-routing, the snapshot's goal changes were small: lease Middle Park fail -> needs check (its stale 24 min walk dropped); temp one Airbnb fail -> needs check (bike estimate 12 -> 11 min). *Outage and fix forward (13:38-13:50 CST)*: the first deploy (33b39e2) went live before `adopt-origins` and the re-estimate ran, so for those minutes every trip read as stale; the dashboard built and persisted (`data/cache/*.pkl`) goal results with no times ('drive to Meta HQ: no time', 'net commute: no times yet'), and a restart restored that snapshot. Reverted (8f3d44d), then redeployed once the live rows carried origins (adopted 6,134 of 6,699 live legacy Google rows, 5,945 still current; estimates recomputed), after checking on a copy of the live DB that the new code gives the same commute times as the old (lease 31 pass both, no 'not computed' commute). Lesson: a change that makes existing rows unreadable ships with its data step done first.
- **2026-11-25: Under a goal with `people`, 'price / bed' sorts by price / person.** User: lease (people 3) sorted 'price / bed' showed $1,221/person, $1,733/person, $1,332/person: the sort read the offer's $/bed (1,221, 1,300, 1,332) while the card shows the offer's price ÷ people. Now the sort key (and the status sort's tiebreak) is the goal's offer price ÷ people when the goal has `people` (no offer: the term's minimum price ÷ people), and the option reads 'price / person'; no goal or no people keeps $/bed. Order: 3BED & 2BATH (Craigslist) $1,221, Palmetto $1,332, 227 Church St $1,733 (was 227 Church St second). Replaced: $/bed sorting under a per-person card.
- **2026-11-25: `parking_cost` falls back to the reading the card shows.** User: Palmetto's card showed '🅿 2 assigned/carport, included' beside '🅿 ? for 2 spots' (lease, nice-to-have `parking_cost {spots: 2}`). Cause: neither guess (spots missing, or kinds/spots parsed but not stored). Every Craigslist reading there stores `spots: 2`, `cost: included` ('Comes with 2 assigned parking spots in a gated carport area ... at no extra charge'), but the goal's offer is the own-site plan row (`direct` palmetto-apartments-plans:r:3-bedroom-2-bath), which has no reading, and Craigslist posts are unit posts that never pool to the place; the card's line is `place_parking`, the best reading of any listing. Now when a row's own and pooled readings state no cost, `parking_cost` reads that card reading (still one reading whole; `goal_inputs` keeps it as `parking_place`, outside `parking_readings` so the parking rule fields do not change). Live lease goal, deployed vs patched code on the box database (read-only): 13 places change from '?': 1 to $0 (Palmetto, '2 included, from another listing here'), 2 to a cost (p_3de791de6a4e4515 +$100, Craigslist 'Parking Available - $50/month'; p_6297a1f5c90f92b3 +$400, Zillow 'Parking available for $200/month'), 10 to no parking (street-only posts: Airbnb 'Free street parking', Furnished Finder 'Street parking only.'); 36 more stay '?' with a different why. Totals: ? 2,774 -> 2,761, $0 76 -> 77, costed 33 -> 35, no parking 222 -> 232. Replaced: a cost from the deciding row's own and building readings only.
- **2026-11-25: A reload of the list keeps showing the goal draft's preview.** User: editing the lease goal (a walk ≤ 25 min row), the list header flipped every few seconds with the 'updating…' spinner between '31 pass · 0 check · 3,067 fail · 155 no listings' and '13 pass · 3 check · 3,085 fail · 152 no listings'. Cause, front end only: while the server answered `X-Updating` the page re-fetched `api/places` every 5 s; `loadPlaces` set the fresh places (the saved goal's results), rendered them, and only then re-posted the preview, whose answer rendered the draft again, so each re-poll showed saved, then draft. The live API held the saved goal steady (13 pass / 3 check, 3,253 places) and its `X-Updating` cleared within ~75 s of each change (polled 13:46 to 13:50: no rebuild loop; the counts moved only when goals were saved, 13 -> 14 -> 9 pass). Now the preview's last answer is kept with the draft it judged (`S.preview`); `loadPlaces` applies it to the fresh places before rendering when it matches the current draft (else leaves the list as it is until the new answer) and re-previews quietly. Replaces: re-preview after rendering the saved results. Test: `test_reload_keeps_showing_the_draft_preview` (renders saved then draft on the old code).

The range-merge agent's 12 kept places: those that moved over 30 m with Google trips (p_0ea2a1a3f72a0365 5 pairs, p_43cffe36cb5421af 7) were re-routed; the others' Google trips are still within 30 m; trips of the absorbed keys are never read (no such place). RGOR1 Midtown (p_103e3b34566518fe) has no Google trips; its 1.3 km move is a Zillow pin, which this rule does not touch. *Times*: Middle Park walk to Menlo Park Caltrain 24.2 -> 21.1 min (Google, from its RentCafe/Zillow position; Palo Alto 32.9 -> 29.9); 825 Menlo walk to Menlo Park 18.5 -> 10.4 min, Meta drive 16.8 -> 14.3, Rengstorff bike+train 35.6 -> 32.9; Revela walk to Mountain View 33.1 -> 39.4; 545 University Ave #4 (Craigslist pin 2.6 km off) walk to Menlo Park KNN 45 -> estimate 78 min, Meta drive 7.5 -> 2.0 min estimate; Maplewood walk to Mountain View KNN 11.7 -> 25.1. *Goals* (old code on the snapshot vs new code on the post-run copy, both with the post-run goal set): lease 19 -> 20 pass: 825 Menlo Apartments fail -> pass (was 'walk to caltrain_stations <= 18: 18 min' and 'bike_train_bike <= 35: 36 min', both at the Craigslist-averaged pin); temp unchanged (44 pass); goal3 unchanged (3 pass; City Center Plaza no_listings -> fail is not a position or trip effect: the same flip appears on a code version without this change). *Legacy check*: old vs new `best_trips` on the post-run copy, places that did not move: 0 times lost and 0 Google-to-KNN/estimate downgrades for any (place, current point, mode) with a place behind it; 31 per-set minimums changed, each where an old estimate to a no-longer-nearest point (or a stay's own estimate at its own pin) undercut the Google or KNN time (e.g. a bus-stop walk estimate 68.0 -> KNN 78.8). - **2026-11-25: An unsaved goal edit shows what it cuts (blue) and adds (pink).** User: "when a plan is diff from the saved plan, highlight the things going to be cut in a diff color (maybe blue?)", then: added places pink, treated like cut ones. The draft's preview (`POST /api/goals/preview`) already returns every place's status and reasons, so the comparison with the saved results is client side, no second request; the one server addition is `i` on each `fails` entry (the rule's index in the goal's must-haves), so the card names the edited rule rather than every failing one: on the live data a place whose plans all fail under the draft is decided by another listing, so beds and baths failed beside the edited walk rule (p_892e221af38932b7: 'beds >= 3: is 1', 'baths >= 2: is 1', 'walk to caltrain_stations <= 18: 19 min'; the card says only the last). Cut and added places bypass the status ticks and the inactive box: tightening lease's walk to Caltrain to 15 first cut 5 with only 4 cards shown (2105 Cornell St has no active listing). The summary first sat under the editor and was off screen behind its rules; it is under the count line. The list re-poll (`loadPlaces`) keeps the diff until the re-preview replaces it (built on `S.preview`, 4c736e8). Live example (snapshot of the box, lease saved with walk to Caltrain <= 18 by then; the probe at <= 21 before that: tightening to 18 cut 6, 0 added): draft <= 15 cuts 5: Avalon Mountain View (17 min), 227 Church St Mountain View (18 min), 2105 Cornell St Palo Alto (19-20 min, 3 nearby Google routes), Palmetto Apartments (15 min: 15.x rounded), The Gardens of Sunnyvale (19 min); draft <= 21 adds 2 Craigslist Mountain View 3Br/2Ba duplexes (21 and 19 min). The preview takes about 1.5-1.9 s on the box and returns 5.7 MB (not slimmed here). Replaced: the draft's results shown with no sign of what changed.

- **2026-11-25: Craigslist-only places sit on their geocoded address; Google's positions are kept in a `geocodes` log.** User approved geocoding the Craigslist-only places that have a street address (they sat on the post's pin, the poster's account or area point; 24 shared a pin with another place). *Storage*: the JSON cache (`data/geocode_cache.json`) only ever kept the street and city, and the SQL views cannot read it. Options weighed: a `checks` row (needs an observation or trip, lapses per listing when the value changes, and Craigslist reposts get new ids: wrong shape); a synthetic observation (would make a listing, polluting `listings`, `sources`, `n_listings`); a small append-only log `geocodes` (chosen: Google's answer about an address string is new data, like `media` and `api_calls`, and nothing else holds it). One row per Geocoding API answer with the raw `location_type` and types, so precision is judged in the view; `geocode()` now writes one for every new address string (all sources), and `aptf-identity positions` asks again, once, for the Craigslist addresses cached before. *View*: `places` takes each listing's position by tier (own pin of a source that places the building; else a Craigslist row's address, precise and within 5 km of its pin; else the Craigslist pin), lat and lon from the same tier; an empty `geocodes` table gives byte-identical `places` output on a copy of the live DB (3,253 rows), the view's time 3.27 -> 3.5 s. The dashboard's freshness check now counts `geocodes` rows (a new position moves a place). *Dry run on a copy of the live DB* (box goals): 323 address strings to ask (320 Craigslist-only places with an address; strings Google did not place precisely before were not asked again), 323 calls, all 323 precise (ROOFTOP 198, GEOMETRIC_CENTER 119, RANGE_INTERPOLATED 6). 295 places move: under 30 m 45, 30-150 m 147, 150-500 m 52, 0.5-1 km 14, 1-2 km 21, 2-5 km 16; median 99 m, max 4.4 km (Villas at Monterosso, Danville); 15 of them had shared a pin. The 5 km guard kept the pin for 7 rows (3300 Old Soscol Way, Napa 14.8 km; 766 First St, Gilroy 18.7 km; '1100 Lincoln Ave, 1307 Mt Pisgah Rd, and 114 Sharene Ln' 67.8 km; San Jose's 5805 Charlotte Dr 12.1 km, 88 Montecito Vista Dr 8.3 km, 5210 Monterey Rd 8.7 km). In the search area 23 places move, e.g. Willow Housing, 605 Willow Rd Menlo Park 3.5 km (the pin was near Stanford; Google's rooftop is on Willow Rd), 746 Connecticut Dr Redwood City 1.26 km, Extended Stay 1255 Orleans Dr Sunnyvale 1.26 km, Waverley Apartments 447 Waverley St Menlo Park 477 m, 854 Jordan Ave Los Altos 164 m. *Suspicious, left as they are*: '2275 Sand Hill Place #206, Menlo Park' answered as 2275 Sand Hill **Rd** #206 (same number, another street type; RANGE_INTERPOLATED, 495 m): `geocode_parts` checks the house number, not the street name; '4401 Central Avenue, Alameda' -> Fremont and '4098 San Pablo Avenue, Alameda' -> Emeryville (Craigslist's 'alameda' area read as the city; the pins agree with Google); '2865 E Homestead Rd, Santa Clara' -> Sunnyvale (a border street). Most other city mismatches are Craigslist area labels ('tenderloin', 'east bay area'). *Co-located now* (separate places within 30 m after the move, split before this change; identity unchanged, nothing merged): 854 Jordan Ave vs Zumper's 854 Jordan Ave #2092 (3 m), 1504 vs 1502 Madrono Ave (3 m), 447 Waverley St vs 443 Waverley St #447 (18 m), 120 East Creek Dr #1 vs 120-122 E Creek Dr (30 m); `suspected_duplicates` 0 -> 1 (255 Rengstorff Ave #109 vs 255 S Rengstorff Ave APT 109, 115 m). Identity on the copy afterwards: 0 links appended. *Trips*: 249 moved places lacked a current estimate (7,719 free estimate rows); 17 places had Google trips now stale, 32 (point, mode) pairs, 40 uncached Route Matrix elements for `reroute-stale`. *Goals* (copy, estimates only, before re-routing): lease, temp and arnav_joshi unchanged (0 places change outcome). Considered and rejected: storing coordinates in the JSON cache (not readable by the views), a `precise` judged at write time only (the view also filters `location_type`), geocoding every cached address again (thousands of calls for places other sources already place). Replaced: a Craigslist-only place always on its pin.
